# 16 — Combat: the arena, the story, and what stacks on it

> **IC boundary clarified by the owner, 2026-10-28:** the OOC simulator is a proving ground
> and consequence-free practice, not the source of IC lifecycle rules. See
> [doc 27](27-ic-field-operations-design-and-survey.md) for the coordinated DT.1–DT.3/H1/H2
> design and survey. IC casualties preserve exact surviving components; physical recovery
> does not repair them. Fixed sides, resets, untargetable wrecks, public replay, snapshot-tail
> rollback and lobby leadership below must not silently become IC requirements. The 10 km
> arena is not a prerequisite merely because an older “Before IC play” note names it.

Draft 0.2, 2026-09-16, written from the answered survey [16a](16a-combat-owner-questions.md) against
`main` at `5da8d6a` and revised after the three-lens review recorded in [16b §10](16b-combat-implementation-plan.md).
The decisions this document rests on are collected in §12 as D-16-1 to D-16-20 (owner, 2026-09-16,
binding; D-16-19 and D-16-20 added by the review).

Reading order for someone new: §1 (what "done" means), §3.1 (the arena, its seam and its tempo), §3.4
(motion and the pulse), §3.5 (how a shot resolves), §7 (where the code goes), §12 (the decisions).

---

## 1. Purpose and the two acceptances

This document specifies the first combat build of the single 2350-era TacSim game: a simulation arena
off the OOC Area in which two players in tanks find each other and fight until one is destroyed. It is
written as the basis for everything that follows (lasers, bipeds, more sensors, ECM, ECCM, elevation,
then real-world fights), so each of those is an addition specified in §9, never a rewrite.

Two acceptances define "done", both on the production composition:

**A1, the story test.** Two connected accounts create characters, walk from the OOC welcome hall into
the simulator lobby, pick opposite teams, board their pods, receive the default tank, start it up, drive
across terrain, see each other only once line of sight and sensor range allow, lock, fire cannons, and
one tank is finished: destroyed, or since 16b cut AP.2 with its guns shot away, which ends a one-tank
side's match (§3.8, §11). The match records a result, both pilots leave, and a second match can start. One
test in `mushd/tests` (in-process) and its twin in `mush-gateway/tests/arena_story_split.rs` through the gateway boundary; both must
pass, because the output path differs (§3.1).

The observer rule is asserted surface by surface, never as one blanket claim: before detection the
other tank appears in none of the cockpit `VIEW` frame, the atlas `+map` frame, `look` in any room,
`@unit/status` and `@unit/show` for the other pilot, contact notices, the TUI panels, and the web feed.
The money rule: nothing in the campaign economy changed.

**A2, the scale harness.** 300 units placed and moving on the arena, 24 of them fighting, 150 weapons on
their cadences, one sensor pass per unit per second of arena time, at least 12 connected fake sessions
receiving output, the LLM stubbed, on one core: per-pulse p99 under 25 ms in `Engaged` tempo (40 ms since
16c `T-109`: the production VPS's figure, §11), resident
memory flat over one hour of arena time with the ledger streamed out (§3.1; measured by the long-run
lane over `TACSIM_LONG_RUN_S` arena seconds, 600 by default, the hour being one run at 3,600: 16b cut
U.2), the streamed ledger under
`harness.max_ledger_bytes`, traces and cell probes per pulse under their bounds (a cell probe being,
since 16b cut J.1, an interior cell read up to and including the first blocker, and horizon probes
reported beside them unbounded since J.2, §11), path queries per pulse
under their bound. The harness runs at the configuration maxima of every world fact in §2. Where it runs
and whether it gates is D-16-19.

---

## 2. World facts the engine relies on

Ratified 2026-09-16 (16a §9). Each fact bounds an engine cost, has a configuration knob, and is asserted
by the harness. They belong in the setting docs beside the Retreat and Concordat material; here they are
stated as engine requirements.

| # | Fact | Engine requirement | Knob | Harness assertion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | Standard ECM defeats detection at long range | Sensor ranges are short and bounded | `sensors.<class>.max_range_cells`, `sensors.max_signature_factor` | Candidates per pass bounded by units within `max_range × max_signature_factor` |
| W2 | Lasers kill drones and soft fliers, not armour | No drone swarms; projectiles primary | `arena.max_units_by_class` | Class counts per side obey the caps (per side and not per map since 16b cut AE.3, §11) |
| W3 | Active sensors are suicide; doctrine is passive | A pass is range plus LOS, nothing else | `sensors.<class>.active = false` | No emitter term in detection |
| W4 | Lasers burn optics in the open; fights are short-range in clutter | Traces are bounded per pulse; arenas are authored with clutter | arena content | Traces per pulse under `harness.max_traces_per_pulse` and cell probes under `harness.max_cell_probes_per_pulse` (16b cut U.2); horizon probes reported beside them (J.2) |
| W5 | Transponders are spoofable; identification takes a close look | Classification is a slower cadence than detection | `sensors.classify_after_ms` | Classifications per pulse bounded |
| W6 | Tactical comms are LOS mesh jammed by the same ECM | Shared contacts fan out only within comms range | `comms.share_range_cells` | Share operations per pulse bounded |
| W7 | NPC lance-leaders are people; their orders reach the lance over the relay; a licensed mind leased over it is advice to offices and never an order's author (doc 17 D-UM-53; 17b cut L) | Deliberation is optional; reflexes always run; a squad with no lance-leader runs on doctrine | `[llm.routes.tactical_flash]` | Harness runs with the LLM disabled |
| W8 | The Compact caps span of control and needs a human trigger | Bounded AI per commander; fire is a discrete act | `command.max_span`, `command.orders_per_minute` (both enforced at the world since 16b cut Y.2, §11) | Orders per commander per minute bounded (reported and asserted by the harness since Y.2) |
| W9 | Heavy hulls are scarce post-Retreat | Twelve a side is the setting's ceiling for a fight | `match.max_slots_per_side` | Fighters at 24 |
| W10 | The sky is a laser kill zone | Ground only on the tactical map; elevation is terrain | driver `media = ground` | No `air` medium participant |
| W11 | Contested ground comes in Interval pockets joined by corridors | One arena per fight; corridors are journeys | one arena per domain | One region per fight |
| W12 | Armour is modular plate by facet | Facet attribution; no impact geometry | facet table in `UnitSpec` | No geometry code in the impact path |
| W13 | Indirect fire needs a spotter net ECM breaks | Direct fire only | none | No `indirect_fire_path` consumer |
| W14 | Interval-grade power plants | One to fifteen second cycle times | `weapons.<id>.cycle_ms` floor 1,000 | Shots per pulse bounded |
| W15 | Ammunition is logistics | Short engagements; units withdraw | `units.<id>.ammo`, `match.max_duration_ms` | Match length bounded in the harness |
| W16 | A battle's canon is reconstructed from black boxes | The match ledger is an in-world artifact | ledger streamed and digested | Ledger complete and digest-matched for a harness match |

Doc 10 §8's combined-arms list is read as strategic and orbital aerospace untouched; tactical-map air is
what W2 and W10 remove; "drones scout" becomes "drones scout until a laser sees them", which kept them
as later content until 16b cut AE.3 fielded one capped `drone_mk1` (§11).

---

## 3. The model

### 3.1 Time, tempo and authority

**The arena is an authority.** One arena per map. While a match runs, the arena is the single writer
for its own facts: unit poses, unit condition cells, contacts, orders, scheduled impacts, the match
ledger. The world task remains the single writer for everything else: identity, focus, leases, pods,
rooms, the economy, and the durable records. The two share no state and no lock (D-16-1). Doc 06 §6.3 is
amended accordingly (§11).

**The seam is eight messages and nothing else** (since 16b cut I, two staff bookkeeping messages beside them, `TempoFloor { period_ms }` and `Watch { on }`, and an `observer` field on `Frames`, none of which the hash folds: §11, the I.3 and I.4 entries; since 16b cut AF.2 one durability message beside them, `Snapshot { match_id, snapshot }` from the arena after the opening pulse and after every pulse that enters a new `snapshot_ms` period of arena time, riding the egress behind the chunks and folded by nothing: the AF.2 entry).

```text
world -> arena   CustodyOut { definition, units[{cells, pose}], pilots[{unit, station, epoch}], seed }
world -> arena   Intent     { unit, channel, epoch, kind, seq, payload }        (bounded inbox)
world -> arena   Control    { unit, channel, epoch, state: Live | Linkdead | Released }
world -> arena   Stop       { reason: Abort | Timeout | Void | Concede{by} | Unattended }   (16b cut D.3)
arena -> world   Ledger     { chunk }                                            (bounded egress)
arena -> world   Frames     { now_ms, pilot -> frame }                           (latest-wins per pilot)
arena -> world   CustodyIn  { units[{cells, final_pose, outcome}], result, ledger_digest, reason }
arena -> world   Deliberate { match_id, signals: squad briefs }                  (droppable, N.2)
```

- `Intent` is validated at the world (focus, lease, channel, epoch) when enqueued and **authorised at
  apply** in the arena against the arena's own per-`(unit, channel)` epoch table (doc 09 C8). The world
  raises that table through `Control` on every seat activation, deactivation, lease revocation,
  linkdead onset and revival (doc 07 §1.2), `EXIT` and `SIM ABORT`; the arena purges every queued intent
  below the new epoch. A pilot who types `FIRE` then `EXIT` in one burst never fires.
- `Ledger` chunks (16 KiB) leave the arena as they fill; the world appends them to a
  `mush_core::Ledger` (`log:match.<id>`) on its own writer lane, ack-gated, best effort during the
  match. The arena's egress is a bounded ring; if the world cannot keep up the arena marks itself
  `Lagged`, and past `ledger.max_unflushed_chunks` the match is stopped with `Void`. The arena never
  holds a store handle (D-16-3).
- `Frames` are one latest-wins slot per pilot. The world task drains them each iteration and emits them
  through its ordinary output path addressed as `SendTarget::Char`, with the loop's own policing and the
  gateway resolving connections; the arena never touches `GatewaySink`. A slow world drops stale
  views, never notices: since 16b cut M.1 a `Frames` message the world could not take is held on the
  arena task and merged into the next pulse's (the latest view wins, the held notices are kept ahead of
  the new ones, at most 64 a pilot, a released pilot dropped), and the end sends what is still held before
  its result, so a notice is delivered at most once and at least once unless the process is stopping
  (§11). Since 16b cut D.1 the message also carries the arena's clock and may carry no
  frame at all: it is sent when a frame exists or the arena second has moved since the last one sent,
  so the lobby board's clock is the arena's own and at worst an arena second stale (§11).
- `CustodyIn` is applied per unit and per-unit fallible (§Custody).
- `Deliberate` is a report-only squad signal; the world may request a bounded LLM
  completion and return an ordinary `Intent`. The arena never waits for it.
  These are eight conceptual seam messages. In `arena_task.rs`, `CustodyOut` is
  the spawn argument and `CustodyIn` rides `Ended`; `LedgerAck` and `Lagged` also
  carry reliability bookkeeping, making nine runtime enum variants in total (eleven since 16b cut I:
  `TempoFloor`, the staff floor of I.3, and `Watch`, the staff watch of I.4, both applied on arrival and
  held to the inbox reserve like `Intent`).

**The arena core is a library.** `tacsim::arena::Arena` holds the state and exposes
`step(now_ms, controls, intents) -> StepOutput { ledger_chunks, frames, tempo, next_due_ms }`, a pure
function of its state and inputs with no I/O, no task and no clock of its own. The arena task is a thin
wrapper that owns an `Arena`, the inbox, the egress ring, a timer driven by `next_due_ms`, a watchdog and
`catch_unwind`. Tests and the harness call `step` directly.

**Tempo** is adaptive per arena (D-16-2):

| Tempo | Period | Enter when | Leave when |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Quiet` | 5,000 ms | default; no hostile contact held by any unit, no pending impact, no fire intent, no AI in `Approach` | a threshold below is crossed |
| `Alert` | 1,000 ms | any unit holds a contact (classified or not) whose Euclidean range is under `tempo.alert_range_cm`, or a fire intent is queued, or an AI enters `Approach` | `tempo.alert_hold_ms` (30,000) with none of those |
| `Engaged` | 500 ms | a shot is fired, an impact is scheduled, or a platform holds a classified hostile it has not read dead (§6; 16b cut E.2) inside its inner fighting reach, the lesser of its own sight and its loaded weapon's first band (16b cut E.1, §11) | `tempo.engaged_hold_ms` (10,000) since the last shot, impact or in-range pair |

Tempo changes are ledger events. The harness's 25 ms p99 (40 ms since 16c `T-109`) applies in `Engaged`.

**Rate independence** (D-16-2). The tempo decides only how often `step` runs and how output is batched;
it never decides an outcome. That is achieved by one rule: **a pulse is a sequence of substeps, and
every due event is applied at its own arena timestamp between substeps.**

- The substep length is `min(tempo.substep_max_ms, (cell_cm - 1) × 1000 / max_speed_cms)`, where
  `max_speed_cms` is `Arena::max_speed_cms`, the true ceiling `fastest × 100 / min(100, min_cost[row])`
  over the units that can move, each on its own locomotion row (16b cuts M.4 and AE.1, §11: a cell cheaper than 100 raises the cap in §3.4 step 2),
  floored at 50 ms, so no unit ever advances more than one cell in a substep (§3.4); a ceiling the 50 ms
  floor cannot bound is refused at `Arena::new` by name (`SubstepBound`) and the match does not start.
  The centimetre taken off the cell is margin (16b cut Q.6, §11): a substep's travel, its carried
  remainder included, is at most `cell_cm - 1`. Substeps are cut from each pulse's own start with the
  remainder last (16b cut M.2, §11): under the scripted zero-`dt` driver, whose pulses land on period
  multiples, the three shipped cadences agree because the shipped substep divides all three periods
  (asserted for every fielded unit and by the harness); a production pulse ends at the lattice point
  phase 5's `next_due_ms` names (16b cut U.1: a period multiple or the first substep boundary at or after
  the next due event, in absolute arena time) plus the OS timer's overshoot, wall jitter, so a late wake's
  boundaries are not lattice-aligned and rate independence rests on the due-event rule below, not on
  alignment. The absolute substep lattice is not built (16c Q24): the wake targets are lattice points
  since U.1, but the partition inside a pulse is still cut from that pulse's start; a unit above
  3,996 cm/s at 10 m cells would need it.
- Scheduled impacts, sensor passes, classification transitions, startup completions, weapon-ready
  times, the match's duration bound and the expiry of a loss hysteresis (16b cut M.2) are events with
  arena timestamps; each is applied in timestamp order at the first substep boundary at or after its
  time, against the positions at that boundary. Nothing due after the duration bound's own millisecond
  is applied or journalled; a kill on that millisecond still wins.
- Every random draw is derived from `(match_seed, label)` with an enumerated label (§3.5); there is no
  stream whose position depends on how many pulses have run.
- All cadences, cycle times, delays and holds are in arena milliseconds.

The property that pins this: one scripted intent and control log replayed at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms
produces an identical ordered event list and identical per-substep state hashes (16b A7).

**Clock and lag.** Arena time is milliseconds since match start, advanced by wall time between pulses
clamped to `max_catch_up_steps × period` (doc 06 §6.4, initially 4). Beyond that the arena records
`lagged` and skips idle wall time; it never expands `dt` to hide lag. Since 16b cut I.3 staff may floor the
WAKE interval (`@arena/tempo`, `Clock::set_floor`, `engaged_ms` to `MAX_TEMPO_FLOOR_MS` 10,000 in steps
of `engaged_ms`): the task then wakes every `max(period, floor)` of wall time, the clamp stays on the
adaptive tempo's own period (so a 5,000 ms floor at `Engaged` moves 2,000 ms of arena time a wake:
slow motion, never a pause), every floored pulse and the first after a lift is cut back to the substep
lattice (`floor_to`; a wake inside a substep is a pulse of no time), and lag is measured past the floor
in force when the interval began (`elapsed − floor_at_last_wake > cap`), so a slowed match journals no
`lagged`. The floor is a view no hash folds; its `tempo_floor` line moves the ledger sequence like every
line (§11, the I.3 entry).

**Pulse phases, in order, each reading the state the previous phase committed** (doc 06 §6.3):

1. Apply `Control` messages to the epoch table; apply `Stop` if present (a staff `Place` still buffered
   when the `Stop` lands is journalled `place_refused` with reason `stopped` before the stop line; 16b
   cut I.3).
2. Drain `Intent`s: authorise each at apply; continuous controls set desired values (latest wins);
   discrete actions are queued per unit, and a seat may give a unit at most
   `intents.max_pending_per_unit` of them per arena second, counted on the order's source so a
   machine's own orders are never counted and a gunner on a machine-driven tank still is (16b cut M.1,
   §11; the queue itself is unbounded, the world side's inbox and pre-pulse buffer are bounded per unit
   at that value, and no order is ever dropped ahead of a later one or by another pilot's burst). An
   order refused here as a wreck's, an immobile tank's, one past the rate or one given with power off is
   told to its pilot; a purged one stays silent (16b cut D.2, §11). A staff `Place` (16b cut I.3) meets
   the unit, epoch and channel gates and skips the wreck, linkdead and rate gates and the pre-pulse
   bound; a refusal here is journalled `place_refused` under this phase's hash and told to nobody.
3. For each substep in order: (a) integrate motion for every unit from the substep-start snapshot, in
   unit-id order; (b) rebuild the spatial index; (c) apply every due event with timestamp at or before
   the substep end, in timestamp order then event-sequence order: impacts (damage), weapon-ready,
   startup-complete, sensor passes (detection and classification), share operations, the duration
   bound, loss expiries; a due event that stops on the bound's millisecond (16b cut M.2); since 17b cut
   M1a.4 a hull's `MotorTick`, and since M1a.5 a mind's `MindLand` and `MindThink` are held while the
   millisecond's other due events run; since 17b cut M1b.0 the held landings then run in unit-id order,
   then whatever they scheduled at that millisecond (a beam's zero-flight impact, a `MotorTick` on a
   substep end), then the thinks (§11).
4. Resolve newly queued discrete actions that became legal this pulse (`LOCK`, `FIRE`, `STARTUP`, since
   16b cut O.2 `EMCON`, since 17b cut M1b.1 a `FIRE <weapon> WHEN …` or `FIRE <weapon or ALL> OFF` arming
   (`Condition`, in arrival order with `LOCK` and `FIRE`, with a slot's own shot evaluated at its trigger's
   millisecond inside phase 3 or after the arming here), and since 16b cut I.3 a staff `PLACE`, first, under the entry test and refused by a round in flight to or
   from the unit) at
   the pulse's end, scheduling their events; then, on a pulse that ran a substep (or, since I.3, a
   floored pulse of no time once arena time has started), one zero-length
   substep applies what phase 4 scheduled at or before that end (a pilot's beam lands in the pulse that
   fired it; 16b cut M.2). A machine's actions are resolved and scheduled at its own tick inside phase 3.
5. Evaluate tempo (not on a pulse a substep ended: nothing is journalled after the end); compute the
   state hash; append the pulse's ledger events; build frames for pilots whose view changed (since 16b
   cut U.2 a frame is sent when a printed figure would change, a contact's age compared at the whole
   second, the stale clause and, on the locked row, the lock's range step, never merely per pulse);
   after the frames, while the match is watched, build the staff `Observer` at most once an arena second
   and on the ending pulse (16b cut I.4: a view, hashed by nothing, with no path into a pilot's frame);
   journal `tempo_floor` once per change of the staff floor (I.3);
   compute `next_due_ms`: the earlier of the next period multiple and the first substep boundary at or
   after the next due event, in absolute arena time, never in the past (16b cut U.1; 16c Q25: due
   events inside one substep coalesce into that substep, each applied at its own timestamp; under a
   staff floor a wall interval of `max(period, floor)` instead, the arena being off the wall lattice on
   purpose and every floored pulse cut back to the substep lattice: I.3). A driver
   that wakes on time is on the lattice; the production task is the lattice plus the timer's overshoot,
   and its deadline is taken from the task's base so the overshoot does not accumulate.

**Determinism** (D-16-4). Authoritative arena state is integer: centimetres, millidegrees,
milliseconds, cm/s, per-mille probabilities. Floating point is allowed inside a computation as long as
every stored result is quantised. Each substep produces a state hash recorded with its pulse.

**Durability** (D-16-3, amended at 16b cut AF). For OOC matches the arena holds all state in memory and
never touches the store. The ledger is streamed (above) so memory stays flat and the black box survives.
Since 16b cut AF the arena is also a document: every `arena.snapshot_ms` of arena time (5,000; and on the
opening pulse) the task encodes itself and hands the world one `Snapshot`, which the world writes beside its
own live record of the match on its writer lane in tacsim's own namespace (`tacsim:snapshot:<n>`,
`tacsim:live:<n>`; §11, the AF.1 and AF.2 entries); a server-half restart resumes the match from the last
snapshot with the tape truncated at a `resumed` marker (the match rewinds to it; the old process's tail is
abandoned, not replayed), every seat released to doctrine and the pilots returning through the reclaim, and a
match that cannot resume is voided by one of twenty-one named reasons (the AF.3 entry; `MindAbi` and `MindSource` since 17b cut M3.3, §11). Before AF a crash
voided the match. The result record (§4) holds the ledger's digest and log id, not the ledger.

**Custody** (D-16-1, D-16-20).

- *Check-out* happens at `Boarding → Running` (§3.8). The world task, in one authoritative action: writes
  on each unit record a `deployed { arena, snapshot }` marker where `snapshot` is the unit's cells and
  pose at check-out; leaves the unit's world placement in place, frozen (so the pilot's channel grants,
  which require a placed unit, survive); refuses ordinary mutation of a deployed unit at the real
  admission sites (`TacticsDestroyGate::check`, `TacticsDetachGate::check`, `unit_damage`,
  `unit_remove`, `@spatial` moves) with one named error; excludes the arena's domain from every world
  map and unit listing (§3.6); and sends `CustodyOut`.
- *Check-in* happens once, at `Ended`, `Aborted` or `Void`. Per unit, fallibly: the returned cells are
  applied as one `UnitMutationPlan` through `apply`, the final pose through `mush_sim::place`, both in
  the same authoritative action; a unit that no longer exists is journalled `custody_lost` and skipped,
  never failing the others. Then the marker is cleared, the tank is reset (§3.3), and seats, leases,
  focus and pods are released. On `Void` (panic or ledger overflow) the world restores each unit from
  the check-out snapshot and commits no arena-derived cell.
- *Boot reconciliation.* Since 16b cut AF.3 the tactics module's recovery pass keeps every `deployed`
  marker for the game crate's claim window; the game crate resumes the match a marker names from the two
  documents when every named check passes (§11, the AF.3 entry) and otherwise treats the marker as a voided
  match: restore the snapshot, clear the marker, release seats, leases, focus and pods, and write an
  `aborted(restart)` result with the reason in the log; a marker nobody claims is restored at
  `on_all_loaded`. Hot restart of `mush-server` under the gateway is routine, so both arms are exercised by
  tests that restart the server mid-`Running`.

**Failure.** `step` runs under `catch_unwind`; a panic voids the match (`CustodyIn` with `Void` and no
cells) and the world continues, consistent with the workspace's panic firewall (nothing a faulted turn
touched reaches the store). A pulse exceeding `tempo.stall_ms` (2,000) is voided the same way by the
watchdog, the pulse that ended the match included: the task reads the watchdog before it honours the end,
returns no units, and the black box keeps the arena's own end (16b cut AA.3, §11).

### 3.2 Space

**Pose is integer** (D-16-4): `Pose { x_cm: i64, y_cm: i64, z_cm: i32, heading_mdeg: u32, posture }`
with `heading_mdeg` in `0..360_000` clockwise from north. The `@spatial/place` wire keys become
`x_cm, y_cm, z_cm, heading_mdeg`; the legacy pose wire arm is deleted. Authored packs keep metres in JSON
and convert at load. `z_cm` is carried from the first build and equals the cell's `elev_cm` (the arena
makes it so at check-out; see §11 for elevation as built).

**Range** is one function used everywhere: Euclidean centimetres between two poses. Range bands,
angular size, flight time and the tempo trigger use it; the atlas `Trace` supplies cover and blocking
only, never distance (its `range_cells` is Chebyshev).

**The arena window** is one `mush-sim` domain over one region surface at `cell_cm = 1000`, 256 by 256
cells (D-16-5): 2.56 km square, the current `MAX_TILE_SIDE` (extended up to 512 by 512 cells across up to
sixteen 128-cell heightfield quadrants in WG.6, §11; doc 30 D-30-7). The path to 10 km is a per-driver tile
side and a tiled composite, specified in §11 as an addition and re-run through the harness first.

**Terrain** is the existing 18-class cell grid with the engine's cover and opacity per class. The ground is
content, not code (`tacsim::ground`, `ground.draft.v1`, doc 30 D-30-1): biome (`temperate`, `mars`,
`lunar`, `ice`) is a region property (`RegionDef.biome`, doc 30 D-30-5), and `rock` is a surface class
with no synthetic height of its own (doc 30 D-30-10). The first
arena is authored with roads, open ground, light and heavy woods, shallow water, a few buildings, two
team entry zones, five named key spots (four until 16b cut AE.1 added The Bluff, §11; drawn, named and answered to on every surface a pilot
reads the ground on since 16b cut V.1, §11), and a waypoint graph for the reflex controller (§6; *the stock mind's and the motor's since 17b cut M4.3, the reflex deleted in M4.6c, §11*).
The proving ground is surveyed ground: its terrain is known to every pilot at check-out, its units are
not (16b cut Q.3, §11).

**Line of sight** is the atlas `line_of_sight` trace's per-cell rule over the shared window with
`Eye{cell, height_cm}` from the unit definition's `sensor_height_cm` (new field; 150 by default). The
arena walks it as `sight_only` (16b cut J.1, §11): one line from the canonical end whichever end asks,
stopping at the first blocker, exact against the walk in everything the pass reads; and over the
terrain's own horizon index (J.2), a read-only pyramid built with the grid at check-out that proves a
run of the line clear without reading it or blocked by reading one cell. There is no cross-observer
cache: the index caches terrain, nothing observed. Each observer's pass traces its own candidates
(§3.6), and the harness bounds traces and cell probes per pulse and reports horizon probes. **Direct
fire** is the atlas `direct_fire_path` (the full walk, `trace_cells`), whose `target_cover` finally has
a consumer (§3.5).

**The spatial index** is a per-arena uniform grid of cell buckets over unit positions, rebuilt every
substep, used for range culls, in-range pair detection, impact candidate lookup and occupancy.

**Edges** stop motion. A unit cannot leave the window.

### 3.3 Units

**Unit truth is M32** (D-16-6): `TacticalUnit` with sections, facets, `ScalarCell`s, components,
capabilities and a `MobilityProfile`; all damage is a `UnitMutationPlan` through `apply`; refuse, never
clamp. No combat-specific unit type exists.

**The stock tanks are persistent objects.** Twelve tank objects (eight until 16b cut Z.3), one per pod, live in the arena's domain
and are created once by the centre's content (§4). At check-in and at every `Idle` transition each is
reset to its `UnitSpec` by one `UnitMutationPlan` (cells to initial values, conditions to `intact`,
capabilities restored). No objects are created or destroyed per match.

**The stock tank** (`line_tank`, one definition):

| Section | Facets (armor cells) | Section cells | Consequence at zero internals |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hull` | `front`, `left`, `right`, `rear`, `top` | `internals` | `destroy_unit` |
| `turret` | `front`, `left`, `right`, `rear` | `internals` | `remove_weapons(turret)`, `destroy_core` (17b cut M3.6; the heavy's turret `degrade_core` instead) |
| `left_track` | `left` | `internals` | `slow(50)`, `no_turn_toward(left)`, `disable_motion_when_all_of(left_track, right_track)` |
| `right_track` | `right` | `internals` | `slow(50)`, `no_turn_toward(right)`, `disable_motion_when_all_of(left_track, right_track)` |
| `engine` | (none; struck through `hull.rear`) | `internals` | `disable_motion` |

Components: `cannon` (turret; cells `ammo`, `ready_at_ms`), `machine_gun` (turret, coaxial; cells
`ammo`, `ready_at_ms`), `visual_sensor` (turret; cell `condition`), `power` (hull; capability
`tacsim.power` with state `off | starting | online`), `core` (turret, since 17b cut M3.1; cell `core_class` 0..=3, capability `tacsim.core` carrying the class's profile: doc 17 §9, §11). `sensor_height_cm` and the silhouette table are
unit-level fields. Every number lives in the definition pack (§4).

**The range target** (`target_mk1`, 16b cut O.3, §11): the stock tank's five sections, consequences,
facet table, silhouettes and `sensor_height_cm` verbatim, the `power` role alone, no weapon, no sensor,
no pod and `max_speed_cms` 0, the fielded catalog's sixth extension table; `SIM RANGE` fields it on the
targets' pods (§3.7, §3.8) and it is inert to the reflex controller (§6). (*Amended, §11: since 17b
cut M4.3 a mind flies it, and since cut M4.6b, under STOP B, it starts up and then parks `Disabled`
once online; the reflex controller was deleted in cut M4.6c.*)

**A wreck** is a defined M32 state: `hull` condition `wreck` (a named `ConditionId`) plus
`RevokeCapability` of `tacsim.power`, `tactics.mobility` and every `tacsim.weapon.*` capability, applied
by the `destroy_unit` consequence in one plan. The game-crate predicate `is_wreck(unit)` reads exactly
that. A wreck is an obstacle and a contact, never a target of a lock, a shot or an impact, which read the
physics; whether a CONTROLLER still treats a held contact as a target is knowledge (§6; 16b cut E.2,
§11): a machine keeps a target it has not read dead until the contact expires.

**Facet attribution** (W12): the impact's relative bearing selects a facet band; a table in the
`UnitSpec` maps each band to the sections it can strike with weights. The default sends `front` to hull
and turret, `rear` to hull, engine and turret, sides to hull, the near track and turret, `top` only to
hull and turret. No geometry is computed.

**Silhouette**: each facet band declares a presented width and height in centimetres.

**Mobility mode rules** for `tracked`: `max_speed_cms`, `accel_cms2`, `brake_cms2`, `turn_rate_mdeg_s`,
`reverse_factor_pct`, decoded from the opaque `ModeRules` by the game crate.

**Startup**: `STARTUP` sets `power` to `starting` and schedules `online` at `now + match.startup_ms`
(10,000) as a due event; until `online`, motion and fire intents refuse with a named error; repeating
`STARTUP` is idempotent.

**Stations are on the tank.** The pod's station record lives on the tank object (`stations` and
`assignments` are keyed by unit), so the `helm`, `sensors` and `fire` grants have the tank as subject.
The pod is where the body sits.

### 3.4 Motion

Per unit per **substep** (§3.1), with `dt` the substep's milliseconds:

1. `heading` turns toward the desired heading at `turn_rate_mdeg_s`, shortest arc, closed toward a
   destroyed track's side (the turn takes the long way round) and closed both ways with both tracks
   gone, when the tank neither turns nor moves (16b cut M.4, §11; the pair rule is authored on both
   tracks of the five tank packs at version 2). A carried remainder dies on a flip of its side or its
   rate.
2. `speed` moves toward the desired speed at `accel_cms2` (or `brake_cms2` when reducing), capped by
   `max_speed_cms × terrain_factor × slow_consequences`, where `terrain_factor = 100 / move_cost` of the
   cell the unit is in, priced on the unit's own locomotion row (16b cut AE.1, §11: tracked, wheeled or
   legged, read at check-out beside its slope limit).
3. The position advances `speed × dt` along the heading in centimetres. By construction of the substep
   this crosses at most one cell boundary.
4. If a cell boundary is crossed, the cell entered is tested: impassable for the unit's row (AE.1), too steep, or off
   the window stops the unit at the boundary with `speed = 0` and one `blocked` line, one `Held:`
   notice and one lesson for the machine per stop, where a stop is one held edge (16b cut M.3, §11):
   the unit is held against the refused cell and its cause, a re-refusal of the same obstacle is silent
   (the exact cell for occupancy; for ground, slope and the window's edge any cell adjacent to the held
   one, since a slanted push slides between the diagonal and the orthogonal neighbour), a different
   cause or a cell not adjacent is a new stop, and the hold clears on entering another cell, on parking,
   or on an accepted in-cell move pointing strictly away from the held cell; `held` is hashed;
   occupied by another unit holds the unit at the boundary keeping its speed, silently, for
   `[tacsim.arena].occupied_stop_ms` (2,000) of arena time and then stops it the same way (16b cut E.3,
   §11). **Invariant: no unit ever occupies a cell that was not tested on entry.**
5. Occupancy is one unit per cell at 10 m cells; there is no ramming and no collision damage.

Disabled units stay on the map as obstacles and contacts. Wrecks (§3.3) likewise.

### 3.5 Weapons, resolution, damage

**Weapon definition** (§4): `delivery: direct | beam`, `mount` (section and facet), range bands with
`max_range_cm` and a per-band `dispersion_mrad`, `cycle_ms` (floor 1,000, W14), `ammunition`,
`burst_rounds` (1 for the cannon, `k` for the machine gun), `muzzle_speed_cms`, `heat_per_shot` (0 for
both stock weapons; the cell exists for lasers).

**Ammunition definition**: `damage` (one fixed value), `penetration_class` (carried, unused by the
stock rules), `count_per_load`.

**Draw labels** (doc 09 C5). Every draw is `draw(match_seed, label)`, a keyed hash, with these labels
and no others: `hit:<shot_seq>`, `facet:<shot_seq>`, `burst:<shot_seq>`,
`classify:<observer>:<target>:<pass_seq>`, `contact:<viewer>:<target>` (the held contact id, §3.6)
and `mind:<unit>:<think_seq>:<n>` (a unit mind's n-th draw of one think; 17b cut M2.2, §11, doc 17
D-UM-29). Adding a draw can never shift another. The label set is
pinned by a contract test, and the arena is recorded as exempt from `provider_rng_policy` because it is
not a kernel action provider.

**Resolution** (D-16-8, 16a Q33), at the moment `FIRE` is accepted — for a pilot's `FIRE` the end of
the pulse that accepted it, for a machine's its own tick (16b cut M.2, §11):

1. Presented silhouette: the target's facet band toward the shooter gives width and height; cover from
   `direct_fire_path.target_cover` scales the presented area (`Partial` halves it, `Full` zeroes it).
   Angular size in milliradians is `width_cm × 1000 / range_cm`.
2. Error cone: the weapon's `dispersion_mrad` for the band, plus the terms of the named config table
   `resolution.error_terms`: shooter speed, target lateral speed, gunnery (every stock rating is 1
   through the `PilotProfile` seam), the condition of the sight (the sensor component the profile
   names, never a pod's; 16b cut M.4).
3. `p_permille = hit_curve(angular_size_mrad × 1000 / error_mrad)`, a named monotone table in config
   with interpolation, clamped to `[resolution.p_min, resolution.p_max]`; zero presented area is zero,
   and a shot past the weapon's last band is blind at zero, not clamped to `p_min` (16b cut M.4, 16c
   Q19: the cockpit's `beyond` quotes 0 per cent).
4. `u = draw(seed, hit:<shot_seq>) mod 1000`; hit if `u < p_permille`. A burst of `k` rounds is one
   binomial sample from `(k, p)` using `burst:<shot_seq>`.
5. The cockpit shows `p_permille` as a percentage in `WEAPONSTATUS` and on `LOCK` before any shot.

**Acceptance and cost.** `FIRE` requires power online, a lock on a contact the platform holds, a ready
weapon and ammunition. A shot whose line is blocked at acceptance, or whose target is behind full cover,
is **accepted**: it consumes ammunition and the cycle exactly like any shot and resolves as a `miss`
with the blocker as its reason. There is no free probe of line of sight.

**Scheduled impact.** An accepted shot sets `ready_at_ms`, decrements `ammo`, records `fired` (since 17b cut M1b.1 with `by` and `slot` when a condition slot or a cited mind shot fired it), and
schedules `impact_at_ms = now + range_cm × 1000 / muzzle_speed_cms`. At impact (a due event, §3.1) the
arena re-validates: the target exists and is not a wreck, its position is within one cell of the
prediction (a target that changed facet band re-resolves with the same `hit` draw; a shot fired past
the last band stays blind), the line is clear from where the round left (since 16b cut AE.2 the impact
traces the line to the target's current cell from the shot's launch eye and reads the facet from the
launch point, whether or not the shooter still stands there, §11).
A failed validation is a `miss` with its reason, which the shooter reads only for a contact its platform
still holds (otherwise `Miss.` in the cockpit and `miss.` in the debrief: AE.2, §11). Beams have `impact_at_ms = now` and land in the pulse
that fired them (16b cut M.2).

**Damage.** A hit selects a section from the facet table by weight (`facet:<shot_seq>`), reduces the
facet's `armor` cell by `damage` down to its floor, applies overflow to the section's `internals`, and
at zero internals sets the section `destroyed` and applies its consequence. All of it is one
`UnitMutationPlan` per impact through `apply`, journalled, with one `hit` ledger event carrying the
plan's cause, both draws (the hit or burst draw and the facet draw) and `armor_section`, the section
whose armour the round spent when it struck through to another (16b cut M.3). `destroy_unit` makes a
wreck (§3.3) and, when it eliminates a side, ends the match (§3.8).
Doc 10 3A holds: disabled precedes destroyed; capture and salvage are not arena concepts.

### 3.6 Perception

**Reused as vocabulary and projection rules, not as storage** (D-16-10): the contact lifecycle
(`observe | refresh | share | unshare | loss | reacquire`), the refresh and TTL bands, and the
existence-hiding rule that an unauthorised viewer's answer is byte-identical to a nonexistent unit's.

**Arena contacts are an in-RAM shadow with their own identity space.** The arena mints one contact id
per `(viewer platform, target)` as a random 64-bit value from `draw(seed, contact:<viewer>:<target>)`,
never sequential, never reused within a match, formatted as the existing opaque hex. It is a distinct
type from `mush_core::ContactId` and never crosses to the durable `tactics/contacts` family, which the
arena cannot write. A pilot neither sees nor types this id (16b cut Q.1, §11): a contact is shown and
typed as a short designator, a provisional viewer-scoped `X<n>` until it is classified and then the team
letter and the slot letter, and the id stays what the arena receives, so a lock survives the rename.
Any name a pilot types that is unresolvable, unowned or expired gets one byte-identical refusal.
Contacts die with the match; the ledger's `detected`, `classified` and `lost` events are the record.

**The pass.** Each unit's pass is due every `sensors.pass_ms` (1,000) of arena time, staggered by
`stagger_ms(seed, id, pass_ms)` (a seeded hash of the unit id, not a draw; 16b cut U.1), and runs as a
due event at its timestamp against the positions at that substep boundary:

1. Candidates: units within `sensors.visual.max_range_cells × sensors.max_signature_factor` of the
   observer through the spatial index, excluding wrecks unless `sensors.visual.sees_wrecks`. The
   factor is at least 1.0 and ships as 1.0 (16b cut M.4: below it the cull would be shorter than the
   range test, and no signature row exceeds 1.0), so the cull is exact.
2. For each candidate, `line_of_sight` from the observer's `Eye` to the candidate's `Eye` must be
   `Clear` or `Partial` with attenuation under `sensors.visual.max_attenuation`. The line is one line
   whichever end it is walked from, in cells and in height (16b cut M.4): A sees B exactly when B sees
   A at equal eyes; it is walked once, from the canonical end, by `sight_only` over the horizon index
   (16b cuts J.1 and J.2, §11).
3. Range test: `range_cm ≤ max_range_cm × signature_factor(target class, posture)`; a stopped tank in
   heavy woods has a lower factor than one moving on a road.
4. A candidate that passes becomes or refreshes a contact with `class = unknown`; after
   `sensors.classify_after_ms` of detection with no gap longer than `sensors.pass_ms` it becomes
   `classified` with the unit's class and team (`classify:<observer>:<target>:<pass_seq>` if the rules
   ever need a draw). Loss follows the TTL.
5. Team sharing (W6): a unit's classified contacts are shared with teammates within
   `comms.share_range_cells` and LOS, as `share` operations bounded per pulse.

**World-side exclusion.** While an arena holds custody, its domain is excluded from `visible_rooms`,
`units_for`, the atlas `+map` frame, `look`, and every world unit listing, for every viewer including
staff. A deployed unit's `unit.status` answers "deployed" to a viewer stationed on it and the
existence-hiding line to anyone else. The only views of arena state are the cockpit frames the arena
builds per pilot. `@find` lists only what `can_perceive` admits, so a held unit is omitted for its owner
and for staff alike with no sentence added, and the world-side propagation pass names a held unit as
neither observer, subject, refreshed holder, expiring holder nor share issuer or recipient (16b cut U.4);
staff `@arena/status` alone names a stranded `deployed` marker's dbref, so `@arena/release` has something
to take. Every other `match_object` call site is listed in `matcher-call-sites.toml` with its guard word,
`locality` and `staff` not satisfying this exclusion (H6's work list).

**What a pilot sees**: `CONTACTS` lists the platform's contacts with designator (§11, cut Q.1), class or
`unknown`, bearing, range band, age and, since 17b cut M1a.3, the side the contact shows (`front`, `side`,
`rear`) and on an armed hull the default weapon's odds against a classified contact or a dash (§11); notices fire once per transition (`acquired`, `classified`, `lost`)
with hysteresis. The hysteresis expires at its own arena millisecond at every tempo (16b cut M.2): a
sensor pass landing exactly on the deadline is applied before the expiry, a mesh share landing on it
after. A loss cancelled inside the hysteresis window is silent unless the row comes back
`unknown` under a different name: then the regression is said once (`unidentified`: `BA is unidentified
again: X2.`) and the old name is never accepted as an alias; a mesh insert that arrives classified
inside the window says the identification once with the old name (`Contact X1 identified: BA, blue.`;
16b cut M.2). The platform is also told when its own
receiver becomes degraded by jamming and when it clears (`receiver_degraded`, `receiver_clear`), with no
source, bearing, team or id (16b cut D.4, §11), and since 16b cut O.2 when its own pod is switched
(`emcon_on`, `emcon_off`, carrying the pod's radius and nothing of anyone else; §11), and since 17b cut M1a.4
when its own `DRIVE` arrives or finds no route (`drive_arrived`, `drive_no_route`; §11). The band word is scope advice, read against the lesser of
the platform's own sight and the fired weapon's last band (16b cut D.1, §11). `VIEW` renders the arena window from terrain plus the platform's own contacts, and since 16b cut AG.1 `VIEW BRIEF` says that window in sentences off the same payload (§11); `SCAN`
reads an identified contact's sections as green, yellow, red or destroyed from a close look, never as a
number (§11, cut Q.2). Teammates appear when shared. The ground is surveyed and the units on it are not
(§3.2). Nothing else about another unit crosses to a pilot before detection, on any
surface, and A1 asserts it surface by surface.

The ECM and ECCM hooks (§9) are two null fields on the sensor and unit rows today: `emission` and
`jamming`.

### 3.7 Control and the cockpit

**Pods** are Things that are interior spaces (`SpatialParent::Interior{space: pod, slot}`), twelve per
centre, six per team room (eight and four until 16b cut Z.3); the lobby, red room and blue room are three rooms off the OOC welcome hall,
authored in the atlas-owned OOC neighborhood pack at its next version (§4).

**Boarding** (`BOARD`, contextual in a team room for a pilot who holds a slot) places the character in
the pod, assigns the tank's station to the character, activates the seat with channels `helm`,
`sensors`, `fire` through `request_seat_activation`, refreshes actor links, and moves the account's
focus to the station. `EXIT` reverses it and sends `Control{Released}`.

**Verbs** (D-16-11), contextual while focused on a station, each a selector that stages one intent
(`ORDER`, since 16b cut Y.1, stages one per machine it reaches; §11):

| Verb | Arguments | Refusals |
|---|---|---|
| `STARTUP` | none | none (idempotent) |
| `HEADING <deg> [QUIET]` | 0 to 359; `QUIET` takes the order without the `Steering to …°.` acknowledgement (the picture's keys send it, doc 21 §7.3; refusals still speak) | power not online |
| `SPEED <kph> [QUIET]` | minus the reverse ceiling to max; a negative speed reverses (16b cut D.3); `QUIET` as `HEADING`'s | power not online; past either ceiling |
| `VIEW [BRIEF]` | none draws the map; `BRIEF`, case-folded, says the same window in sentences (16b cut AG.1, §11) | any other word: the one usage line `VIEW [BRIEF]; BRIEF says the window in sentences.`, the same bytes for every word and never an echo (AG.1) |
| `CONTACTS` | none | none |
| `LOCK <contact>` | a designator, `X<n>` or team and slot letters, case-folded (16b cut Q.1) | one byte-identical refusal for any name the platform does not hold |
| `FIRE [<weapon>]`; since 17b cut M1b.4 `FIRE <weapon> WHEN <condition> [AND <condition>]…` (at most four, no `OR`, over `odds [weapon] >= n`, `aspect = front, side or rear`, `range < or > n m`, `band = close, effective or far`, `rounds > n or n%`, `reserve`, `target = identified`, `lock`) and `FIRE <weapon or ALL> OFF` (§11) | a weapon id, its short name (`mg`) or a unique prefix, case-folded (16b cut D.2); default cannon; an arming names its weapon | no lock, cycling, empty, power not online; a word two weapons answer to; an arming: a condition outside the eight, one under the match's Compact floor (`unlawful`, refused as typed off the frame and again at the landing), every slot armed (`no_slot`; since 17b cut M2.2 told by the arena a pulse after the acceptance line, the cockpit's count pre-check gone); each reads `FIRE <weapon> refused. …`; since 17b cut M2.2 `range < n m` fires strictly nearer than `n` and `range > n m` strictly farther (§11) |
| `WEAPONSTATUS` | none; since 17b cut M1b.4 an armed weapon's row is followed by its arming line (`  S1: >=60% side/rear`) | none |
| `STATUS` | none (since 16b cut V.1 it also names the nearest key spot with its letter, range and bearing) | none |
| `RANGE [<contact>]` | a designator, else a key spot's whole name or its letter, case-folded (16b cut V.1); default the lock (Q.1) | neither a lock nor a name (its own line, not `FIRE`'s); the one byte-identical refusal for a word that is neither a name the platform holds nor a spot; a spot named before the first frame (`The panel has told you nothing yet.`, V.1) |
| `BEARING [<contact>]` | as `RANGE` (a designator, else a spot; default the lock) | as `RANGE` |
| `SCAN [<contact>]` | a designator; default the lock (Q.2) | as `RANGE`; the contact is not identified yet; no reading yet, close the range for a look |
| `ORDER <squad or ALL> HOLD, ADVANCE <spot>, ENGAGE [<contact>], WITHDRAW, SCREEN <bearing> or SUPPORT <callsign> [HOLD FIRE]`, and `ORDER <squad or ALL> RELEASE` | a squad by its word (`red-1`) or its bare ordinal on the pilot's own side, or `ALL`; an `ENGAGE` hint resolved on the pilot's own held rows, an `ADVANCE` spot as `RANGE` resolves one, a `SCREEN` bearing in degrees as `BEARING` prints one, a `SUPPORT` callsign a tank of the pilot's own side (their own legal); the side's lead orders any squad of the side, anyone else the machine in their pod pair, addressed by its squad's word and acknowledged by its callsign (16b cut Y.1, §11); `RELEASE` hands the squad back to doctrine (*to their own minds since 17b cut M5.6, §11*) at once (16b cut Z.2, §11) | usage; `DESTROYED`; one byte-identical `No such squad.` for the other side's, a nonexistent and an unauthorised squad alike, before any contact or callsign is looked at; `You have no squad to order.` for an empty `ALL`; the one byte-identical refusal for an `ENGAGE` word no held row answers to or an `ADVANCE` word that is no spot; one byte-identical `No tank of your side flies that callsign.` for a `SUPPORT` word that is the other side's, provisional, empty or nonsense; the seat's refusal; W8's span and rate (Y.2, §7), a `RELEASE` spending the rate and never a span slot |
| `EMCON on`, `EMCON off`, bare `EMCON` | `on` or `off`, case-folded; bare reads the switch off the last frame (16b cut O.2, §11) | this tank carries no jamming pod (off the frame's `ecm`, refused locally, nothing forwarded); no frame yet, bare or worded; `EMCON is already on.` or `off.`, read from the bridge's hint first and the mirror second; usage; the seat's refusals |
| `WATCH on`, `WATCH off`, bare `WATCH` | `on` or `off`, case-folded; bare says which it is; `on` also says the held frame's block at once (16b cut AP.3, §11); stages no intent and reads nothing but the seat's own switch, so a wreck may watch | the one usage line `WATCH [ON\|OFF]; bare WATCH says which.` for any other word; the seat's refusals (the one byte-identical `You are not at the station of a deployed unit.` outside a cockpit, as for every selector) |
| `DRIVE <spot or x,y>`, `DRIVE HOLD`, `DRIVE OFF` | a key spot's whole name or its letter as `RANGE` resolves one, or an arena cell `x,y`, case-folded (17b cut M1a.4, §11); stages one continuous `Goal` on the helm, counted on the seat's rate, that the hull's own drive-by-wire follows until it arrives (`DRIVE: arrived.`) or finds no route (`DRIVE: no route from here; holding.`); `HOLD` stops where the tank is with no goal, `OFF` lets go of the drive, and a `HEADING` or `SPEED` typed afterward disengages it | destroyed, cannot move and power not online, in `HEADING`'s words; the one usage line `DRIVE <spot\|x,y\|HOLD\|OFF>; STATUS names the nearest spot.` for the bare verb; one byte-identical `DRIVE refused. That is not a spot, a cell, HOLD or OFF.` for any other word, a contact's designator included; `DRIVE refused. That cell is not on the window.` for a cell off the ground; the seat rate's refusal past `max_pending_per_unit` |
| `MIND`, `MIND PROFILE <name>`, `MIND RULES` | bare, none: reads the hull's sealed mind off the last frame's `mind` key (17b cut M5.4, §11) in at most seven lines (four today: the mind and the class it thinks at on its core, both read live; the profile it will fly, the others sealed and the switch; the channels the pilot holds, the mind dormant while they fly; `Rules: none sealed`), `RULES` its one rules line, both staging no intent; `PROFILE`, a profile sealed on the hull by its name, case-folded: stages one `Profile` intent on the helm that the arena takes from a seat alone, once in ten seconds, with no power test (a cold tank switches) | before any frame `The panel has told you nothing yet.`, on a hull no seal reached `This hull carries no seal.`; for `PROFILE`, each in one line opening `MIND refused.` whether the cockpit or the arena refuses it: a profile not sealed (a word no preset answers never echoed), the profile already flown, inside ten seconds of the last switch (the countdown read on the mirror's arena clock), no frame, no seal, a wreck, and the arena's own `MIND refused. That profile cannot be switched to now.`; `MIND TRACE refused. This core keeps no trace of its thinks yet.` until doc 17's M6; any other word the usage line `MIND [PROFILE <name>\|RULES]; bare MIND reads your tank's sealed mind.` |
| `EXIT` | none | none |

`RANGE`, `BEARING` and `SCAN`, like the four read verbs before them, stage no intent: they answer from
the pilot's last frame (§11; a key spot's range and bearing from the pose on that frame, with no stale
clause, since 16b cut V.1). Since 16b cut D.2 every ordering verb (`STARTUP`, `HEADING`, `SPEED`,
`LOCK`, `FIRE`, since 16b cut O.2 `EMCON` and since 17b cut M1a.4 `DRIVE`) is refused locally on a destroyed tank, and the motion verbs (`HEADING`, `SPEED` and `DRIVE`) on one that cannot
move, off the frame's `wreck` and `immobile` keys; since D.1 `STARTUP` answers from the mirror when
power is already online or coming up (§11). Since 16b cut AP.1 `FIRE`'s cycling pre-check and
`WEAPONSTATUS` read the last frame's countdown aged by the wall clock since the frame's receipt, unaged
under a staff tempo floor, so a `FIRE` typed once the gun is truly ready is forwarded and the arena takes
it at its next wake (§11); since AP.3 `WATCH`, the fifteenth selector, stages no intent either (§11). Since 17b cut
M1a.4 `DRIVE` is the sixteenth selector and stages the continuous `Goal` the native motor drives (§11); since 17b cut
M5.4 `MIND` is the seventeenth, a read of the keyframe's `mind` key whose `PROFILE` form stages one intent (§11).

Lobby and team-room verbs: `SIM TEAM red|blue`, `SIM LEAVE`, `SIM START`, `SIM ABORT`, `SIM STATUS`
(while a match runs a player reads their own side in full and the other side as index, callsign and
pilot name alone; 16b cut D.1), `SIM AI [<n>]` (the size of the AI fill; a side's lead or staff, during
`Selection`, on a server with `[tacsim.match].ai_fill` on; 16b cut Q.7), `SIM UNIT [<type>]` (what the
caller's own slot will field, during `Selection`; Q.8), `SIM MIND [<mind> [PROFILE <p>[, <p>]] or none]` (since 17b cut M5.1: the mind the caller's own slot will
seal, chosen during `Selection` from the holdings of the character who boards — Standard Doctrine and its six presets in
production — with as many profiles as the sealed class keeps, fitted at the choice and told to the caller alone; §11),
`SIM AI MIND [red or blue] [<mind> [PROFILE <p>] or none]` (the mind a side's AI fill will seal, set during `Selection` by
the side's lead from their own holdings or by staff on Standard Doctrine's presets, answered in `SIM AI`'s order; M5.1, §11), `SIM MODE [<mode>]` (the rule the next match is
fought under, `skirmish` or `hold`, refused on a ground whose pack declares no such mode, and `range` only
through `SIM RANGE`; set during `Selection` by a side's lead or staff and announced to the centre; 16b cut
O.1), `SIM RANGE [<n>]` (a range of `n` targets on the other side's pods, 1 to that side's pods, set during
`Selection` by a side's lead or staff with every claimed slot on one side and announced to the centre;
O.3), `SIM CONCEDE` (a side's lead, while the match
runs; 16b cut D.3), `SIM HISTORY` (the centre's last twenty results in public words, newest first;
16b cut V.2), `SIM REPORT [<match>]` (the reader's own debrief of a match they flew, read from the
report document written at check-in; V.3), `SIM REPLAY <match> [<m:ss> or next or prev or first or last]
[<callsign>]` (since 16b cut I.1: the objective replay of a finished match the centre remembers, both
sides shown and said so, public in the centre with no pilot gate, one keyframe's window in line mode
and the whole document on screen 0 of the TUI and the web; `Match N is still running.` is its one
answer for an unfinished match), `BOARD` (since 16b cut Y.3 also during `Running`, from the
pod or the team room, for a pilot whose seat was dropped: §3.8). Bare `SIM AI` and bare `SIM UNIT` are reads and answer anybody in the
centre in any state (D.1), as do `SIM HISTORY`, `SIM REPORT`, `SIM REPLAY`, bare `SIM MODE` and bare `SIM RANGE` (O.1, O.3), and bare `SIM MIND` and
bare `SIM AI MIND`, each the reader's own read, fogged by the centre's state (17b cut M5.1, §11). Staff have `@arena/status|start|intent|abort|reset|release|pose|report|tempo|place|watch` (`release`
since D.3; `tempo`, `place` and `watch` since 16b cut I.3 and I.4: `@arena/tempo <match>=<period_ms>` floors a
running match's wake interval without pausing it, `@arena/place <unit>=<x>,<y>` moves a unit under the
entry test with the tape recording it and its pilot told nothing, `@arena/watch <match>` binds at most
eight staff who hold no slot and no seat to the match's `Observer` picture on `arena.observer` (§11);
`report` since 16b cut V.3: `@arena/report [<match>] [<chunk>]` pages the match's tape
sixteen chunks a request, one line an event with every field; `pose` since 16b cut U.4: `@arena/pose <callsign>=<x> <y>[ <heading>]` sets a slot's opening
cell for the NEXT match, kept on the match book beside the centre, validated against the centre's arena
when set, refused while a match is live, cleared when the match that used it finishes, by `=off` and by
`@arena/reset`; `@arena/status` also names any stranded `deployed` marker). The shipped `mush-sim` combat verbs (`+fire +launch +heading +thrust
+posture +dc +dock +eject`), their `tactical_intent` staging and the placeholder resolution are retired with this build
(D-16-20); ships and other domains later get verbs on the same arena model.

### 3.8 Lifecycle

```text
Idle → Selection → Boarding → Running → Ended → Idle
                 ↘        ↘        ↘
                  Aborted ← Aborted ← Aborted | Void → Idle
```

- `Selection`: `SIM TEAM` claims a slot; one slot per account; one to `match.max_slots_per_side` per
  side; no budgets, terms or consent (16a Q28). `SIM UNIT` chooses what the caller's own slot fields,
  `SIM AI` sizes the fill, `SIM MODE` picks the rule and `SIM RANGE` makes it a range (§3.7; 16b cuts O.1
  and O.3).
- `Boarding`: `SIM START` (a team lead or staff). With `[tacsim.match].ai_fill` on it needs one claimed
  slot in the whole centre, and the machines take the unclaimed pods of both sides (16b cut Q.7, §11);
  with the fill off it needs at least one pilot per side (and, since 16b cut AE.3, a gun on every side that
  is not a range's targets: `NoGun`, §11); under a range (16b cut O.3, §11) it needs one
  claimed slot on the shooters' side, fill or no fill: the machines take no pod, the targets' pods 1 to
  `n` are fitted to `target_mk1` and fielded unseated, flown by a mind (17b cut M4.3; the reflex
  controller before it), and the deadline and a
  leave drop shooters alone, aborting only a centre with no slot. After every refusal and before a pod locks,
  every pod's tank is fitted to its slot's chosen type or else its authored one (Q.8, §11); pods lock to
  their pilots; pilots `BOARD`. The transition `Boarding → Running` fires when every claimed slot is
  seated or `match.board_timeout_ms` elapses with unseated slots dropped (with the fill off an empty side
  aborts; with it on only an empty centre does; a dropped lead, or a lead who leaves the tank mid-match, hands the side to its first remaining
  claimant who has not left, as `SIM LEAVE` does: 16b cuts AA.2 and B.4, §11). Check-out happens on that transition.
- `Running`: the arena steps. Team elimination (every unit of a side a wreck or disabled with no weapons;
  since 16b cut AE.3 also a side whose every unit still in action is a drone when it fielded a non-drone, and
  since 16b cut AP.2 also a side that came in armed and whose in-action units have all had their weapons
  shot away, on either side by D-16-5, both at once the elimination draw, §11)
  ends the match, a range's targets included (16b cut O.3); under Hold (16b cut O.1, §11) a side alone
  (its drones counting for nothing, AE.3) inside the declared square of the key spot for the declared time wins, reason `hold`, settled by its
  own due event at its own millisecond before elimination and `spent`, elimination still ending it; both
  sides running dry with nothing in flight ends it as a draw, reason `spent` (Q.7, §11; never a range,
  whose targets were never armed); `match.max_duration_ms` (900,000 since 16b cut AP.2, 16c Q47) ends it as a timeout; `SIM ABORT` by any participant or staff aborts it;
  `SIM CONCEDE` by a side's lead ends it as a recorded win for the other side, reason `conceded`; a match
  whose every pilot has left ends by itself `match.unattended_ms` (300,000) later, outcome `timeout`,
  reason `unattended` (both 16b cut D.3, §11); a panic or ledger overflow voids it.
- `Ended | Aborted | Void`: check-in (§3.1); result written; tanks reset; seats, leases, focus and pods
  released atomically; the reset is idempotent and re-runnable by staff. An abort from `Selection` or
  `Boarding` has no custody to return: it releases slots and pods and, since D.3, puts the stock tanks
  back, through the one funnel every end before `Running` takes, a refused `Boarding → Running`
  transition included. In `Selection` `SIM ABORT` is staff's alone; `SIM LEAVE` is a player's way out.
- Disconnects: on linkdead onset the world sends `Control{Linkdead}`; the tank keeps its continuous
  orders and fires nothing; on revival within the doc 07 D1 grace the pilot reclaims the seat at a pulse
  boundary with a new epoch; after the grace, or at once on a `QUIT`, the seat is released and the hull's
  own mind (§6; since 17b cut M4.3 — the reflex controller before it), seeded `Withdraw`, flies the tank
  home, the board reads `flown by doctrine` (*`flown by its mind` since 17b cut M5.3, §11*), and since 16b cut Y.3 the pilot is
  told so on reconnect and may `BOARD` again from their pod or their team room once
  `match.reclaim_min_ms` (5,000) has passed: the seat path runs again, `Live` goes out at a new epoch on
  every channel, a control below a channel's epoch purges and applies nothing, the tank is theirs where
  it stands, the heir keeps the side's lead (16c Q42), and the first frame back carries the catch-up
  report (Y.4, §5); `EXIT` stays one-way, before or after a drop, and a match whose stop is in flight
  refuses the reclaim (§11). Before 16b cut N.1 the released tank idled, "idle" substituting for doc 07
  §1.1's fallback controller until the reflex controller took that role (§11).

---

## 4. Records and definitions

**Content packs.** Game data lives in `crates/tacsim/content/` and loads through `mush-content` as
`RulesDefinition` packs with the existing latch; the centre's rooms live in the atlas-owned OOC pack:

```text
crates/mush-atlas/content/neighborhood.ooc_area.json   version 4: room budget raised to 10; the
                                                       simulator lobby, red room, blue room, their
                                                       exits, the selector item, and eight pod items (twelve since version 7, 16b cut Z.3)
                                                       carrying `interior: true` (new item field)
crates/tacsim/content/
  units/line_tank.json            UnitSpec: sections, facets, cells, components, capabilities,
                                  mobility mode rules, facet table, silhouettes, sensor_height_cm
  units/target_mk1.json           the range target (16b cut O.3): the stock sections, the power role
                                  alone, no weapon, no sensor, speed 0; the fielded catalog's sixth
                                  extension table, compiled in and never applied as a pack
  cores/cores.json                the three cores (17b cut M3.1): doctrine I, line II, command III;
                                  a class and the governed bit, the figures the class table's (doc 17 §9)
  weapons/cannon_105.json         delivery, mount, bands, dispersion, cycle_ms, ammunition, burst,
                                  muzzle speed, heat
  weapons/mg_coax.json
  ammunition/he_105.json          damage, penetration_class, count_per_load
  ammunition/ball_762.json
  sensors/visual_mk1.json         max_range_cells, max_attenuation, classify_after_ms, sees_wrecks,
                                  emission: null, jamming: null
  resolution.json                 hit_curve table, error_terms, p_min, p_max
  arenas/proving_ground.json      the 2.56 km arena: tiles, entry zones, key spots, waypoint graph,
                                  the stock tanks' initial placements (eight; twelve since version 3, 16b cut Z.3),
                                  the modes the ground supports (`modes`, version 4, 16b cut O.1: hold at
                                  The Crossroads, six cells, 120 s; outside the definition digest)
  arenas/winter_valley.json       the second shipped 2.56 km arena (`tacsim.arena.winter_valley`, WG.5, §11),
                                  extracted from Unity terrain/splatmap (`scripts/ground-extract/`) via
                                  `content/drafts/winter_valley.draft.json` (`ground.draft.v1`) and built by
                                  `ground-pack` into `tacsim.winter_valley` + `tacsim.pack.winter_valley`
  drafts/*.draft.json             `ground.draft.v1` authoring drafts (`proving_ground`, `winter_valley`),
                                  round-tripped through `tacsim::ground` (`generate`, `route`, `check`,
                                  `build`, `from_packs`, `render_png`; doc 30 §4–§8)
  resources.json                  the ten commodity families as InstrumentDef rows (Commodity),
                                  shipped unconsumed by the story
```

Every definition is registered through the C1 registry with an exact `DefinitionRef`; a persisted unit
points at exact versions; a changed definition is a new version with a migration chain.

**Arena state** (RAM): the arena definition, per-unit `{pose, speed_cms, desired_heading,
desired_speed, power, unit: TacticalUnit, ready_at per weapon, lock}`, the epoch table, contacts per
platform, due events (a min-heap by timestamp then sequence), the spatial index, the tempo, the ledger
egress ring.

**Ledger events** (JSON lines, chunked at 16 KiB, streamed): `{seq, t_ms, kind, ...}` with kinds
`match_started, tempo, startup, power_online (16b cut Q.1: at the startup due event's own timestamp),
position_summary (every unit, every ledger.summary_ms of arena time,
in every tempo), blocked (one per held edge, with its cause since 16b cut M.3), detected, classified,
lost, locked, fired, impact, hit, miss, section_destroyed,
unit_disabled, unit_destroyed, receiver (16b cut D.4: the unit and a degraded flag, one line per
transition of a platform's own receiver between clear and jammed, none for a change of degree), ordered
(16b cut Y.1: one line per unit an order landed on, at the pulse's own timestamp under phase 2's one
hash, carrying the squad, the issuer, the objective, the target hint, the spot, `hold_fire` and the time
to live; nothing for a refused order), order_released (16b cut Z.2: one line per unit a `RELEASE` dropped to doctrine,
at the pulse's timestamp under the same hash, carrying the squad and the issuer, distinct from `order_expired`), tempo_floor
(16b cut I.3: `period_ms` or `null`, once per change of the staff floor, under phase 5's hash), placed (I.3: `unit`, `x`, `y`,
`from_x`, `from_y`, under phase 4's hash) and place_refused (I.3: `unit`, `x`, `y` and a `reason` from `unknown`, `epoch`,
`channel`, `impassable`, `occupied`, `in_flight`, `stopped`; every place the arena receives leaves one of the two lines and no
notice), emcon (16b cut O.2: the unit and `on`, one line per real flip at the intent's own timestamp under
phase 4's hash, none for a no-op), lagged, mind_sealed (17b cut M3.6: one per unit after match_started, citing the sortie,
the mind, the source and card digests, the class, the governed bit, the licence and the principal), sortie_closed (M3.6: destroyed right after a
wreck's unit_destroyed, match_end for each open sortie immediately before the end's own line), mind_fault and mind_dropped (17b cut M3.4:
a mind's fault word; its drop to the ROM with the cause, the last fault's word and the budget),
match_ended, aborted, void`, each carrying unit ids, its draws (`draws`, a list of label and value,
absent when none: a `hit` carries the hit or burst draw and then the facet draw, a `miss` and a
`classified` one; 16b cut M.3), and the substep state hash. `section_destroyed` and
`unit_destroyed` carry `by`, the unit whose shot did it, left off when nothing shot it (D.4). There is no per-pulse `moved`. Bounds:
`ledger.summary_ms` (5,000), `ledger.max_bytes` (64 MiB; at 90% summaries stop, at 100% the match is
stopped with `Timeout`), `match.max_duration_ms` (900,000 since 16b cut AP.2; 3,600,000 before).

**The result record** (world side, one document per match, well under the 256 KiB record ceiling):
teams and pilots, outcome (`red | blue | draw | abort | timeout | void`) with reason, since 16b cut O.1
the `mode` the match was fought under (`skirmish`, `hold` or `range`; a restart abort's document, since 16b
cut AF.3 written only for a match the boot could not resume, carries none, and the replay document carries the same key, absent reading skirmish), per-unit outcome
(`intact | disabled | destroyed | custody_lost`, shots fired, hits), the ledger's log id and digest (the
digest over the bytes that left the arena in chunks, which the log reproduces; 16b cut M.1), the
arena definition digest, the match seed (staff-only: it re-derives every draw and every contact id, so
no player verb prints it; 16b cut M.3), start and end times. Since 16b cut V.2 every unit row and every
pilot row carries its `callsign` (read off the arena the world built, the one place the slot rule was
applied) and the ledger entry its `chunks` (the arena chunks appended; a restart abort writes `""` and
`0`), and since V.3 `reports` names the units a report document was written for: one
`tacsim:report:<match>:<unit>` document per human pilot, the pilot's own projection of the tape (§11),
written in the same action as the result and deleted when its match leaves the centre's twenty-row
history; `record_version` is still 1. Since 16b cut I.1 the result also carries `replay` (a boolean;
a restart abort writes `false`): one `tacsim:replay:<match>` document, the objective picture of both
sides (positional rows of cell and whole degrees per roster unit per keyframe, each shot's time and
outcome word, each wreck, callsigns, sides, the ground's digest and no object number, pilot, contact
id, draw, seed or z), folded as the tape passed the world and written in the same action before the
result, held under `MAP_MAX_BYTES` 256 KiB by a byte budget behind caps of 736 keyframes and 1,024
shots (`truncated` and a `cut` list say what was sealed off), deleted with the reports when the match
leaves the ring, and read from RAM by `SIM REPLAY` and the `arena.replay` query (§11). The six outcome words are closed; a match that ends
because both sides ran dry is a `draw` whose reason is `spent` (16b cut Q.7, §11), a Hold win is the
holding side's with reason `hold` (16b cut O.1), a conceded match is the
other side's win with reason `conceded`, and a match every pilot left is a `timeout` with reason
`unattended` (16b cut D.3, §11). A unit's `hits` counts
the rounds that took something off something, which is the black box's `hit` lines less the rounds
that landed on an already spent section (Q.6).

---

## 5. Verbs and output

Cockpit output is two feeds (16b cut Q.3, §11). `arena.cockpit` is pushed, carried as
`Panel{feed, kind: Keyframe}` with the map frame (terrain glyphs plus the platform's contacts),
the status block (power, speed, heading, armor and internals per section as current over max, ammunition
and ready state per weapon, current lock and its percentage), and the contacts list (since 17b cut M1a.3 at
`FRAME_VERSION` 2: `t_ms`, the standing `order` re-keyed to the hull's own rows, each contact's `aspect`
and `fatal` words and, on an armed hull alone, `reads_dead` and per-weapon `odds`, each weapon's `bands_cm`,
and no `weapons` block on an unarmed hull; since 17b cut M1b.3 at `FRAME_VERSION` 3 each weapon's `armed`
object — `null`, or its slot and terms with the target's kind and never a designator — and the top-level
`odds_floor_permille`; §11); plus `Notice`
events for transitions and refusals. Since 16b cut Y.4 the frame also carries `catchup`, `null` on every
frame but the first a reclaimed pilot reads (§3.8): the report of the time away, built from the
platform's own rows and said once on every surface (§11). Since 17b cut M5.4 the seated pilot's own
frame also carries `mind`, the hull's sealed mind for the person in the seat (`null` on a hull no seal
reached), with `FRAME_VERSION` held at 3 (§11). `arena.map` is the whole arena's terrain and its named key spots
(16b cut V.1) and nothing about any unit: surveyed ground, pulled by a panel client through a query and never pushed, answered
by the asker's standing and never a parameter (since 16b cut I.1 and I.4, in order: a seated pilot's
own match; the match a staff watcher watches; the finished match the asker's `SIM REPLAY` cursor names
when its ground is held; else the unseated pair). Two more feeds since cut I: `arena.replay` (the
pushed keyframe window `SIM REPLAY` prints, and the query answering the cursor's whole replay document
or `available: false` with a sentence) and `arena.observer` (the staff picture, pushed to watchers
alone; the query answers the last picture or `watching: false`). Frames reach the world task as latest-wins slots and go out
through its normal output path (§3.1). The ANSI line renderer prints the frame as text; the TUI and the
web client render the same structure on their screen 0 under one map-window rule; parity is one
canonical fixture set whose line-rendered grid is the byte target for all three renderers (and since
16b cut I.2 a second set under `web/src/lib/fixtures/replay/`, whose `replay_grid.txt` is the byte
target of the TUI's and the web's replay compositors). Nothing in
the frame identifies another unit by object id or name before it is classified; until then it is a
provisional `X<n>` that says nothing of team or slot. A classified contact is named by class, team and
its slot designator. The slot designator is public lobby knowledge, so the lobby board names the pilot
behind it; the owner accepted that, and the board is not blanked (Q.1, §11). Under Hold (16b cut O.1) the
clock crosses to no pilot, and a pilot standing alone on the spot past the declared time with no win can
infer that an undetected contestant stands inside the radius: an inference inherent to the mode, accepted
with elimination's precedent (§11). Since 16b cut AH.1 the five first-time hints (*six since 17b cut M5.6, §11*) ride the same `Sys`
path on the arena's reply category: one fixed sentence naming the next verb at each of five points of a
first match, once per account, never a frame field and never a word about another unit (§11). Since 16b
cut AP.3 a line pilot who typed `WATCH ON` is pushed, after the keyframe, one `Sys` block of one to three
lines (own line, guns with the lock, contacts) from the pilot's own frame whenever the block would read
differently from the last, and since AP.4 a connection's `OUTPUTPREFIX` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX` bracket every
typed line's whole step while every pushed notice lands outside them (§11).

---

## 6. AI

**From 17b cut M4.3** the AI fill and every released hull are flown by Standard Doctrine on a unit
mind (doc 17), sealed on every hull at check-out at `[tacsim.minds].fill_class` (on the native stock
mind since the fill decision of 2026-10-09, §11); the reflex controller below was the fixtures' until
17b cut M4.6c deleted it on 2026-10-10 (§11), and its doctrine is what Standard Doctrine re-expresses
(the native stock mind's divergences are held in §11's M1a.6 and M4.3 entries).

**The reflex controller** (D-16-16) is a deterministic state machine per AI unit in the game crate, run
in the arena's pulse (phase 4) and submitting the same intents a pilot's verbs stage:

`Startup → Patrol (a movement order, else the search, else the hunt and then the arena pack's waypoint
graph, or under Hold the key spot in their place; 16b cuts E.4 and O.1) → Approach (nearest classified hostile by a utility score of range against the
platform's own fighting reach, facet exposure, own health; it closes to `engage_band_permille` of the
lesser of its sight and its loaded weapon's inner band, 420 m on the stock tank, or on the plot's own
cell when it has no line; a machine that cannot close, its engine dead or its hull authored with no
speed, enters Engage anywhere inside that lesser reach, 600 m: 16b cut AA.1) → Engage (stop, turn, lock,
fire when ready out to that lesser reach, and back to Approach past it, 600 m) → Withdraw (below a
health threshold, toward the entry zone; on the way it
turns only on what is inside the close band and while it has `stand_min_permille` of its plate, and once
home, or when it cannot close, it shoots back at anything inside its release reach) → Disabled` (the
ranges: 16b cut E.1, §11; the immobile machine: AA.1, §11). A unit that cannot move and carries no
weapon (the range target, §3.3) is inert to the machine: cold it takes `STARTUP`, online it is parked in
`Patrol` with nothing to search, hunt, path or approach, and a squad whose commander is inert is never
deliberated for (16b cut O.3, §11).
Reactions to `blocked`, `lost`, `no line` and `unreachable` are edges in the machine; `unreachable` (the
bounded A\* over budget or no path) falls back to the nearest waypoint, and when the cell that failed was
a live `Advance` order's own it ends that order for the unit (16b cut E.2); an `Advance` held by a unit
that cannot close ends for that unit on its next decide tick the same way, while a `Hold` holds it as it
holds any machine (AA.1). `lost` is read off the
contact: a target is dead when the platform's own gunner killed it or its held reading shows a fatal
