- **§3.1 "The seam" and §5 (a player who types ahead is served)**: as built (B.5; doc 09's admission contract amended in the same commit): a typed line, a panel query or a reply-carrying world read (`NameFree`, `UnlinkPlayer`, `InInputContext`, `ConnectionReady`, `LegalActions`) that meets another action's commit is no longer refused at once. The world task holds it in a bounded defer queue (four typed lines a connection, 64 entries in all, 250 ms from arrival on the runtime's clock) while it keeps polling its inbound, drains the queue one entry a turn once the receipt lands, before the lifecycle slot and before anything new, and refuses a typed line still waiting at the bound with `The world was busy too long for that line; please retry in a moment.` (`RefusalReason::CommitTimedOut`, its tail the phrase every test rig's retry loop matches), while a timed-out query or read keeps its typed `Busy`; the lifecycle messages (`ConnUp`, `ConnDown`, `SessionGone`, `Apply`) keep the one deferred slot, `Composing` is still dropped, and a mutation is never queued. A barrier that arrives behind queued entries waits behind them, so `barrier_result`'s contract (everything sent before the call has been processed) holds; one that meets a full queue is refused `Busy`, retryable and retried by the gateway, rather than answered ahead of the receipt. `commands_total` counts a line once, at admission or final refusal, and `mush_command_deferred_total` and `mush_command_defer_timed_out_total` show the queue at work (16c Q7; the measurement was cut AA's two sweep flakes, which are its regression fixtures). This retires the D.1 entry's open ruling on `B-13`. In the session layer a retain-latest `Keyframe` lives in a per-feed slot beside the resume ring (the Q.3 to Q.5 entry above, amended in place).
- **§3.1 "The seam", "Durability", "Pulse phases" step 2 and §4 (the seam delivers what it produced)**: as built (16b cut M.1, 2026-09-23): `Task::end` waits, racing the stop signal, for every queued ledger chunk, then for the frames the world could not take, then for `Ended`; a wait the stop signal or a closed egress interrupts returns with no result, since a stopping process owes none (the watch change a wait consumed never fires again). The result's `ledger_digest` is the digest over the bytes that LEFT the ledger in chunks (`Ledger::shipped_digest`), equal to the digest over every byte appended on every end whose step flushed and, on the panic arm, the digest of what did leave (the open chunk never does), which is the only claim the black box can honour; A1 reassembles the stored chunk lines in order, hashes them and asserts the result's digest, and since M.3 re-derives one `classified` and one `facet` draw from the result's seed (16c `B-48`, `T-07`). A full inbox on a live task, and a closed inbox whose `Ended` is still on its way to the world, answer a new `ControlRefusal::Busy`: `SIM ABORT` and `SIM CONCEDE` read `The arena is busy; try that again in a moment.`, `@arena/abort` reads `Match N is busy; try @arena/abort again in a moment.`, the unattended deadline keeps its stamp, and nothing is finished, announced or restored from snapshots; `Gone`, the abort that finishes from snapshots, is only a task that died unreported (`B-49`). The pre-pulse buffers are pure rules: a `Control` is never dropped (one per `(unit, channel)`, the highest epoch standing, a displaced `Released` kept aside and drained first so `drop_pilot` runs before the next `Live`), continuous orders coalesce per `(unit, kind)` to the latest, discrete orders alone are bounded, per unit at `max_pending_per_unit + 1` with the NEWEST refused, so a `LOCK` is never dropped while its `FIRE` survives and one pilot's burst never drops another's, and the drain reads `Intent::seq`; the inbox reserves `3 × units + 2` slots no intent may take, so an `EXIT`'s control always fits (`B-50`, `T-11`). A `Frames` message the full egress refused is held on the task and merged into the next pulse's under the rule §3.1 now states (`B-77`). `intents.max_pending_per_unit` is the discrete orders one seat may give a unit per arena second, counted on the order's source (a machine's own never, a gunner seated on a machine-driven tank still), the `queue_full` word and sentence unchanged (`T-82`; amends the D.2 entry above). `match_::start` replaces a stale `Boarding` record with the fresh one, so roster, digest, start and seed are the custody's by construction (`T-12`). Three claims the tree made are held by tests: the split twin crashes the server while `Running` and asserts the restart's result document and the freed unit; the custody round trip stops the tank and asserts the placement equals the flushed log's last summary; `@domain/retire` is pinned by its refusal (`T-42`, in part). Recorded and not taken: the journalled forced void (the AA.3 entry above); an `Ended` the world's own staging queue drops leaves every stop answering `Busy` until a restart.
- **§3.1 "Rate independence" and "Pulse phases", §3.5, §3.6 and D-16-2 (timestamps)**: as built (M.2): the duration bound is a due event scheduled at match open (`DueKind::MatchDeadline`); the due loop stops after its millisecond, so a kill on the whistle still wins and nothing later is applied or journalled, the timeout carries the bound's own millisecond at every cadence, and phase 5 keeps its check as the backstop for a pulse that ran no substep and journals no `tempo` or `lagged` line after an end (`T-01`); motion, heat and the `blocked` notices still integrate to the substep end past the deadline, which in production is timer slop since the task wakes at the deadline. `resolve_fire`, `resolve_lock`, `resolve_startup` and `raise_heat` take the arena millisecond they resolve at: phase 4 hands them the pulse's end and the reflex tick its own timestamp, and the planner reads readiness at the tick too, so a machine's startup, lock, shot, cycle and impact are stamped and planned at the tick and not the substep boundary (`T-02`). **D-16-2 amended** (16c Q23, `T-03`): a discrete action given by a pilot resolves at the end of the pulse that accepted it, so its arena millisecond is that pulse's end, which in production is a wall-clock instant, arena time being elapsed wall time and a pulse ending at `min(period, next due event)` (since 16b cut U.1 at the lattice point §3.1 phase 5 names: the U.1 entry below); everything the action schedules from there is a due event and rate-independent, and a machine's actions resolve at its tick; a production-shaped task test asserts a pilot's `startup` line equals the accepting pulse's clock and `power_online` lands exactly `startup_ms` later, never a modulus and never a value. A guarded zero-length substep after phase 4 lands a pilot's beam in the pulse that fired it, the opening pulse drains nothing (its `t = 0` events pop in the next pulse's first substep, so AA.3's backstop stands), and the task's timer floors at one millisecond (`B-51`); `substeps` counts what ran (`T-10`). The loss hysteresis ends by its own due event (`DueKind::LossSettles`) at the deadline rather than by a sweep at the pulse's end, settled in the staging scan in `(t_ms, seq)` order (a window shorter than the substep, no shipped config, by the scan that staged it), `held_losses` folded into the state hash under the one-millisecond rule §3.6 states (`T-83`); a mesh share that renames a held row inside the window says the identification with the old name, once, for a mesh insert only (`B-74`; closes the D.4 entry's open pair); the cross-tempo perception comparator compares staged notices, with a moving observer (`T-84`); §3.1's substep-alignment sentence is `T-05`'s (Q24, the lattice not built), asserted for every fielded unit and by the harness. Beyond the timestamp items: the tick-stamped startup moved E.3's routeless approach into its window and showed that a one-cell look-ahead cannot stop a tank needing 56 m, so `hold_routeless` also holds a routeless machine when any cell on its heading within its stopping reach would be refused (16c `B-44`, reopened and re-closed).
- **§3.4 step 4, §4, §5 and the hash (what the hash and the ledger cover)**: as built (M.3): one stop is one held edge, as §3.4 step 4 now says: `held` is the refused cell and its cause, settled in `integrate` alone, and the ledger's `blocked` line, the pilot's `Held:` sentence and the reflex controller's lesson all fire on that one edge and nothing else, so the line count no longer depends on how the pulse is cut and a tank pushed at a river bank is told of one stop once (`T-04`, `B-27`, 16c Q22; closes the Q.6 entry's owed ruling); the line carries its cause, the tick re-stamps a held occupied cell before the avoid list's TTL sweep, and `held` is hashed. Heat and its carry are hashed (`T-06`). Ledger lines carry a list of draws (`draws`), the `hit` line the shot's draw and then the facet draw, the result document the match seed (staff-only, §4), and `DrawLabel::decode` lets a reader re-derive any draw (`T-07`); a struck-through hit names whose armour the round spent (`armor_section`, `T-13`). Phase 4 and a substep's reflex ticks take their shared hash lazily at the first line they journal, so a quiet pulse takes none (`T-23`); the designator book folds a running digest of its mints instead of both maps every substep (`T-27`); `set_unit` recomputes the condition bands only when the plan touched a band and never for a unit the arena does not hold (`T-28`); the k+1 staging writes are said in `hash.rs` and four stale comments are true (`T-09`, `T-80`). The frame field is `held` in Rust with the `blocked` wire key kept for both panels until a client cut renames it. Open, from the plan's review: the keyframe's row `id` and a notice's `contact` are the seed-derived contact id, invertible to the keyed state and every draw, which is why the seed is staff-only until a per-pilot opaque row key lands on the cockpit seam.
- **§3.1 "Rate independence", §3.3, §3.4 step 1, §3.5 and §3.6 (weapons, sight and entry)**: as built (M.4): a tank on no tracks neither turns nor drives: `MotionLimits` carries two closed sides, damage ORs them in, `turn` takes the long way with one closed and does not turn with both, and the five tank packs (`line_tank`, `_laser`, `_thermal`, `_ecm`, `_eccm`) author `disable_motion_when_all_of` on both tracks at version 2, re-signed in place with their pins moved by hand, the biped left at version 1 because its legs already carry the rule (`B-47`, Q32); a live world takes them through the boot reset's refit. The two carry sign bytes `turn_side` and `speed_rate` zero their carry on a reversal or a rate flip (`T-16`). Past the weapon's last band a shot resolves blind at nought (`Resolution::beyond`), the cockpit's `beyond` quotes 0 per cent, and `reresolve` keeps a beyond shot blind with only the band changed, so a target that turns in flight earns nothing back (`B-52`, Q19). `SensorProfile` names its component and the gunnery term reads that cell, so the ECM tank's pod no longer stands in for its sight (`B-53`); `Jammer::read` refuses a pod at condition zero as `Eccm::read` always has, both read at check-out only (`B-54`). `[tacsim.sensors].max_signature_factor` below 1.0 is refused at boot by name, the default is 1.0 in every shipped file, and the perception cull is floored at 1.0 in the library too, so it is exact and never shorter than the range test (`B-55`, `T-26`; a box whose `mushd.toml` is not re-rendered keeps an explicit 1.5, which still validates as a wider cull). `mush_atlas::trace::line_cells` is drawn from the lesser endpoint and `line_height` uses the symmetric numerator `(z_from·(n−i) + z_to·i)/n`, so A sees B exactly when B sees A, held by property tests on a synthetic relief and on the proving ground (`B-56`). `Arena::max_speed_cms` is the scaled ceiling §3.1 now names, `Arena::new` refuses a ceiling the 50 ms floor cannot bound (`SubstepBound`) and an impassable opening cell (`CellImpassable`), `from_pack` refuses an entry zone with no passable cell that has a passable neighbour (`EntryZoneUnusable`), and `opening_pose` requires a way out in both its arms; none of the three new sentences names a unit or a cell, since the lobby reads them to the centre, where the older `CellOccupied` and `PoseOffWindow` still do (`B-57`, `T-14`, `T-15`). `step_refusal` exposes motion's crossing test so `path.rs`'s shoulder rule, that the search refuses diagonals motion allows, is stated and held by a property (`T-17`).
- **§3.1 "Rate independence" and "Pulse phases" step 5, §3.6 "The pass", §1 A2 and §8 (the loop the task runs, and the cohort)**: as built (16b cut U.1, 2026-09-23): `Arena::next_due_ms` answers, in absolute arena time, the earlier of the next period multiple strictly after `now_ms` and the first substep boundary at or after the next due event, never `now_ms` itself, so every period multiple is a wake, a due event wakes the task at the substep boundary it already belongs to, a due event inside a substep is applied in that substep at its own timestamp (16c Q25), and a wake that arrived late is pulled back onto the lattice by the next target (from 253 with a due at 500 it asks for 247); the task's deadline is `max(base + next_due_ms, now + 1 ms)` from the task's own base, so the pulse's cost and the timer's overshoot no longer accumulate into arena time. Arena time stays wall time (Q-U1, the scheduled wall, is the owner's): production wakes are the lattice plus the OS timer's overshoot, and the harness measures that cost with `TACSIM_WAKE_JITTER_MS` (report-only at 3 ms: 81 short substeps in 120 wakes, per-window p99 11.291). This amends the D.1 entry's `at worst an arena second stale`: a `Frames` message is at most one second stale at `Engaged` and `Alert`, and at `Quiet` one second plus the earliest recurring offset rounded up to the lattice, at most `2,000 − substep` (1,750 on the shipped lattice); and a `WeaponReady` wakes the task within one substep of ready. The lattice is the current `Arena::substep_len_ms`, constant on the shipped content because the 250 ms cap binds and able to lengthen mid-match on content whose fastest unit cuts it shorter (a candidate 16c row). First passes and reflex ticks are staggered by `rng::stagger_ms(seed, id, cadence)`, splitmix64 of `seed ^ id` modulo the cadence, not a draw and no label, so contiguous ids no longer put every pass in the first 300 ms of each second; the two tempo signals test membership as `SeenPose::within_cm` with no root, outcomes identical. The harness fields the shipped catalog through `custody_unit` (`COHORT[(n / 2) % 8]`, both sides alike: tracked, legged, laser, thermal, ECM, ECCM) over an integer relief (the arena's fixed-point sine table, so a baseline is host-independent) with two seeded terraces that refuse tracked and legged edges differently, steps at `next_due_ms` for `TACSIM_ARENA_S` (30) arena seconds, folds every wake into 500 ms windows and asserts the 12 ms lens per wake AND per window on the CI cohort (sight 60 to 24, share 30 to 8), with the authored reach report-only beside it until cut J's Q26; `blocked` is split by cause with `too_steep` reported and not asserted (the controller still produces a few), while the relief's refusing edges, the cohort's slope limits and `ReflexCounters.slope_refusals` are asserted; the LLM cost run rides the same field. The per-pulse unit changed, a wake being one substep, and the 16b §9 baseline table says so.
- **§1 A2, §2 W4, §3.6 (ECM), §3.7 and §5 (bounds, the jam memo and the age a frame is sent on)**: as built (U.2): five `[tacsim.harness]` thresholds the harness asserts and never throttles, `max_cell_probes_per_pulse` 32,000, `max_jam_checks_per_pulse` 2,048, `max_rss_growth_kib` 65,536, `max_frame_bytes` 16,384 and `max_output_events_per_pulse` 512 (the plan said 256; the fan-out lane's first measurement was 288 on the acquisition pulse, 24 keyframes plus eleven contact-notice lines per pilot, so the default is sized on the measurement with headroom: Q-U2). One jamming fold per unit per substep: `Arena.jam_fold` memoises `jamming_at`, unhashed on the cache argument, cleared at both index rebuilds, after every `StartupComplete` and `Impact`, the only due events that move a pod's power or wreck inside a substep, and since 16b cut O.2 on every `EMCON` flip, so the memo is exact (a pod that comes online, and one wrecked, between two folds are seen by the second); `jam_checks` counts misses only, a field with no pod pays nothing and a stock match is bit-identical (16c T-29; amends the X.4 and D.4 entries above). A frame is sent when a printed figure would change and not per pulse: `FrameContact::same_as` compares the age through `age_key(age_ms, pass_ms)`, the whole second and the stale clause past `pass_ms`, and on the locked row through `lock_range_step_m`, which moved into `arena::perception` under T-20's one-function rule with the cockpit re-exporting it; the keyframe still carries `age_ms`, nothing on the wire moved, what a pilot is sent changed and what they may read did not (T-30; 16c Q27 read at every resolution a surface derives). Resident memory is read through `memory-stats` on every platform, `VmRSS` beside it on Linux; a long-run lane steps the CI cohort for `TACSIM_LONG_RUN_S` (600) arena seconds asserting the RSS growth and ledger bounds and reporting the due heap; a fan-out lane seats 24 pilots on 24 line tanks through the staff station and assign verbs, starts one match with `@arena/start` and pumps the real egress one message per apply so events per pulse are exact, bounding keyframe bytes and events per pulse and reporting bytes per pilot per arena second, the opening only (the sides 2.43 km apart against a 600 m sight; an engaged pulse at twelve a side is unmeasured).
- **§8 A2, D-16-19 and the D-H-8 entry above (the weekly delta)**: as built (U.3, 2026-09-24): `tacsim_harness_delta_against_the_committed_baseline` compares the fresh CI-cohort report to `crates/tacsim/tests/harness_baseline/arena.<os>.json`, the deterministic block exact on every leaf (`thresholds.assert` and `thresholds.lens` excluded, so a run by hand without the variable does not fail it), the timing block (`p50`, `p99`, the window `p99`) only on a matching host keyed by OS, cores and hostname and failing only past twenty per cent slower, a faster run said and passed, a missing file said and passed, a report older than the test binary refused; `TACSIM_BASELINE_BLESS=1` writes the file with commit, date and host; the Windows file is committed and the Linux one is the owner's to bless. The CI job stays report-only for the absolutes (no `TACSIM_ASSERT`, a shared runner) with the delta as its one failing step and every `harness*.json` uploaded; `scripts/harness-weekly.sh` runs the same two cargo lines by hand with `TACSIM_ASSERT=1`, and a test holds the two equal; `docs/ops.md` says how, and never on a host serving players. The ground-pulse lane's three zero counters, `TACSIM_WEAPONS` and the `[tacsim.harness].weapons` key are deleted (a hand-kept `mushd.toml` carrying it refuses to start; the template no longer renders it). The burst test (`FIRE` then `EXIT`) poses the tanks inside sight and outside the withdraw band, waits for the lock notice and asserts the accepted `Firing cannon.` before `climb out` and no `fired` at or after the release; `tacsim`'s workspace edge set is pinned (closes 16c T-42).
- **§3.6 "World-side exclusion", §3.7, §3.8, §5 and §7 (the world side)**: as built (U.4): `@find` skips what `can_perceive` denies, so a unit an arena holds is omitted for its owner and for God alike with no sentence added, and `@arena/status` names each stranded `deployed` marker's dbref beside the release command; every `match_object` call site in `crates/*/src` is listed in `matcher-call-sites.toml` with one of five guard words (perceive, locality, staff, self, none) by a contract test that scans comments, strings and `#[cfg(test)]` out, the header stating that `locality` and `staff` do not satisfy this exclusion, so the `none`, `locality` and `staff` rows are H6's work list (16c Q34). `mush-propagation`'s pass names a held platform as neither observer, subject, ageing holder, expiring holder nor share issuer or recipient (`deployed::excluded` on every path). `@arena/pose` keeps a slot's opening cell on the match book beside the centre (which every claim, leave and staff start rebuilds), settable in every state but a live match, validated against the centre's arena at set time, offered to `opening_pose` as the world pose at check-out (an override the cell cannot honour falls back to the entry zone with a `warn!`), cleared when the match that used it finishes, by `=off` and by `@arena/reset`, which now says how many it cleared. `GameModule::on_all_loaded` runs once per module after every module's `on_world_loaded`, and tacsim's performs the pod sweep's moves there, before any tick, recording each body so the first tick that finds the pilot attached says `The hatch of … swings open.` and shows the room, the line held for at most `HATCH_HOLD_S` (60 world seconds) and dropped after, because boot output reaches no session while a split-server session survives to read it (16c B-37; a split-gateway test reads the line after a server restart). A cockpit verb resolves `Seat { driver, unit, station }` once and every reply goes to the typist, so a proxy driver's `STATUS` is byte-equal to the NPC's (T-53; the `arena.map` pull still keys on the typist). The 256 KiB map bound is a rule at pack apply: `arena_map_within_bound` encodes the largest frame the handler can send and refuses the arena by name (`MapTooLarge`; the shipped proving ground encodes to 17,646 bytes); `frame_contacts` and `frame_scan_tables` stay `pub` for their integration-test callers (T-57's `pub(crate)` not taken).
- **§3.2, §3.7 and §5 (the named ground)**: as built (16b cut V.1, 2026-09-24): the arena's authored key spots reach every surface a pilot reads the ground on. `arena.map` carries `spots` (`letter`, `name`, arena `cell`, in authored order; still pulled and never pushed, the unseated answer unchanged), and the map window is composed under one rule on all three renderers: the spots inside the window in authored order, then the pilot's `@`, then the contacts in frame order, a later marker overwriting an earlier one (so a contact standing on a spot hides its letter), and named the other way round, the contacts that own their cell first and then the spots that own theirs (`x BA  + RB  ? X3  A The Ford`), so a narrow TUI legend cut by `fit_legend` and a zoomed web canvas keep the hostile before the name of a field; a spot's name goes into the legend beside the grid and never into it, so the three compositions of the committed fixtures still equal `view_grid.txt` byte for byte (the fixtures moved once, with three spots: over the pond, under a contact, off the window). The marker alphabet is 22 letters (`cockpit::SPOT_LETTERS`; `D`, `T` and `W` are terrain glyphs and `X` the provisional prefix), the letter is the spot's authored index (The Ford `A`, The Crossroads `B`, North Barn `C`, East Compound `E`; The Bluff `F` since 16b cut AE.1), and `MAX_KEY_SPOTS` came down from 32 to 22; a spot's name is checked at load (2 to 40 bytes of printable ASCII, no space at either end, unique ASCII-case-folded, never a designator's shape), so the two resolution arms cannot collide; the check encodes nothing and the definition digest did not move. `VIEW` follows its panel and the beyond-window line with `Off the window: C North Barn, 1390 m at 045°; E East Compound, 2080 m at 075°.`, every undrawn spot with its range to the cell centre rounded to 10 m and its absolute bearing. `RANGE <spot>` and `BEARING <spot>` are a second resolution arm behind the designator arm (the whole name or its letter, case-folded, no prefixes): `Range to The Ford (A): 430 m.` and `Bearing to The Ford (A): 087° (rel 357°).` with no stale clause, since ground does not age; a word that is neither a held contact nor a spot keeps the one byte-identical `No such contact.`, `SCAN` and `LOCK` never resolve a spot, and a spot named before the first frame is refused with `The panel has told you nothing yet.` (surveyed ground names no unit, so the frame's absence may be said there). `STATUS` prints `  nearest spot: The Ford (A), 120 m at 045°` after the position line. The observer test pins the map's `spots`, the bystander's two keys, and the pre-detection battery (`RANGE BA` answering exactly what `RANGE ZZ` answers). Nothing runs per pulse: the cockpit frame did not change (`FRAME_VERSION` 1, no wire key), the ground is read once per typed verb, and the harness delta was equal.
- **§3.7, §3.8 and §4 (the result reader and the centre's history)**: as built (V.2): the result document carries each unit's callsign on its unit row and its pilot row (`"callsign"`, read off `Arena::callsigns()`, the arena the world built in `spawn_arena` before it moved into its task, handed back through `open_match` onto `MatchRecord.callsigns`, so the slot rule is applied in one place) and `"ledger": { "log", "digest", "chunks" }` with the count of arena chunks the mirror appended; `record_version` stays 1; a restart abort writes `""` and `0`. The centre keeps a RAM ring of its last `HISTORY_DEPTH` (20) results on the `MatchBook`, ordered by id whatever fills it, loaded from the boot documents before the boot's restart writes and written on every result write from the very value pushed, so RAM and the store cannot disagree; a row evicted from the ring has its report documents deleted in the same write, and the result documents stay. `SIM HISTORY` (the ninth selector, gated by the centre like the rest, no new verb) prints the ring newest first in public words: `  #12  blue won (red eliminated)  4 m 32 s  2 v 2  2026-09-24 14:02 UTC  (you flew RA)`, the suffix only on a row the reader piloted with a non-empty callsign (a document written before V.2 prints none), the outcome words from one closed table beside `end_words` (`draw (both sides out of ammunition)` for `spent`, `timed out (every pilot had left)` for `unattended`, `aborted (the server restarted)` for `restart`, `void (the tape overflowed)` for `ledger_overflow`), `0 v 0` on a restart row whose units are on no roster, and `The centre has no finished match on record.` when the ring is empty; no row names a unit's object number, a pilot, a position or a contact, and the observer test reads every row word by word against that shape.
- **§4, §5, §3.6 and §1 (the debrief)**: as built (V.3): the match ledger has a reader, and it is a projection from its first commit (16c Q37 at its default; the fog does not lift). One pure `match_::report::Fold` per human pilot lives on the `MatchRecord` from check-out, built with the match's own `designate_on_detection` and the tank as checked out, fed every chunk's events in `arena_bridge::on_chunk` as the chunk passes the world (each line parsed once for every reader; a chunk that is not UTF-8 marks every fold's `gap`), never inside `Arena::step`, and finished at check-in into one `tacsim:report:<match>:<unit>` document per pilot (`record: "tacsim.report"`, `record_version` 1: `lines`, `truncated`, `tail`, `gap`, `rounds` per weapon, `kills`) written in the result's action and named on the result's `reports`. A pilot's report keeps only what their own platform held, from the tape's `detected`, `classified` and `lost` lines with `viewer == me` (two bits per held contact: designated names it, classified earns it a section word; a loss and the pilot's own destruction clear them), and resolves a shot's name and section WHEN ITS OUTCOME IS FOLDED, as the in-match notice resolves them at the hit: `  0:42  cannon at BA: hit, turret.`, `cannon at a contact: hit.` for a target not classified when the round landed, `miss: the round went wide.`, `no outcome on the tape.`; what their rounds destroyed (`BA destroyed by your round.`); the hits on their own tank in their own `Struck` vocabulary and never who fired them; their own power, locks and lost sections (worded over the consequences that fired); and their end and the match's end as tail lines never truncated. No range (the tape's `range_cm` is the target's true pose, not what the cockpit showed) and no damage figure (the in-match hit notice carries none), no draw, seed, contact id, object number or hash reaches a pilot's line; the timeline is sorted by stamp and bounded at `REPORT_MAX_LINES` (200) while the match runs. `SIM REPORT [<match>]` (the tenth selector; bare, the newest ring row the reader piloted) refuses in one sentence each, in order: a running match, a match the ring does not remember, a restart (`there is no debrief of it`, before the pilot check), a match the reader did not fly, a document written before reports existed, a read already in flight; then reads the one document through tacsim's first `StoreReadCompletion` (`Your report of match 12 is still being written; ask again in a moment.` when the read outran the commit; the actor, the centre and the staff bit re-validated on completion) and prints the public header (`Match 12 on The Proving Ground, 2026-09-24 14:02 UTC: blue won (red eliminated), 4 m 32 s.`, `Red lost 1 of 2; blue lost 0 of 2.`, `You flew RA: destroyed; 14 shots, 6 hits (42%).` with the bracket since 16b cut O.3, `You fired 14 rounds of cannon and 60 of machine_gun.`) then the timeline in `Sys` blocks of 25 lines joined by newline, so no report can overflow a 256-event connection queue. Staff read the tape itself through `@arena/report [<match>] [<chunk>]` (a switch on `@arena`, the verb's own gate; no live, restart or named gate, since a running or crashed match's tape is the black box an operator most needs): sixteen chunks a request from the chunk named, one line an event but `position_summary` with every field, units as `RA(#101)`, contacts as their sixteen digits, the draws and the seed on the header, and `Next: @arena/report 12 16` on the tail of a whole page (a page cut at 200 lines goes on from the chunk it was cut in). The observer-rule test grew a report column: each pilot's report is checked against the store's tape for every callsign their platform never classified, the shooting test's target reads no shot, the undetected test's pilot reads no callsign but their own, and a bystander teleported into the lobby is told `You did not fly in match 1.`.
- **§3.7, §4, §6 and the hash (`ORDER`)**: as built (16b cut Y.1, 2026-09-24): a seated pilot commands their side's machines from the cockpit with `ORDER <squad or ALL> HOLD, ADVANCE <spot>, ENGAGE [<contact>] or WITHDRAW [HOLD FIRE]`, the thirteenth selector (since 16b cut Z.2 also `SCREEN <bearing>`, `SUPPORT <callsign>` and `RELEASE`: the Z.2 entry below). Authority is 16c Q38 at its default read on the shipped centre: the side's lead orders any squad of the side; anyone else orders the one machine in their pod pair (an odd slot with the next, an even one with the one before), addressed by the word of the squad that holds it and acknowledged by its callsign; a pilot whose pair is a human or empty, or a slotless seat, commands nothing. The refusals, in order: usage; `DESTROYED`; one byte-identical `No such squad.` for the other side's, a nonexistent and an unauthorised squad alike, refused before any contact is looked at so that no order teaches the other side's squad count; `You have no squad to order.` for an empty `ALL`; the one `No such contact.` for an `ENGAGE` word no held row answers to or an `ADVANCE` word that is no spot (a hint on a row the panel classes as the pilot's own side is dropped to a bare engage, since the machines never fire on their own side); then the seat's refusal, the seat authorised once on the issuer's own tank and `helm` channel (`arena_bridge::authorise_seat`, factored out of `forward_intent` byte for byte) and the order offered per unit with that unit's own epoch (`forward_order`), a unit a human has since boarded refusing `NotReflex` silently as before. `Order` carries `issuer` (the scope a target hint is written in; `None` is the squad commander, the LLM seam's) and `spot` (the commander's word for an `ADVANCE` cell, for the record and the debrief only); `ordered_target` resolves the hint on the issuer's contacts and `best_hostile` still chooses among the receiver's own, so a commander may point and a tank that cannot see still cannot see; the issuer's `u32` is folded into `state_hash` after the `hold_fire` byte (the cut's one hash move, `spot` not folded; since Z.2 a screen's facing and a support's charge fold after the issuer), and `apply_intent` journals one `ordered` line per unit at the pulse's timestamp under phase 2's one lazy hash, asked before the order is written (§4). A human order stands for `MAX_TTL_MS`, ten minutes, or until the pilot's next (or their `RELEASE`, Z.2); the acknowledgement goes to the issuer alone in the doctrine's own words (`Ordered red-1 to withdraw: it breaks for its entry zone and still fights inside its bands. It stands for 10 minutes or until your next order to it.`, `, holding fire` before the colon, `(A)` after a spot, no radio line), the debrief prints the issuer's own orders once each, `@arena/status` prints `orders: N by pilots, M by lance leaders` after each match row (`by the machines` until 17b cut L.2, which adds the `leaders:` row: the L entries below), and a live human order shields its squad from the LLM seam in both halves, no signal spent and a reply dropped until the watch expires (§6). 16c Q43 at its default: a non-lead's order to their wingman and the lead's to the squad holding it are latest-wins per unit, nobody told.
- **§2 W8 and §7 (the span and the rate)**: as built (Y.2): `[tacsim.command].max_span = 4` (1 to 32, the ceiling `MAX_SQUADS_PER_SIDE`) and `orders_per_minute = 12` (1 to 600), validated by name at boot in the Q.7 shape (`[tacsim.command].max_span must be 1 to 32 (it is 0)`) whether or not the layer is on, in `mushd.toml.example` and the instance template the deploy renders and preflights with both halves (a block hand-added to a live toml ahead of a deploy, or left in for a hand rollback to an older binary, is refused under `deny_unknown_fields`). Both are enforced at the world in `cockpit::order`, after the authority and the objective and before the fan-out, on the registry's own RAM, so a refusal reaches no pulse and can move no outcome. The span is concurrency: the squads whose world watch names the pilot's tank and has not run out, a replacement never counted, the watch holding the full ten minutes because the world never learns an early arena end, unless the issuer's own `RELEASE` drops it (16c Q44, decided at 16b cut Z.2: the Z.2 entry below); refused `You hold orders on N squad(s) already; wait for one to run out, or RELEASE one.` whenever the other squads alone fill the span and `ORDER ALL would put M squads under you; your span is 4. Order them one at a time.` only when one at a time would pass. The rate is a per-issuer ring of world stamps, one per `ORDER` (`ALL` is one), refused `You have given N orders in the last minute; wait.` at the ceiling and cleared with the match. The harness prints `harness_arena_command` on both reaches and report-only in the cost lane, counts `orders` as distinct issuer-and-timestamp pairs rather than unit lines, asserts both bounds under `TACSIM_ASSERT` on the CI cohort (zero there: no human and the LLM off) and keeps the `command` object outside the deterministic block; `Arena::live_orders_by_issuer` is the one arena addition, a pure read that nothing in the arena calls.
- **§3.7 `BOARD` and `EXIT`, §3.8 and doc 07 §1.4 (the reclaim)**: as built (Y.3): the N.1 and F.1 entries above are amended: a seat dropped past the linkdead grace, or by a `QUIT`, is no longer lost for the match. The slot keeps `left` (out of the cockpit by any route) and gains `dropped_at_ms`, written by the disconnect hook's seated arm before the `Released` goes out and cleared by `EXIT`, so the board reads `flown by doctrine` (*`flown by its mind` since 17b cut M5.3, §11*) for a drop and `left` for a deliberate leave, which stays one-way before or after a drop; the other side's reduced row shows neither word. A reconnecting dropped pilot is told `<tank> is flying on doctrine; BOARD takes it back.` (*`<tank> is flying on its mind; BOARD takes it back.` since 17b cut M5.3, §11*); `BOARD` during `Running` answers `<tank> is still being handed over; try again in N second(s).` inside `[tacsim.match].reclaim_min_ms` (5,000; 0 to 600,000, validated by name), `You left <tank> for the rest of the match; it cannot be taken back.` after an `EXIT`, the stopping sentence while the record is `Stopping`, the already-aboard sentence for a seated slot, and otherwise re-runs F.1's seat path idempotently from the pilot's own pod or their own team room: `declare_station`, the interior placement only when the body is outside, the activation re-taking the billet the drop left on the tank, the links, `bind_pilot`, then `Live` on every channel at a new epoch, then the slot back with the unattended clock cleared and the leads resettled (16c Q42 at its default: the heir keeps the lead, and a side whose every claimant had left gives it to the first back), ending `You climb back into <pod>: <tank> is yours again where it stands.` and a room line only when the body was moved. The arena re-adds the pilot to its frame roster on a `Live` naming one (`Arena::seat_pilot`, before the controller changes, so the next `frames()` sends a whole keyframe), and a control whose epoch is below the channel's current purges below it and applies no seat state (equal epochs still apply: K4's revival), so a `Released` racing the reclaim is settled by the epoch table and not by arrival. Proved at three cadences, in the `Rig`, and over the real front door.
- **§5, §3.8 and doc 07 §1.3 (the catch-up report)**: as built (Y.4): a pilot who takes their tank back reads once what happened while they could not see. The arena snapshots the platform on the first `Linkdead` or `Released` that names a pilot (its cell, power, the frame's own contact rows and designators, each section's condition word with the facets' armour summed and its internals, each weapon's rounds), so the grace is counted from the last moment the pilot could see; a `Live` that finds the pilot still on the roster (a revival inside the grace) spends the snapshot and builds nothing; the `Live` that re-seats them reads the machine's state, goal and order before its row is released and builds the report against the snapshot: the time away, cells then and now (now is the re-seat's, read in phase 1 before that pulse's motion, which is what makes it one set of figures at every cadence; the carrying frame's own cell may lead it), contacts new and lost by contact id from `frame_scope`'s own rows sorted by designator and capped at 12, sections whose word changed or whose armour or internals fell capped at 6, rounds spent per weapon read off the ammo record capped at 6, power then and now, the wreck flag. Both tables are views beside `last_frames` and fold into no hash. The report rides exactly one frame as the additive key `catchup` (`null` on every other frame, `FRAME_VERSION` still 1, `mush-proto` unchanged), `same_as` never calls such a frame unchanged, and `merge_held` carries a held pulse's report onto the next frame as it carries notices. Line mode says it as one `Sys` block tagged `arena-notice` before the frame's notice lines (`Back aboard RA after 2 m 14 s away.` and the indented machine, position, contacts, sections, rounds and power lines, an eighth `Your tank is a wreck.`, the duration from the wire's own `away_s`) and the mirror stores it cleared, so `STATUS` and `VIEW` never re-say it; the TUI and the web compose the same lines from the object's fields onto their notices rings, every string cleaned and capped, and one hand-written nine-case fixture (`web/src/lib/fixtures/cockpit/catchup_lines.json`) pins all three renderers. Nothing in it names a unit, a pilot, an object number or another unit's position: the contacts are the platform's own designators, held or once held (the observer test's catch-up column). Doc 07 §1.3's heat changes and actions taken are not reported, and `machine.goal` rides the wire unsaid.
- **§5 and §3.7 (the TUI at the 120-column floor)**: as built (16b cut AB.1, 2026-09-25; the owner's ruling, 16c `Q-AB1`): the B.3 entry above is history for the layout. The TUI is laid out for a floor of 120 columns and nothing is drawn narrower: `FLOOR_COLS` 120 in `screen.rs`, a width alone (no row floor, 16c `Q-AB2` at its default), and below it `draw` paints one dim sentence wrapped at the terminal's width (`The TUI needs at least 120 columns and this terminal is 80 wide: make it larger, or press Ctrl-] or type @notui for line mode.`, the terminal's own width named, 80 for a client that reported none), the input box, and whatever owns the keyboard (the palette, the help overlay, a pending ask), touching no state, so the resize repaint the gateway already fires brings the screen back as it was; below the floor a letter typed in nav mode opens the box with that letter instead of firing a hotkey, so `@notui` typed straight in never walks a one-letter exit or runs `scan` (an Alt+letter is not guarded). The WHO rail's 100-column rule is gone (the rail always draws); the chrome still drops under 12 rows. The cockpit re-derived: the map's 36 columns unchanged, the Left column 34 (status `min_h` 7 over damage `min_h` 12; no status row wraps at 32 inside, the speed row at three-digit kph with `DESTROYED` being 32), the Right column 46 (weapons, contacts, and the notices pane MOVED there under contacts, 16c `Q-AB3`; a contact row at the widest shipped words never wraps at 44 inside; the SCAN row under a classified contact is a second line by design and wraps once below 126 columns), the map taking the `Min(1)` remainder while the Right column takes half the surplus (Left 34, map 38, Right 48 at 120; 34, 58, 68 at 160); the damage pane packs facet cells (`front 400/400`) whole into rows of its width, two-space indented, so a facet's name is never split from its numbers, the shipped line tank taking 12 rows, and with no pane size (the line client's paths) one row per section as before; the cockpit is whole for the shipped tank from 120x30 (`COCKPIT_ROWS`, a documented derivation `draw` never reads: seven chrome rows with the gauge and the Left column's 9 + 14), the notices pane has seven inner rows at 120x34 and twelve at 160x48, and below 30 rows `damage` goes first as B.3 built it; the legend keeps whole entries in order with `? help` always last when it would overflow (the cockpit's at 120 ends `b bearing · ? help`, whole from 148 columns); the tab strip's rule and the notices ring are unchanged. The goldens are the floor's (120x34, 160x48, the 80x24 floor sentence; every below-floor golden deleted), the split-gateway and observer witnesses open 120x34 ptys, and the help says the floor, that 160 to 180 is the width the TUI is happiest at, and that line mode has no hard floor though its maps and tables are drawn for 80 columns. Recorded and open (16c): the biped's 19 damage rows show 12 at 120x34 and 18 at 160x48; the SCAN row's one wrap between 120 and 125 columns; the cockpit strip without its who block between 120 and 127; the catch-up report cut from the top at 120x34 (six of its rows shown); the contacts pane's five inner rows at 120x30; the line client's `DAMAGE_COLUMNS` 76, `VIEW`'s 78-column window and `SIM HISTORY` still assume eighty.
- **§3.2 "Line of sight", §3.6 step 2, §1 A2 and §2 W4 (the arena's trace)**: as built (16b cut J.1, 2026-09-25): the arena's line of sight is `mush_atlas::sight_only`, one walk from the canonical end (the lesser endpoint in tuple order, the direction `line_cells` already draws every line in) with the walk's own per-cell rule written once as `terrain_sight_rule` and called by `walk` too, the straight M.4 height as `line_height_cm`, a lazy `Bresenham` iterator, no allocation and a return at the first blocker; its `Sight` is byte-identical to `trace_cells` from the canonical ends for every pair and grid (`Edge` for an off-grid endpoint as the walk answers it), so it agrees with the directed walk on everything `sees()` reads, and `Arena::sight` is the one door for detection's step 2 and the mesh line, counting `traces` and `cell_probes`. `cell_probes` changed unit: the interior cells read up to and including the blocker, never an endpoint (the walk's `visited` counts every cell with both endpoints). The early stop is the atlas O lens's one exception, taken because the pass's cost already depends on the hidden field through the index and thermal culls and no arena reader can time one line; the world's walk keeps the lens and a contract scan pins `sight_only(` to the atlas and the arena's `horizon.rs`. The fire path (`damage.rs`) stays on `trace_cells` for `target_cover` and its readers. Proved by every ordered pair of a 16 by 16 relief at two eye heights, 20,000 pairs on the M.4 relief, 256,000 seeded pairs on the shipped proving ground and the ignored `tacsim_harness_sight_differential` lane over about 68 million pairs (`TACSIM_SIGHT_ORIGINS`, 2,000 by default; asserting always); every sight outcome, ledger byte, hash and cross-tempo fixture unchanged, the CI baseline re-blessed for the three `cell_probes` fields alone after the delta named only them.
- **§3.2 "Line of sight" and "The spatial index", §3.6 step 2, §2 W4 and the hash (the horizon index)**: as built (J.2): `crates/tacsim/src/arena/horizon.rs`, a two-level pyramid over the terrain at 16- and 4-cell blocks (`LEVELS = [4, 2]`) holding each block's highest and lowest cell and whether any cell is opaque or partial (`Block { max_elev_cm, min_elev_cm, flags }`, six bytes; 26,112 bytes on the shipped 256 by 256 window, reported as `horizon_bytes` under `T-36`'s memory bound). A query materialises the canonical line into a stack buffer of `MAX_LINE_CELLS` (512), splits it into runs per block coarse to fine, proves a run clear without reading it (no flag and no cell above the run's lowest line height, `line_height_cm` being monotone), proves it blocked by reading its first interior cell through `terrain_sight_rule` on the carried attenuation, descends, or walks at the finest level; an off-grid endpoint, a line of 512 or more cells or a terrain without levels goes to `sight_only` whole. Every answer is `sight_only`'s to the byte and the cells read never exceed its `visited`. The index is a private field of `ArenaTerrain` built by its one constructor (so a swapped definition or terrain carries its own and `Arena` gains no field), queried by `ArenaTerrain::sight`, with `without_horizon()` for the replays; it is a cache of terrain on `jam_fold`'s argument, folded into no hash, and not this section's cross-observer cache, since nothing observed is cached. `PerceptionCounters::horizon_probes` (one per block read, whichever rule settled the run; unbounded) joins `traces` and `cell_probes` on the harness lines, the JSON and the deterministic block. Proved by the fold property, the worked pins (the wall line at 2 cells and 5 blocks, the M.4 tie at 2 and 6 both ways and 4 and 8 at 199 cm), 40,000 seeded pairs, the proving-ground third column on 256,000 pairs (cheaper on 236,516), and the designator script and a shipped-ground fight with fire replayed with and without the index to equal ledgers, hashes and finals; the CI baseline re-blessed once more for three moved and three new probe fields.
- **§1 A2, §8 A2 and D-16-19 (the authored reach's number)**: as built (J.3, 2026-09-25): the CI cohort's warm-up wreck assertion applies at the default sight knobs only (`reach == Reach::Ci && sensor_cells() == SENSOR_CELLS && share_cells() == SHARE_CELLS`, byte-for-byte U.1's at 24/8), so the CI lane at other knobs is a sizing run of another field that prints `killed_warming` and asserts nothing about warm-up. The authored reach (60 sensor and 30 share cells) measured three times by the weekly script after J.1 and J.2: per wake p99 7.699 to 8.156 ms (from 9.399 at U) and per window p99 14.108 to 15.092 ms (from 17.123), against the 12 ms lens and the 10.3 ms (12.0 / 1.16) a weekly assertion needs to hold through the harness's recorded 16 per cent run-to-run spread; per-wake peaks 2,994 traces, 19,858 cell probes (from 89,197), 17,785 horizon probes, 1,124 jam checks and 452 share operations (against 256, the one per-wake bound still exceeded). The window is over, so the lane stays report-only (`asserting` still requires `Reach::Ci`) — until J.3b, the next bullet —, no bound moves, no authored baseline is blessed, no config site changes, and 16c Q26 is put to the owner with a figure beside each arm from five report-only sizing runs of the CI lane (60/8 11.354 ms per window, 48/30 14.454, 36/30 11.234, 240 units 9.911, 200 units 9.478); the candidate cull's `(2 × span + 1)²` slot window (15,129 at 60 cells) is recorded as a cost no trace change touches (16c `T-87`). Nothing is gated in CI: the job is billing-blocked and has no Linux baseline, so an asserted authored lane would be asserted and delta-compared by hand on the blessing host; the Linux figure is not measured in cut J. N5 landed through step 2; J.4, the work budget, is built only on the owner's word.
- **§1 A2, §8 A2, §2 W6 and D-16-19 (the authored lane asserted)**: as built (J.3b, 2026-09-25): the owner answered 16c Q26 on the bounds arm ("keep the authored reach and the 300 units, raise the bounds and set the lens with margin"), and the authored-reach lane asserts under `TACSIM_ASSERT` for the first time. What moved: `[tacsim.harness].max_share_ops_per_pulse` 256 to 768 at exactly the five default sites (tacsim `HarnessConfig::default`, mush-config `TacsimHarnessCfg::default`, the mush-config key table, `mushd.toml.example`, `deploy/instance/mushd.toml.tmpl`; three halves of the authored per-wake peak of 452 is 678, rounded up to the next multiple of 256, J.3's rule for a moved bound; mush-config's defaults test moved with it) and no other bound; in `harness.rs` `ARENA_S_P99_MS` 12.0 kept as the per-wake lens for both cohorts and the CI cohort's per-window lens, the new `ARENA_AUTHORED_WINDOW_P99_MS` 17.5 (15.092 × 1.16 = 17.507: the worst of J.3's three runs carrying the harness's recorded 16 per cent spread, given to the owner as 17.5 and taken as 17.5) and `const fn window_lens_ms(reach)` choosing between them; `asserting = knobs.assert` (the `&& reach == Reach::Ci` clause gone), so under `TACSIM_ASSERT` the authored cohort is held per wake at 12 ms and per window at `window_lens_ms(reach)`, with every per-pulse bound, `engaged_p99_ms`, the ledger bytes, the substep rule and the command bounds asserted on it too; `thresholds.window_p99_ms` on the `harness_arena_thresholds` line and in the JSON right after `cut_s_p99_ms` (12 on the CI cohort, 17.5 on the authored), compared by the delta since `RUN_ONLY_THRESHOLDS` is unchanged; the on-push test `the_lens_is_the_cohorts_own` bracketing the authored lens between 1.15 and 1.16 times the figure it was set on, and the delta test gaining the 17.5-against-12.0 case; the committed `arena.windows.json` re-blessed once at `eb1702aa` for exactly the two threshold fields, its 104 deterministic fields equal and `window.ms_p99` 9.735; `docs/ops.md` "What asserts where" and the weekly script's head comment naming both lenses. What did not move: no arena source, no reach (60 sensor and 30 share cells as the catalog fields them), no 300-unit target, no authored baseline and no delta on the authored report (deferred: it would double the baseline files for counts that differ from the CI cohort's only by reach), no CI change (`ci.yml` untouched; nothing is gated in CI, the job billing-blocked with no `TACSIM_ASSERT` and no Linux baseline, so the assertion is by hand on the blessing host through `scripts/harness-weekly.sh`), the CI cohort loosened by nothing (12 ms per wake and per window as before; its share-operations peak of 59 sits in the deterministic block the delta compares exactly), the warm-up wreck gate in `open_field` unchanged. The first three asserted runs: the authored cohort per wake p99 8.099, 7.785 and 8.150 ms against 12 and per window p99 15.339, 14.057 and 14.732 ms against 17.5, its per-wake peak 452 share operations against 768, every bound held; the CI cohort 5.537, 5.397 and 5.280 per wake and 10.398, 9.735 and 9.572 per window; the gate's fourth run 8.377 and 16.298, the lens held by 7.4 per cent. The lens carries its 16 per cent margin over 15.092, the figure it was set on, and less over the runs since (10.7 per cent over J.3's fourth run, 14.1 and 7.4 over J.3b's first and the gate's), so a loaded week can miss on noise and only the owner moves it (16b §9, row J). J.4, the work budget, is not built and no longer pending: Q26 is answered. Linux not measured. Amended by J.3c (`036633d6`, later on 2026-09-25): the owner raised the lens to 20 ms ("bump the lens up to 20 to give us some headroom but keep a reasonable boundary") after J.3b's own gate read 16.298 ms per window, which 17.5 held by only 7.4 per cent; `ARENA_AUTHORED_WINDOW_P99_MS` is 20.0, `AUTHORED_WINDOW_P99_WORST_RECORDED_MS` is the worst asserted run on record (16.298) rather than the figure the lens was set on, and `the_lens_is_the_cohorts_own` now pins the lens at the owner's 20, at or above 1.16 times that worst run (18.906) and under the 25 ms `engaged_p99_ms` ceiling read from `HarnessConfig::default()` (the check that keeps it a boundary, as the owner asked), with the CI lens pinned at 12 literally and the delta test's example case moved to 20.0. Twenty is also the figure the 300-unit harness gate was first proposed at on 2026-09-16, before doc 16 set the finished arena's ceiling at 25. Nothing else moved: no bound, no baseline (the CI report's `window_p99_ms` stays 12.0), no reach, no target; the confirming weekly run after the change read 15.732 ms per window on the authored lane, asserted, the delta clean. Amended by J.3d (`75309700`, 2026-09-29): the owner retired the guard's 16 per cent ratio check; the test now holds only that the 20 ms lens is above the worst asserted run on record (18.022 since cut AF) and under the 25 ms ceiling, and headroom is read at a review rather than asserted.
- **§1 A2, §8 A2 and D-16-19 (the lenses follow the production host)**: as built (16c `T-109`, the owner, 2026-10-09: *scale the wake and window limits to the VPS, since that's where the game will run*, the figures confirmed the same day): every lens was a measurement on the owner's desktop (ALIEN18), and the conclav.io VPS that serves the game (4 vCPU AMD EPYC 7543P) is 1.75 to 2 times slower a core, so the harness's lenses moved to its figures — per wake 12 → 20 ms on both cohorts, the CI window 12 → 22 ms (split from the per-wake lens), the authored window 20 → 32 ms, and §1 A2's `engaged_p99_ms` ceiling 25 → 40 ms so the authored window stays under it as Q26's answer requires; each window figure is the harness's 16 per cent spread over the VPS's worst ordinary run with `T-87` paid (the spatial index's unit-table walk, the same spike). The committed `arena.linux.json` is blessed on the VPS under its own host name, so the delta's timing gate is live on the production host; the fill decision's native `minds.fill_authored` block (its seven leaves; the fill decision's entry below) was carried onto that bless when `perf/vps-300` was merged after 17b cut M4.6, with no re-bless (`docs/ops.md`, *Blessing*). The 300-unit target, the reaches and every per-pulse bound are unchanged.
- **§3.1 "The seam", §3.6, §6 and §7 (lances)**: as built (16b cut Z.1, 2026-09-25; 16c N6 at its defaults): a side's reflex machines are dealt into squads of at most `[tacsim.arena].squad_size` (4; 1 to 16, refused by name in the Q.7 shape): `ceil(n / size)` squads in unit-id order whose sizes differ by at most one (five machines are a three and a two, four are one squad; past thirty-two squads the rest fight on doctrine), N.2's pairs and their four-unit floor gone, the record's and the arena's groupings still one function of one data. The commander's brief carries every member's own picture (`SquadSignal.members`, `BriefMember`: ordinal, state word, health per mille, band and bearing from the commander, read off each member's own pose, the squad's own knowledge and never a contact; the object id rides the seam and never the prompt), filled only for a squad being reported and printed after the `units:` line, so the LLM may name a member to support by ordinal. A 12 v 12 lane, `tacsim_harness_lance` (ignored, in the weekly sweep, with a small un-ignored smoke): twenty-four units of the fielded catalog on the shipped proving ground at the authored reach, fifty-four cells apart astride the crossroads, a scripted commander a side that is its own lowest unit opening every squad on `Engage` and stepping one squad a minute through the other arms, W8's bounds asserted under `TACSIM_ASSERT` and, at the lane's own defaults, equal to the squads a side; its timing report-only beside 12 ms per wake and 20 ms per window (0.609 and 1.119 ms in the cut's sweep), its JSON outside the delta. One replay of a five-machine side at 5,000 / 1,000 / 500 ms pins events, hashes, finals, the roster and the opening pulse's signals; later signal lists are cadence-dependent by design, the signals being taken once per pulse.
- **§3.7, §4, §6 and the hash (`SCREEN`, `SUPPORT` and `RELEASE`)**: as built (Z.2; 16c Q44 decided: built): two squad roles the reflex machine obeys from what it holds, `Objective::Screen { facing_mdeg }` (code 5) and `Objective::Support { of }` (code 6), their payloads folded into `state_hash` after the issuer (the cut's one hash move). A screen holds where it stands, faces the bearing, and locks and fires on a classified hostile whose plot lies inside `SCREEN_HALF_ARC_MDEG` (60,000: a 120° arc) of it and inside `release_cm`, never giving chase (the machine stays in `Patrol`; under `hold_fire` it locks and fires nothing). A support reads its charge's position off its own contact on the charge and its charge's designation off the charge's own reflex row or lock, never another unit's knowledge: it closes to `SUPPORT_STATION_CELLS` (5) of that plot and holds there, drops any chase past `SUPPORT_LEASH_CELLS` (15) and rejoins, prefers the object its charge is fighting, holds and fires from a full-arc stand when it cannot see its charge, and ends the order through `B-41`'s route with one `order_expired` line when the charge leaves or reads dead; a unit whose charge is itself fights on doctrine. From the cockpit `ORDER <squad or ALL> SCREEN <bearing> [HOLD FIRE]`, `SUPPORT <callsign> [HOLD FIRE]` (a `Designator::Slot` callsign of the pilot's own side, resolved after the authority and the squad, the pilot's own tank legal; one byte-identical `No tank of your side flies that callsign.` for the other side's, a provisional, an empty and a nonsense callsign) and `RELEASE` (no tail): `IntentKind::ReleaseOrder { squad, issuer }` under `Order`'s buffer key so the pilot's last word in a pulse applies, applied beside `Order` before the continuous gates, dropping a unit to doctrine only when its order was written for that squad through the one `drop_to_doctrine` the expiry block shares (a released `Withdraw` reads `Patrol` at once when health allows), journalling `order_released` under phase 2's one hash and never `order_expired`, and marking the squad for its next signal only once no living unit of it still holds the order. At the world a `RELEASE` spends a rate stamp and no span slot and drops the squad's watch only when its issuer is the releasing tank, so the span is freed at once and no expiry sentence is spoken, while a wingman's release leaves the lead's watch alone; `SpanFull` reads `You hold orders on N squad(s) already; wait for one to run out, or RELEASE one.`. The acknowledgements, verbatim: `Ordered red-1 to screen 090°: it holds where it stands, faces 090° and fires on what comes inside its arc without giving chase.` and `Ordered red-1 to support RA: it keeps within 150 m of RA and fires first on what RA is fighting.` (*`… and fights what it finds there.` since 17b cut M5.6, §11*) (each with the ten-minute tail, `, holding fire` before the colon), and `Released red-1 to doctrine: it fights on its own reflexes until it is ordered again.` (*`Released red-1 to its mind: it fights on its own until it is ordered again.` since 17b cut M5.3, §11*) (no tail; true on an LLM-on server, where the machine's own commander may order it again at the next signal). The LLM's tool takes `screen_bearing_deg` and `support_member` (a member ordinal from the brief); the debrief prints `released red-1 to doctrine.` (*`released red-1 to their minds.` since 17b cut M5.6, §11*) once per squad for the issuer; the `ordered` tape line carries the arm's word and neither the bearing nor the charge.
- **§3.3, §3.7 "Pods", §3.8 and §4 (six a side)**: as built (Z.3; 16c N7 and Q39 at their defaults): `PODS_PER_SIDE` 6 and twelve `POD_ENTRIES` (red 1 to 6, then blue 1 to 6); `neighborhood.ooc_area` 7 (four more pod items, the team rooms reading six), `neighborhood.staff_lobby` 5 (requiring OOC 7 for a fresh boot; on a live world a no-op plan whose latch stays at 4 with one harmless info line a boot) and `tacsim.pack.proving_ground` 3 (`red_5` at (6, 101), `red_6` at (4, 94), `blue_5` at (249, 101), `blue_6` at (251, 94), inside the ford's rows, checked by the pack validator), the three digests re-pinned. The game's two boot packs are on the `@atlas/pack` shelf beside the starter packs whatever `[tacsim].enabled` says (the boot pass unchanged: a first boot applies, a latched world logs), so a standing world takes them by hand, OOC first then the arena, with the centre idle: `ArenaApplier` refuses `a match is running; apply with the centre idle` before any write while a roster tank is deployed, adopts each slot from the roster document before the name (a renamed stock tank yields no thirteenth) and owns a new tank as the roster's are. The lobby's cap follows what exists: `slots_per_side` is the least of the configured count, `PODS_PER_SIDE` and `centre::fieldable_per_side` (the longest prefix of pods and tanks standing on both sides, never below one), so an un-migrated world stays four a side and no slot can be claimed that `SIM START` cannot field; `centre_readiness` counts pods and tanks against twelve and boot warns `simulator centre partly ready: N of 12 pods, M of 12 tanks; the lobby fields the smaller count a side; take the packs by hand (deploy/README.md)`; `SimError::NoUnit` names the `@atlas/pack` route. `SIM AI` takes 1 to 6, pod 5 pairs with 6, and one pilot with `SIM AI 6` fields five machines in two squads, so `ORDER ALL` at span 1 answers `ORDER ALL would put 2 squads under you`. Nothing yet re-makes a stock tank lost on a world already at version 3 (a 16c residual).
- **§3.7, §3.8 and §5 (the side nets)**: as built (Z.4; 16c N8 at its defaults: not jammed by ECM, the callsign as alias, no cross-side channel): two persistent closed comsys channels, `RedNet` and `BlueNet`, made once at tacsim's boot through the new module API `mush_comsys::api::ensure_channel` (join, see and admin locks shut, speak open, the new `closed` flag on, the policy re-asserted every boot with colour, history and members kept), with `api::set_title` and `api::members_of` beside it. A closed channel is members only: invisible to a non-member in `clist`, `@channel/who` and `addcom` (`No such channel.`; staff excepted through the admin bypass) and delivered to its members whatever its see lock says (`Channel::hears`), so the other side never reads who is on it, who climbed out or who dropped, the fact D.1 redacted from `SIM STATUS`. The lobby joins every human pilot of the centre's slots in `run`'s success arm at the `Boarding → Running` trigger (the last `BOARD` or the board deadline, in the same batch ahead of `Good hunting`), under the callsign lower-cased as alias and the callsign as comtitle after every member not on the side's roster is swept (`You have been joined to channel RedNet (alias: ra).`); `hand_seat_back` (`EXIT`, the sweep, check-in, the boot pass) leaves (`You have been removed from channel RedNet.`, before `EXIT`'s own line); a drop past the grace stays on through the reclaim; machines, staff `@arena/start` matches and the harness join nobody, and nothing is ever posted to a net automatically. The line is comsys's own, `[RedNet] RA Ada: contact east`, on both clients' existing channel screens (the cockpit's notices ring carries `Sys` alone), so no client changed; `RANGE` and `BEARING` are typed past their first two letters while the alias stands.
- **§4, §5, §3.7 and §1 (the replay: the fold and line mode)**: as built (16b cut I.1, 2026-09-26; 16c N9 and Q37 at their defaults: a finished OOC match's replay is objective and public, `ledger.summary_ms` stays 5,000): every finished match the centre remembers has a replay, and it is a picture at cell resolution and not a second tape. One pure `match_::replay::ReplayFold` per match lives on the `MatchRecord` beside V.3's debrief folds, seeded at check-out from the opening poses captured before the custody crosses the seam, fed every chunk's parsed events by `on_chunk` (`position_summary` keyframes as positional rows of `[x, y, heading_deg]` in roster order with `null` for a unit not in the keyframe, `fired`/`hit`/`miss` as shots with the firing time and the outcome word, `unit_destroyed` as wrecks, nothing per-viewer ever read), and finished by `finish`, which writes one `tacsim:replay:<match>` document through `store::push_op` in the result's own action before the result (`replay: true`; a restart abort writes `false`), takes the registry's `Arc<ArenaDefinition>` before `untrack` and keeps it on the book keyed by arena digest so twenty lobby matches hold one ground, and holds the replay on the ring in RAM; the ring's eviction deletes the document with the reports, and a boot reads back only what the ring remembers, building no ground (a ground missing after a boot is built once per digest inside the verb and kept only on a digest match). The budget is bytes, not shape: `REPLAY_MAX_BYTES = MAP_MAX_BYTES` (256 KiB, `T-57`'s bound on a pulled keyframe) behind `REPLAY_MAX_KEYFRAMES` 736 and `REPLAY_MAX_SHOTS` 1,024, a cut being a seal (the first refused keyframe drops everything later, so `next` never steps across a hole) named in the document's `cut` list with `truncated` exactly its non-emptiness; pinned: a twelve-tank lobby hour folds whole at 154,173 bytes and a twenty-four-unit staff hour is sealed at keyframe 677 (56 m 20 s). `SIM REPLAY <match> [<m:ss> or next or prev or first or last] [<callsign>]` is the eleventh `SIM` selector and no new verb, public in the centre with no pilot gate: it pushes one keyframe's window on the new `arena.replay` feed under `VIEW`'s 39 by 20 rule at two printed columns a cell with `retain: latest` (spots first, then the roster's units in roster order as `o` red, `*` blue, `%` wreck at or before the keyframe's horizon, `you: null`), prints the header naming both sides (`Replay of match 12 at 1:30, keyframe 19 of 55 (4 m 32 s, red won (blue eliminated)): both sides as the arena recorded them every 5 s, around RA.`), the off-window, shots-since, wrecked-since and per-cause truncation lines, the two lists capped at eight entries, and writes the actor's RAM cursor, which every refusal but the two end-of-tape ones drops; `Match 12 is still running.` is the one answer for an unfinished match, before the ring is consulted. The last keyframe of a replay not cut at keyframes runs to `end_ms` (`Replay::horizon`), because the arena writes no closing summary and the shot and the wreck that ended almost every elimination would otherwise be shown nowhere. Every panel query is keyed on the actor and carries no parameter, because `Query::PanelSubscribe` carries a feed alone and the web bridge forwards nothing else: `arena.replay` answers the cursor's whole document under `schema { id: "arena.replay", version: 1, shape: "replay" }` or `available: false` with a sentence, and `arena.map` answers in the order §5 now states. Doc 16's observer rule holds by construction: the document names callsigns and sides and no object number, pilot, contact id, draw, seed or z, and the observer battery reads `Match 1 is still running.` from every witness while match 1 runs and both callsigns and `both sides` from a bystander after. One owner question raised (16c `Q-I1`, default assumed): whether the line printer's one grid budget of 78 by 20 printed columns falls under the 120-column ruling.
- **§5 and §3.7 (the replay on screen 0 of the TUI and the web)**: as built (I.2): the grid `SIM REPLAY` pushes opens a replay of its match on screen 0 of both clients for a viewer holding no seat, as the cockpit screen's second layout gated on the manifest and (a seat or an open picture): the parameter-less `arena.replay {}` is asked ONCE for the whole document and `arena.map {}` under the cockpit's own map latch until the ground is held, the document re-asked only on a hello re-hydrate and on `p` and never while an ask is outstanding (the outstanding mark ageing after ten seconds, because the bridge drops a throttled query in silence and a 256 KiB answer re-asked on a timer would queue copies on a slow socket), a seated pilot's grid opens nothing, a seated frame closes an open picture, and any `available: false` answer closes the picture and dismisses the match. The picture has its own five-key table (`,` prev, `.` next, `u` focus, `x` close, `p` pull) drawn and dispatched instead of the cockpit's ten while it is the layout, stepping being a client action over a document the client holds and never a `Run` of `sim`. The TUI steps keyframes and never scrubs: the map windowed on the focus unit under an owner-rule legend, six status sentences at or under the 32-column Left pane (`Match 7  aborted`, `Keyframe 2/3  0:05 of 0:10`, `Focus RA  (u next)`, `Arena positions, every 5 s.`, `Both sides shown: match over.`, one `Cut:` line per cut word), wrecks and the step's shots newest first, and a selectable contacts pane whose `Enter` moves the focus; goldens at 120 by 34 and 160 by 48 pin the picture legend whole at the floor. The web scrubs: `replay.ts` interpolates cells linearly and headings along the shorter arc between keyframes, a clamped frame is `held`, `ReplayScreen` plays at one times arena time and labels every drawn frame (`Between keyframes 19 and 20: drawn, not recorded.`; `After keyframe 3 (0:10): the last recorded positions, held.`), and `ReplayStore`'s dismissal is keyed on the envelope seq and reset on a fresh attach. Parity is a second canonical fixture set, `web/src/lib/fixtures/replay/` (`replay.json`, `replay_window.json` blessed by `crates/tacsim/tests/replay.rs`, `replay_grid.txt` blessed by `mush-ansi` from the line client's print, `replay_view_model.txt`), the TUI's map rows at a 39 by 22 pane and the web's `composeReplayWindow` at 39 by 20 cells equal to `replay_grid.txt` byte for byte. The observer-rule battery reads whole TUI frames through an SSH window-change and shows both sides named by callsign, no object mark and no pilot, and a refused `SIM REPLAY 2` opening nothing (`available: false`, the unseated pair). Nothing under the arena library changed.
- **§3.1 "Clock and lag", "The seam" and "Pulse phases", §3.4 step 4, §4 and §7 (`@arena/tempo` and `@arena/place`)**: as built (I.3; 16c N10 at its defaults: slow and never a pause, a place in `Running` with nothing in flight to or from the unit, journalled always and announced never): staff slow a running match without pausing it and move a stuck unit with the tape recording it. `@arena/tempo <match>=<period_ms>`, `=off` or bare floors the task's WAKE interval in wall time, `engaged_ms` to `MAX_TEMPO_FLOOR_MS` 10,000 in steps of `engaged_ms` (`A tempo floor is 500 to 10000 ms in steps of 500, or off.`); the catch-up clamp stays on the adaptive tempo's own period, so a 5,000 ms floor at `Engaged` moves 2,000 ms of arena time a wake, at `Alert` 4,000, at `Quiet` 5,000, and the reply says all three (`Match 12 under a floor of 5000 ms: engaged 2000 ms of arena time a wake every 5000 ms, alert 4000 every 5000, quiet 5000 every 5000; orders wait for the next wake. @arena/tempo 12=off lifts it.`). Under a floor `next_due_ms` answers a wall interval and consults no due event (the arena is off the wall lattice on purpose), `Arena::step` cuts every floored pulse and the first after a lift back to the substep lattice (`floor_to`, the excess discarded as the clamp discards its excess; a wake inside a substep is a pulse of no time), the phase-4 zero-length drain also runs on a floored pulse of no time once arena time has started so a beam fired in a pull-in pulse lands at the millisecond it was fired, and lag is measured past the floor in force when the interval began, so a slowed match journals no `lagged` and neither does the pulse after a lift. `ArenaMsg::TempoFloor` is applied on arrival and, like `Stop`, pulls the task's deadline in to one millisecond; the floor is a view no hash folds, journalled once per change as `tempo_floor` under phase 5's hash (never on an ended match), the line moving the ledger sequence like every line, so the cross-tempo tests compare three floored runs with each other and a jittered floored run with a clean one; `MatchMirror.tempo_floor_ms` is written only after the task took the message and `@arena/status` prints `  tempo floor: N ms` while one stands. `ArenaConfig::validate` refuses `substep_max_ms > engaged_ms` by name (§7). `@arena/place <unit>=<x>,<y>` rides the intent path as `IntentKind::Place`, the eighth arm, stamped as the unit's own seat, discrete on `helm`, skipping the wreck, linkdead and rate gates and the task's per-unit bound (a staff act spends none of the pilot's eight, and a pilot's order is never dropped because staff placed the tank); phase 4's `resolve_place`, first in the match, tests in order `unknown`, `impassable` (off the window, not passable, or no way out), `occupied` and `in_flight` (any round whose target OR shooter is the unit, because the impact traces the line from the shooter's pose at the impact), takes the hash before any write, and on success moves the pose to the cell's centre at the cell's elevation with the heading kept, the speed, carries, holds, `blocked` and grade zeroed, the index moved, a machine's route forgotten (`ReflexMemory::forget_route`: goal, route and search; state, order, waypoints and target kept) and no contact dropped (ids and designators are drawn once per viewer and target for the match, so a drop could mint no new name; the next sensor pass moves the track), then journals `placed { unit, x, y, from_x, from_y }`; every place the arena receives leaves `placed` or `place_refused` with its reason (a phase-2 refusal under phase 2's hash; a place still buffered when a `Stop` lands as `stopped` before the stop line) and nothing is ever staged to a pilot, because a refusal naming a round in flight would leak an impact the pilot's sensors never saw. The world's own refusals (`That unit is not in a running match.`, `Match N is {state}.`, `Cell (x, y) is off the arena window.`, `Cell (x, y) is impassable, or has no way out.`, the busy and ended sentences) reach no tape; the reply `Placing #U at cell (x, y): the arena answers at its next pulse and the tape records it.` has one documented exception, a place queued in the pulse whose own substep ends the match, which leaves no line because nothing may follow `match_ended`. The staff fold of `@arena/report` prints the three kinds; the pilot's fold prints none. Four cross-tempo tests (three floors, a jittered floor, a place at a beat, a place refused under a round in flight at either end) and the task-shaped driver at period 500 under a 5,000 ms floor (every wall gap 5,000, every wake 2,000 arena ms) prove the floor and the place decide no outcome; the harness's deterministic block was equal and no baseline moved.
- **§3.1 "The seam" and "Pulse phases" step 5, §3.7, §5 and §1 (`@arena/watch` and the `Observer`)**: as built (I.4; 16c N10's third lever): staff see a running match. `arena/observer.rs` holds `Observer { now_ms, tempo, in_flight, units }` and `ObserverUnit { unit, callsign, team, cell, heading_mdeg, speed_cms, power, wreck, held, controller }`, a type distinct from the pilot's `Frame` with no path into one, built by `Arena::observer()` in phase 5 after the frames at most once per pulse and once an arena second while `Arena::watched` is set, and on the pulse the match ends; `watched` and `last_observer_s` are views, so a watched match hashes equal to an unwatched one and the cross-tempo test proves it at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms with one event list and one hash sequence (the pictures counted per distinct arena second). `ArenaMsg::Watch { on }` is seam bookkeeping applied on arrival and held to the inbox reserve; `ArenaOut::Frames.observer` carries the picture, held latest-wins when the egress is full and sent by `Task::end`; an abort's `stopped()` builds none. The world keeps the watchers on `ArenaRegistry` (`bind_watcher`, `unbind_watcher`, `watchers_of`, at most `MAX_WATCHERS` 8 a match), publishes `match_::observer::observer_frame` (schema `arena.observer` version 1 with `retain: latest`; `match`, `now_ms`, `clock`, `tempo`, `floor_ms`, the ground's size and positional unit rows of callsign, team letter, cell or `null`, whole degrees, object id and flags) on the new feed to each watcher still present and still `SIM_ADMIN`, answers the feed's query with the last picture or `watching: false`, and answers a watcher `arena.map` the watched ground. `@arena/watch <match>`, `off` or bare refuses in order an actor who holds a centre slot (their own or any of their account's, a dropped or `EXIT`ed pilot's slot counting) or a seat (`You are flying, or about to; EXIT and leave your slot before you watch.`), a match the book does not hold or not `Running`, and a ninth watcher, then drops the actor's replay cursor and binds; `SIM REPLAY` refuses a watcher with `You are watching match N; @arena/watch off first.`. A disconnect, `finish` (unbinding before `untrack`, the cleared frame sent after) and a lost `SIM_ADMIN` at the match's next picture all unwatch; `BOARD` needs a slot, which no watcher holds. The first picture opens a live picture on screen 0's layout of the TUI and the web, closing an open replay; `,`, `.` and `x` are unbound live, `u` walks the units, `p` pulls, and only `watching: false` closes it (a local close the next keyframe undoes is worse than no key); both clients re-ask the picture after fifteen quiet seconds so a server-half restart closes it honestly; the TUI's status sentences (`Match 12  engaged  floor 5000`, `Live, at most once a second.`, `In flight: 2`, `@arena/watch off stops it.`) and its contacts rows fit the 120-column floor. The payload names object numbers on purpose, and three pins hold that no pilot is ever sent it: `arena_library.rs`'s scan (`cockpit.rs` and `Arena::frames` never name the type), the `mush-server` composition test over a second connected character and the registry bound directly (every `arena.observer` event reaches the watcher and never a pilot), and the observer-rule battery (God watches over the web while Ada's fenced socket receives no `arena.observer` envelope, Cy's query answers `watching: false`, and Ada's `@arena/watch 1` is `Permission denied.`).
