A 300-unit picture prints at 11,618 bytes under `max_frame_bytes` 16,384, and eight watchers add at most eight events to a pulse under `max_output_events_per_pulse` 512 (`T-36`'s bound). The line client prints nothing of a watch (the payload has no shape), a 16c residual.
- **§6 "Deliberation" and "Provider failure", §7 and §8 (the world task's service on the production shape)**: as built (16b cut AC.1, 2026-09-26; 16c `B-82`; the owner's fix cut before the dev1 redeploy): §6's seam, `LlmService::complete` off the world loop, ran on the all-in-one `mushd` only. On the production shape (`mush-gateway` supervising `mush-server`) `mush_server::serve` built the world task's `ServerDeps` from `in_memory()` and overwrote five fields but never `llm`, so every LLM-backed `Effect::ModuleAsync` on dev1 met no service: a squad's deliberation re-entered `unavailable_reply` and stayed on doctrine (the fallback of "Provider failure", reached before any provider was asked, with `WARN no LlmService on this world task` on every signal since 2026-09-21), and an NPC turn, `@gen` and a weather top-up were dropped, while the same boot's `llm service built` line and the NPC decision-provider registry, fed from a different place, held the service. Now `ServerDeps::production(&BootedEngine, store, guard, metrics, quarantine_spool, outbox_adapters)` in `mush-server/src/world_task.rs` is the one constructor both `mush_server::serve` and `mushd::main` call: it takes the boot, so the booted staff queue and service cannot be orphaned and `llm` is never optional in production; the split keeps `booted` whole, makes the process's one metrics registry explicitly (the `SocketSink`, the writer and reader loops, the archive pass and the world loop share it), and feeds the provider registry from `deps.llm.clone()`, the same read the all-in-one's wrapper makes, so the registry and the effect dispatcher are fed from one field. No seam: `boot.rs`, every `serve*` signature and the gateway half's code and dependencies are untouched. Pins: `boot_parity.rs` holds the three identities (`llm`, `staff` against the boot, `metrics` against the caller's registry) and counts at the source of both roots one `ServerDeps::production(`, no `ServerDeps::in_memory()`, no `ServerDeps {` and one `Metrics::new()` in `lib.rs`'s production half (comments count); a world-task unit pin drives a tacsim `Effect::ModuleAsync` at a mock and reads a `malformed` refusal after exactly two calls with the service and an `unavailable` refusal after none without it; and `mush-gateway/tests/two_process_llm.rs` boots the real split through the unchanged `serve_with_store` with `[llm]` naming a keyless `openai`-kind provider at a loopback stub (the config-level stand-in dev1's own path goes through), walks one pilot into a match against a machine squad and proves the stub is hit, the log carries `world task running` and `llm service built enabled=true providers=1 routes=1`, and the WARN never prints; it fails on the old `lib.rs`. Nothing in §6's text changes: the floor, the bounded order, the time to live and the doctrine fallback are as written, and the fallback is now what an unreachable provider produces rather than what every production deliberation produced. From the deploy made from this cut, dev1 spends on the provider for the first time across four features (`tacsim_deliberate`, `npc_dialogue_extra`, `npc_dialogue_minor`, `npc_summarize`) inside the shared daily `[llm.budgets] global` cap and the background concurrency caps, with no per-feature cap on any of them (a 16c row for a later cut). Residual: the split proof settles 1 s between the room walk and `BOARD` because a `BOARD` typed the instant the room view arrives was reproduced going unanswered on the split, with and without `[llm]`, a separate defect the sleep accommodates and does not pin (a 16c row with an SSH-level repro is owed).
- **§7 "Configuration" (the IC clock as the instance's)**: as built (AC.2; 16c `T-88`): this document never names the world clock, and the game crate's configuration is unchanged; what moved is where the instance template's `[game].ic_ratio` and `ic_epoch` come from. They were literals in `deploy/instance/mushd.toml.tmpl` (`1` and `"2020-01-01 00:00"`), so every deploy's render silently reverted the `4` and `"2026-09-22 00:00"` the owner wrote into dev1's rendered `mushd.toml` by hand on 2026-09-22, and since `load_or_seed_world_timeline` seeds generation one from `[game]` only on an EMPTY store, a wipe after such a render would have frozen the template's clock into the new world. Now the template renders `ic_ratio = __IC_RATIO__` and `ic_epoch = "__IC_EPOCH__"` (the quotes are the template's; the env carries the bare `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`) from the instance env: `instance.env.example` at `1` and `2020-01-01 00:00` (the old literals) and `deploy/dev1/instance.env` at `4` and `2026-09-22 00:00`. The epoch is the IC reading at unix 0, not the date the clock starts at (`WorldTimeline::from_game_clock` seeds `real_anchor: 0, ic_anchor: epoch_offset, rate: ic_ratio.max(1)`), so for generation one today's IC date is the epoch plus the ratio times the seconds since 1970 (a staff `@tacsim/time rate=` re-anchors a later generation), and a dev1 world seeded at the 2026-09-27 redeploy reads about **2253-10-29 00:00** and gains four IC days a real day, a date the owner confirms before the wipe because only another wipe moves the epoch afterwards. `render.sh` exits INCOMPLETE and leaves the live config alone on an env without the two keys; a hand edit of the rendered file is reverted by the next deploy, and `scripts/reset-instance.sh` restarts on the rendered file without re-rendering, so the order for a world about to be wiped is edit the committed env, deploy, THEN wipe (the deploy README and `docs/ops.md` say so; `ops.md`'s stale `game clock installed` line is now the true `world timeline installed` line with generation and IC rate). Pins: `shipped_configs.rs`'s `t88_the_shipped_instances_render_the_clock_they_chose` holds both envs' parsed clock, a parsing `game_clock()` and the exact rendered lines, the placeholder-completeness test covering the tokens by construction; `provisioning_smoke.rs`'s boot case overrides the env to `3` and `2031-01-01 00:00` (nobody's default, no old literal, no shipped env) and pins the loaded config and a fresh store's generation one at rate 3 anchored at that epoch with `real_anchor` 0, so it fails on the old template. No Rust source, no verb, no config key and no gate moved.
- **§3.7, §3.8, §4, §5, §6 and §7 (`SIM MODE` and the Hold match)**: as built (16b cut O.1, 2026-09-26; 16c N11 at its defaults: Hold first, the mode both a lobby choice and an arena property, elimination still ends it, public in selection): a ground declares the modes it supports (`ArenaDef.modes`, `ArenaModeDef::Hold { spot, radius_cells, hold_ms }`, six load-time refusals in order: a mode declared twice, a spot the ground does not name (case-sensitive), a radius outside 1 to 32 cells, a time outside 10 s to 3,600 s, a time not in whole seconds, a square that reaches an entry zone; the resolved list rides `ArenaDefinition.modes` and is NOT folded into the definition digest, so a declaration moves no digest and a pack that adds one is still a new version: the proving ground is at version 4 declaring hold at The Crossroads, `B`, six cells, 120 s, taken by a live world through the shelf with the centre idle). `MatchMode { Skirmish, Hold, Range }` is closed; `SIM MODE` (the twelfth selector) reads for anybody in any state (`Mode: skirmish. The Proving Ground supports: skirmish, hold.` and the Hold sentence) and sets in `Selection` by a side's lead or staff, announced to every centre room and refused on a ground that declares no such mode (`arena {arena} declares no mode {mode}`, the same refusal at `Arena::new` and `match_::start`); `@arena/start` is always a skirmish. Under Hold the arena reads at every substep's end, after the due loop and before elimination and `spent`, which side stands alone inside the Chebyshev square of the key spot (one comparison per unit in id order, wrecks and disabled units excluded), opens a `HoldSettles` due event at `since + hold_ms` when a side becomes the sole occupant and drops the claim when nobody or both stand there (a stale event fires into nothing; `destroy_unit`'s retain keeps them); the arm awards the win only on a fresh presence reading at the pop, ends the match as `EndReason::Hold { winner }` (reason word `hold`, the outcome the holder's side, `red won (red held the key spot)`) before the two settles and shrinks the horizon as the deadline does, under three pinned tie rules (a holder wrecked at an earlier millisecond of the last substep voids the win, one wrecked at the hold's own millisecond still wins with the hit journalled first, a hold at the deadline's millisecond beats `Timeout`); phase 5 gains no backstop. The mode and the clock are hash item 12; the Hold clock crosses to no pilot (the board says the rule and the spot, never the clock; §5's inference). The result and replay documents carry `mode` (absent on a restart abort's document; absent reads skirmish), `HistoryRow.mode` is an `Option`, the ring line, the debrief header and both replay heads carry ` [hold]` (the TUI's on its replay map heading, the 32-column status row having no room), the board prints `  mode: hold — keep The Crossroads (B) uncontested for 120 s` under a non-skirmish mode only and the terminal head `red wins: held The Crossroads`. The machines: under Hold doctrine's own goal is the key spot in place of the hunt and the waypoint walk (`hold_goal`: parks at speed nought inside radius − 1, stands when it cannot close, gives up an unreachable spot as E.4 gives up the search through a `hold_retry_ms` row clock of `max(hunt_after_ms, repath_ms)`), a holder inside the square takes `release_cm` as its close edge and `here` as its goal so it fights from inside and never walks off, an `Advance` whose cell lies in the square ends on arrival inside it and hands over, and the commander's brief carries `objective: key spot B at 129,97; the side alone inside 6 cells of it for 120 s wins the match` with no order word, the system clause mapping engage, advance and hold. A report-only Hold lane (`TACSIM_HOLD_LANE=1`; never the weekly job, 16c Q-O3) prints `harness_lance_goal_cost` beside the lance lane's. The cross-tempo tests: two in `tests/arena.rs` and the three-cadence machines test in `tests/reflex.rs`. Recorded and not taken: an attacking machine contesting a held square (it stops at `engage_cm`, so a human can camp the spot against an AI side that shoots but never enters: 16c Q-O1), the blue-first asymmetry of the shipped ground, a second declaration (`Defend`, `Escort`).
- **§3.1 "Pulse phases", §3.6, §3.7, §4, §5 and the X.4 and U.2 entries above (`EMCON`)**: as built (O.2; 16c N12 at its default, on at check-out): a tank carrying a jamming pod can switch it. `UnitState.ecm_on` is arena state, `true` at every check-out, never written back and hash item 1.19 (one byte per unit), so no content byte and no digest moves; the one clause is in `Arena::jamming_at` (`wreck || power != Online || !ecm_on`), which both jamming folds read through the U.2 memo, so a silent pod neither blinds its carrier nor cuts its own side's mesh, and the memo is cleared on every flip. `EMCON on` and `EMCON off` are one discrete order on the sensors channel (`IntentKind::Emcon { on }`, the ninth arm; every pilot gate, no power gate), resolved in phase 4 beside `STARTUP` through `resolve_emcon`: a no-op journals nothing and stages nothing; a flip writes the switch, journals `emcon` (the unit and `on`, at the intent's timestamp under phase 4's hash; the twenty-ninth kind) and stages one notice to the pilot's own frame, `emcon_on` or `emcon_off` (`EMCON on: the pod radiates; every sight inside 60 m is jammed, your own among them.`, `EMCON off: the pod is silent; your own sight clears, and nothing hides you.`; the wire detail carries `radius_m`), while everyone in the bubble, the carrier included, still reads a receiver transition only at their own next pass. The frame carries `ecm` (`null` with no pod, else `on` or `off`), `STATUS` prints `emcon on: the pod radiates` or `emcon off: the pod is silent` after the receiver line, the TUI's and the web's scope row carry `  EMCON on` or `  EMCON off` as a suffix (no eighth row; `scope unidentified  EMCON off` is 29 of 32 columns), the ring words are `EMCON on`, `EMCON off: pod silent` and `no jamming pod`. The verb (the fourteenth cockpit verb, a non-baseline stanza) refuses locally a tank with no pod (`This tank carries no jamming pod.`, nothing forwarded), the dark panel before its first frame, and `EMCON is already on.` or `off.`, read from a bridge hint (one Quiet period plus a second, `STARTUP`'s shape) before the mirror so off then on inside one pulse are two true replies; the staff fold prints `emcon unit=RA(#101) on`, the pilot's own fold `you switched EMCON off.`. The observer rule holds: a bystander's frame reads `ecm` null and none of the EMCON words, learning of a silenced pod only through his own receiver clearing by the ordinary path. A machine keeps the switch it inherits (a released `EMCON off` tank fields a silent pod for the rest of the match; restoring on at handover needs an owner ruling); a flip flood is bounded by the observers' pass cadence and not floored. One cross-tempo test over the ECM fixtures at 5,000 / 1,000 / 500 ms.
- **§3.3, §3.7, §3.8, §4, §6 and the Q.8 and V.3 entries above (`target_mk1` and `SIM RANGE`)**: as built (O.3; 16c N13 at its defaults, a mode of the centre with still targets, and N2's hit rate): `tacsim.pack.target_mk1` version 1 is the fielded catalog's sixth extension table (`EXTENSIONS_TARGET`, last; compiled in, no apply, its digest pinned): the line tank's five sections, consequences, facet table, silhouettes and `sensor_height_cm` verbatim, the `power` role alone, no weapon, no sensor, no pod, `tracked` at `max_speed_cms` 0 (a legal ceiling: `UnitDef::check` puts no floor under it, and `cannot_close` reads it). `MatchMode::Range` is the third arm (code 2 on hash item 12, no new byte); `SIM RANGE [<n>]` is the thirteenth selector: bare it always reads (`Range: 2 targets on the blue side.`, `Range: 2 targets.` in a terminal centre whose rows are released, else `The range is not set; SIM RANGE <n> sets it in selection.`); a number sets in `Selection` by a side's lead or staff with every claimed slot on one side and `n` from 1 to the side's pods, and announces `Range set: 2 targets stand on the blue side; the match ends when every target is out of action, or on SIM ABORT.` to every centre room (`1 target stands` for one); `SIM MODE range` is refused (`SIM RANGE <n> sets the range.`), `hold` or `skirmish` clears the range, a claim on the targets' side is refused with the other side named, `SIM AI` reads `AI fill: none on a range.` and refuses a set, the board prints `  mode: range — 2 targets on the blue side` and never the `AI fill:` line. The four one-sided gates (`start`, `settle_boarding`, `board_deadline`, `ready`) read the shooters' side alone under a range, so a range starts on a fill-off server with one claimed slot, the machines take no pod, the targets' pods 1 to `n` are refitted to `target_mk1` at `SIM START` (a refit refusal refuses the start with the pod's index; the `Idle` reset puts the authored type back) and fielded unseated under the reflex controller, and the boarding deadline and a leave drop shooters alone, aborting only a centre with no slot. A range ends by the ordinary elimination rule (every target a wreck or disabled with no weapons: a target is out of action by the wreck, both tracks or the engine through the rear) and is never `spent` (`spent` needs both sides armed at check-out); `unattended` applies as today. A unit that cannot move and carries no weapon is inert to the reflex controller: a cold one takes `STARTUP`, an online one is parked in `Patrol` with its goal, route, target, hunt and last plot cleared and never reaches the search, the hunt, a path query, `hold_goal`, the `Approach` rule or the `Advance` clause (so a range target never turns), and `take_deliberation_signals` skips a squad whose commander is inert, so the commander is never asked about targets. The pilot's debrief line carries the hit rate: `You flew RA: destroyed; 14 shots, 6 hits (42%).` (rounded down, `(0%)` for shots and no hit, no bracket at no shot; staff rows unchanged). The ring line, the report header and both replay heads carry ` [range]` through O.1's tag; the outcome words are the skirmish's (`red won (blue eliminated) [range]`). The range is public in `Selection` (the announcement and the board line reach everyone in the centre) and the report is the pilot's own. `SIM UNIT target_mk1` for a human is allowed and harmless. Cross-tempo tests in `tests/weapons.rs` (the range's end under real fire) and `tests/reflex.rs` (the inert unit).
- **§5 and §1 (the observer rule on a name: `@macro`)**: as built (16b cut AD.1, 2026-09-27; 16c N14 at its defaults, platform-wide with three positional arguments, and `Q-AD1` to `Q-AD3` at their defaults): a player names a line once and types the name, on every surface, and no macro reaches a verb its player could not type. `@macro <name>=<line>` defines or replaces, bare `@macro` lists, `@macro/del <name>` deletes: a kernel verb in `mush-core/src/commands/macros.rs` (Player, no lock) beside a kernel `MacroStage` that `register_core_commands` registers, so it is the first alias stage on every composition and comsys's channel aliases ride behind it. A name is 1 to 16 letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.`, folded to lowercase, and its characters are checked BEFORE anything consults the registry, so every `@` or `+` word answers the one `BAD_NAME` sentence whether or not a staff verb of that name exists (`@arena`, `@unit` and `@zzqnotaverb` read byte-identical; `observer_rule.rs` pins it); a bare word answers the one `TAKEN` sentence, naming nothing, when it is `home`, `quit` or `respond` (answered before the command table), an exact registered name or alias of any command whatever its role (`Registry::spec_of`), or a word an alias stage claims through the new default `AliasStage::claims_word`, whose doc binds every stage to the actor's own settings or static vocabulary (comsys answers from `membership_by_alias`); a word that only shortens a verb (`f` for `fire`) or names an exit is allowed, the exit winning whenever the whole typed line is its name (the TUI's exit keys and the web's exit buttons submit exit names) and the note `Note: north is also an exit here; typed alone, the exit wins.` said only for an exit the actor's room view lists. A line is 1 to 200 characters, starts with a word and not `"` `:` `;` `\` (`Q-AD1`), holds no `$` in its first word (the verb is fixed at definition), is never `@wait` (`Q-AD3`: the one player verb that re-dispatches a stored line) nor `@sshkey`, `@newpassword` or `@newchar`, and never chains to another of the player's macros in either direction; twenty per character (`Q-AD2`). The document is one JSON attribute `macros` on the player's own object, written with `World::set_attr` on the store's ordinary snapshot path the way `@ignore` keeps its list (no state slot, no namespace, no pack, no hash), read from the object's own map once per typed line and never the parent chain, a text-valued attribute holding no macros, so nothing `@set` can write is ever expanded. The stage, on a typed macro: `NotMine` when the whole line names an exit here; `That macro takes nothing after its name.` when the line uses no `$n` and words were typed; `That macro's line comes out too long.` over 8192 characters (the typed-line cap mirrored); the expansion's verb refused as the definition refuses it, whatever the document holds; then one `tracing::debug!` (actor, macro name, verb, never the arguments) and one `AliasOutcome::Rewrite` of the line with `$1` to `$3` from the words typed after the name, the highest `$n` used taking the rest of the line, a missing one empty, `$$` a plain dollar. Dispatch's step 3 changes its contract once: a rewrite is gated by `command_gates_refuse` at once and offered to the later stages only, so a stage never sees a line twice, a macro is expanded exactly once by construction and a channel-alias macro reaches comsys; the rewritten line then goes through `home`, exits and the command table's role, power, privilege and lock gates as the typist's own, so a refusal reads as it would had the line been typed (a mortal's `@dig` macro answers `Permission denied.`). Nothing in the arena, the frame, the hash or the renderers; `mush-bot` refuses `@macro` by its first word; `help macros`. Not built: `$*` and `$0`, per-account macros, `@wait` inside a macro, a client-side set of macro names for the TUI's and the web's sticky modes (which wrap a macro as speech; the help says to type a macro with the mode set to none), and a verb registered later under a stored macro's name stays shadowed for that owner until they delete it (the list marks the row).
- **§3.1 "The substep length", §3.2, §3.3, §3.4 steps 2 and 4, §4 and §9 item 6 (every unit pays its own row; three chassis; The Bluff)**: as built (16b cut AE.1, 2026-09-27; 16c `B-58` and `T-18`, N15's chassis; `Q-AE1`, `Q-AE3` and `Q-AE5` at their defaults): a cell's price is the unit's locomotion row's. `CostRow { Tracked, Wheeled, Legged }` is read at check-out beside the slope limit through `driving_mode`'s ladder (tracked, wheeled, legged; `hover` or no mobility reads as tracked) and laid down on `UnitState.cost_row`, a fit constant the hash does not fold. `ArenaTerrain` carries one class table (`row_costs`, 18 classes by 3 rows, with `min_cost` per row at construction) that the loader alone fills from the shipped locomotion table through `ArenaTerrain::priced`; `ArenaTerrain::new` leaves it empty, so a fixture's `move_cost` is its word for every row and every fixture, the CI cohort and the canopy window are byte-identical; `TerrainCell.move_cost` stays the tracked price, and `has_way_out`, `min_move_cost` and `TerrainCell::passable` stay tracked readings (the two world-side prechecks in `verbs.rs` and the loader's zone check read them, so it is the arena and not the world that refuses a car's rubble or admits a walker's thick woods). Every reader that knows the unit reads its row: motion's factor and refusal (`Impassable`, the same word and notice), `path_with` with a row and the reflex's heuristic scale on that row's minimum, the shoulder rule, `first_step_is_open`, `way_ahead_is_open`, `hold_routeless`, the three goal pickers, `place_refusal`, `opening_pose` and `Arena::new`'s opening cell; the substep ceiling is the max over the units that can move of `max_speed × 100 / min(100, min_cost[row])` (the wheeled row's road at 80 gives a 3,000 cm/s car 3,750 and a 250 ms substep). `MobilityDef.wheeled` is a fourth mode (the tracked rule set; `tacsim.mobility.wheeled`, a fifth `schema_payloads` entry so no existing `DefinitionRef` moves; slope limit 25) and the engine's `locomotion.json` prices wheeled water at 400 (`LOCOMOTION_DIGEST` re-pinned). Three chassis are compiled in as `EXTENSIONS_CHASSIS` after the target: `scout_mk1` (wheeled, 3,000 cm/s, a hull, a turret, wheels as a pair and an engine; rubble, rock and heavy woods refuse it), `heavy_mk1` (the stock five sections at 1.75 times the armour and two thirds the speed) and `biped_mk2` (the beam in an arm, a thermal head, heat 60/3, legged 1,000); `SIM UNIT` lists ten types. The ground moved: the atlas region pack `tacsim.proving_ground` is at 2 with The Bluff, a grass mesa at 141..147 by 105..118 at +500 cm over a −200 shelf whose 104 rim edges rise 700 cm (70 per cent orthogonal, 49.5 diagonal: tracks and wheels refused every way, legs admitted; one corner 800 for the shoulder rule) with a ramp down its north side (230, 230, 240) whose foot any chassis enters from the west and a tank from the east, every edge past 45 per cent between passable cells on the map one of the bluff's 107 and the steepest elsewhere 20 orthogonal and 21 diagonal; the arena pack `tacsim.pack.proving_ground` is at 5 with the fifth key spot The Bluff at 144,111, lettered `F`, and its note rewritten to 1,006 bytes; the generator lives in `tests/support/ground.rs` (`atlas_pack_at(bluff)`), the fixture chain runs v5 to v4 to v3 to v2 with the atlas v1 and arena v4 digests pinned, and a shelf test boots at atlas 1 and arena 4 and takes both packs in order (the atlas plan one update, `field.q1_0`; the arena one update, latch 5, the twelve tanks kept). A live world takes the spot at the server-half restart (the definition is compiled in and its digest folds the spots, so every earlier replay loses its map there) and the ground through `@atlas/pack plan` and `apply` for `tacsim.proving_ground` then `tacsim.pack.proving_ground` at once after it with the centre idle and no `SIM START` between; the map draws ground and not height, and the help says where the ramp is. The CI harness cohort keeps its own one-table pricing and the committed baseline was re-blessed once for that leaf's text alone; the lance lane and the sight differential fight on the shipped ground priced from the table since this cut, W8 holding at the lane's defaults. `ArenaDefinition::digest` is unmoved by the table.
- **§3.5 "Scheduled impact", §5 and the F.2 entry above (the autocannon, the missile, the round traced from where it left)**: as built (AE.2, 2026-09-27; N15's weapons; `Q-AE2` at its default, no guidance and 250 m/s): four compiled packs, `ap_30` (30 mm AP, damage 20, 240 rounds) and `autocannon_30` (direct, bands 1 km at 4 and 2 km at 8, cycle 3,000 ms, burst 3, 1,050 m/s: three rounds a pull every three seconds, each its own draw under `burst:<seq>`, a cannon's damage a second in bites of 20), `missile_round` (350, shaped, 6 rounds) and `missile_lr` (direct, bands 600 m at 12 and 3 km at 2 so `alert_range_cm` is unmoved, cycle 10,000, burst 1, 250 m/s); `scout_mk1` at 2 with the autocannon first and the coaxial slaved, `heavy_mk1` at 2 with the six-round rack beside the cannon. The missile is a longer schedule and not a mechanism: no guidance, no incoming warning, nothing new on the wire; since a lock is never held past a sight (600 m own, about 900 m on a teammate's shared plot) a shot in play is 2.4 to 3.6 s in the air and the impact re-validates within one cell of where the target was, so a stock tank walks 2.8 cells out from under it and reads `miss: moved` while a machine holding a square, a range target or a hulk takes 350; the 1.5 km six-second case lives in the arena's tests with a planted contact. For every direct weapon the round is now traced from where it left: `Shot` records the shooter's pose and eye at acceptance (`from_x_cm`, `from_y_cm`, `from_cell`, `from_height_cm`; folded into `state_hash` as a count only) and `resolve_impact` traces the line to the target's current cell from that eye (`fire_trace_from`) and reads the facet from that point (`facet_band_from`) whether or not the shooter still exists, `note_no_line` teaching the reflex only when the shooter still stands in `from_cell`. An unheld target's miss carries no reason: the cockpit reads `Miss.` and the debrief `miss.` for a target the platform no longer holds (`Hit.` on an unheld target as before; a held contact keeps `Miss on X: <words>.` and `miss: <reason>.`), one gate in `step.rs` and one in the report fold. `FIRE`'s default stays the author's choice by component order (`cannon`, else the first weapon), no field declared; `WEAPONSTATUS` draws the new rows in the existing format, `FIRE auto` and `FIRE mis` are prefixes, and the help says to stay in sight until a slow round lands.
- **§2 W2, §3.3, §3.7, §3.8, §4 and §7 "Configuration" (one drone under the cap)**: as built (AE.3, 2026-09-28; N15's drone; `Q-AE4` at its default, the cap per side): `UnitClass { Armour, Walker, Drone }` is a closed word on the unit definition, written into the rules blob only when declared and derived when absent (a legged driving mode is a walker, else armour; `schema_payloads` untouched, so no earlier pin moved), read off the unit at check-out through the one platform change, `mush_tactics::TacticalUnit::rules()`, and laid down on `UnitState.class`, a fit constant not hashed. `[tacsim.arena].max_units_by_class` is a class-word map on every config layer, default `drone = 1`, each value 0 to 24 refused by name (0 means the class cannot be fielded), an operator's entries laid over the compiled default in `config_ext` (`walker = 2` leaves the drone at 1; an unknown word refused), written in `mushd.toml.example` as an inline table under its section and absent from the template, which reads the default; **the cap is per side and not per map** (W2's row amended above). `Arena::new` counts per side and class and refuses `ClassCap` by name; `SIM UNIT` refuses at the choice over the caller's own side's other slots by effective type (`A side fields at most 1 drone; pick another type.`; at 0, `This server fields no drones; pick another type.`), naming no other slot or pilot. The end rules a gunless unit needs, keyed on the class and never on arms: a side is eliminated when every unit it still has in action is a drone and it fielded a non-drone (a side of drones alone still stands while one runs); a drone holds and contests no key spot; a side that fields no gun is refused `NoGun` in `ready()` where the sides are final (`The red side fields no gun; a side needs a machine that carries one.`; a boarding drop that leaves a lone drone calls the match off, and every `ready()` refusal's call-off now reads with one stop, `Match n could not start: …. The pods are free again.`) and at `SIM START` as a courtesy before the first write through the same `fill_pods` walk; `spent` is unchanged. `drone_mk1` is compiled in last (class drone, one `body` section 60/50/50/40/40 over 60 internals, a visual sight on a 250 cm mast, no weapon, tracked 2,000 cm/s), never armed at check-out and so never approaching, engaging, locking or firing under the machine; it patrols, hunts the far zone, drives to a Hold spot and shares through the mesh like any machine, withdraws hurt, and a bare `FIRE` in it reads `This unit carries no weapon.` (`CockpitError::NoWeapon`; a named weapon keeps `NoSuchWeapon`); `SIM UNIT` lists eleven types. With the fill on at its default size a lone drone pilot is still refused `NoGun` (the fill tops nothing up beside one claimed slot; 16c `Q-AE6`). T-54's per-channel lease is not built: no unit of this cut carries a second station.
- **§3.1 "Durability", §3.4 and §4 (the arena as a document)**: as built (16b cut AF.1, 2026-09-28; 16c N16 at its defaults, Q40 amended; `Q-AF1`, `Q-AF3` and `Q-AF4` at their defaults): `arena/snapshot.rs` turns an `Arena` between two pulses into one self-describing document, `record tacsim.snapshot`, `format 1` (`format 2` since 16b cut AI.1, whose two reflex fields moved the carried shape: the AI.1 entry below), the ground id and its digest, the compiled locomotion table's digest (`LOCOMOTION_DIGEST`, which the ground's digest omits since AE.1, so a re-priced build is refused by name), `at_ms`, the two ledger sequences, `state_hash` as a decimal string, a SHA-256 `state_digest` and the state as one compact integer-only JSON string, so a store that re-serialises JSON cannot move a byte of it. All 43 `Arena` fields are placed by an exhaustive destructuring into a borrowed `ArenaStateRef` for the encode and an owned `ArenaState` for the decode, 31 carried (`UnitState` whole, the ledger's counters, open chunk and two SHA-256 running states through `SerializableState`, the clock's tempo and signal times, the three frozen config blocks, the squads, the squad watch and the expired set), 4 re-derived from the caller's ground and compiled code (the definition, the spatial index, the per-row cheapest cell, the jamming memo) and 8 views and per-pulse scratch reset, so a field a later cut adds does not compile until it is listed; the five maps keyed by tuples or the `SquadId` struct are written as pairs; `HashMap` joined the arena library's forbidden list. `Snapshot::decode` checks record, format, ground digest, physics digest, state digest, shape, the pose refusals `Arena::new` makes of a check-out (never `CellImpassable`), the state hash and the head's agreement with its state, in that order, on every decode; `Arena::resume_at(wall)` re-anchors the clock on the caller's wall with the floor lifted, so arena time does not advance across an outage. `[tacsim.arena].snapshot_ms` 5,000 (0 off, else 1,000 to 60,000) on every layer. Proved: a snapshot after every pulse of the scripted match resumed to a byte-identical tape, hash sequence, finals, notices, squad signals and ledger digests at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms, under the task's own wakes, across a drop and a reclaim, in a squadded arena and on the shipped twelve-tank centre with a round in flight (196,775 bytes at 130,000 ms); every refusal by name; the document's shape pinned structurally (`AF1_SHAPE_DIGEST`), moved only with a format bump.
- **§3.1 "The seam", "Durability" and "Pulse phases" step 5, §4 and §5 (the `Snapshot` message and the two documents)**: as built (AF.2; `Q-AF3` and `Q-AF5` at their defaults): the arena task reads `snapshot_ms` and, after the publication of the opening pulse and of every pulse that enters a new period of arena time, never on a pulse that ended or stalled the match, encodes its arena and hands the world `ArenaOut::Snapshot { match_id, snapshot }`, at most one waiting with a newer replacing the older, riding the retry queue behind every chunk closed before it and never counted against the chunk bound, drained by `Task::end` before `Ended`, so the world always holds a snapshot's chunks before the snapshot. The world writes `tacsim:snapshot:<n>` and its own live record `tacsim:live:<n>` (`record tacsim.live`, `LIVE_FORMAT` 1: the match record with its debrief folds and replay fold, the centre with its slots, the tape's counters and `resumed_from_ms`, the state one digested string; an added field is `#[serde(default)]`, anything else bumps the format and voids at the reload) in one action on the module's own lane, disjoint from the tape and readable by nobody but the boot; the live record is also written at check-out and the moment a staff abort or the unattended stop parks the record in `Stopping`, so a stop in flight at a crash is never undone; both go with the result on every end and with a boot that does not resume. `@arena/status` prints `last snapshot: at N ms of arena time`. The harness's 30-second lane alone takes the task's snapshots by the task's own rule and reports `harness_arena_snapshot` outside the compared block, the encode timed inline and never folded into the wake, the decode after the timed loop; `[tacsim.harness].max_snapshot_bytes` 7,340,032, three halves of the authored lane's measured 4,198,651 bytes rounded up to the next MiB, set after the measurement; off against on moved no lens, the encode costing about 12 to 14 ms on the task thread per snapshot of a 300-unit field, and about 215 KB together as stored for twelve tanks a minute into a fight.
- **§3.1 "Boot reconciliation" and "Durability", §3.7, §3.8, §4, §5, §6 and §8 "Restart mid-match" (the boot resumes)**: as built (AF.3, 2026-09-28; `Q-AF2` and `Q-AF4` at their defaults; 16c Q40 amended, the tail abandoned behind a marker rather than replayed): `mush-tactics`' boot pass keeps every `deployed` marker it finds, on the record, in `mush_sim::deployed` and on disk through both asset scrubs, and hands the list to the game crate through `custody::take_boot_deployed`; whatever the game crate does not claim in its own `on_world_loaded` is restored at `on_all_loaded` through `custody::restore_at_boot`. Phase A (`match_::resume::phase_a`, inside `boot_matches`) reads every live record and snapshot, raises the id high-water past both, and decides each marked match by a fixed order of named checks from the closed `ResumeRefusal` list of nineteen (the layer on, snapshots on, the live record present, decodable and `Running` on the record and on the centre, the head readable, the grounds and the locomotion table agreeing, the two documents from one moment, the markers exactly the record's roster, not already resumed from this snapshot, the ledger tail restored from its own chunk documents, the tape reaching the snapshot; then, in phase B, the ground built and matching its digest, the state hash, a runtime, the task opened); a match that cannot resume is voided by name through `write_restart_abort` with one error line, its units restored and its seats released, and the document pairs nobody claims are deleted. Phase B (`on_all_loaded`, before the pod sweep) runs per match in id order with the fallible steps first, the ground built once per digest, one `Snapshot::decode`, the `resumed { from_chunk, at_ms, restart_ms }` marker prepared and the task re-opened through `resume_arena` on a fresh base with `flushed_upto` at the last closed chunk; then the record and the centre restored as the live record left them, the marker appended in the boot's own write, every seat the arena held released to doctrine (`slot_dropped`, `Released` on every channel) and every seated slot the arena does not hold re-dropped, the unattended clock restamped and the live record rewritten with `resumed_from_ms`, the resume-once rule's memory (a process that dies again inside one period voids rather than resumes). `tape_in_order` truncates every kept chunk above a marker's `from_chunk` and every reader uses it; the staff pager prints `-- resumed at chunk N (M ms of arena time); the old server's chunks after N are abandoned --` at its place under 120 columns with nothing on the fold reset. A pilot the resume released reads `The server restarted and the match resumed from its last save; what happened after that save did not count.` then `<tank> is flying on doctrine; BOARD takes it back.` (*`<tank> is flying on its mind; BOARD takes it back.` since 17b cut M5.3, §11*) once, through the boarding tick with the surviving client's panel cleared or through a later login, and takes the tank back through Y.3's reclaim and Y.4's catch-up; `@arena/status` prints `resumed: from a snapshot at N ms of arena time`; the pod sweep leaves a resumed match's pods and tanks alone while a crash during `Boarding` still opens the hatch (B-37). A resume forgets the human-order watches, span and rate, the `@arena/pose` overrides, the staff tempo floor, a watcher's binding, the hints, the replay cursors, the last frames and a deliberation in flight; every released tank goes to `Withdraw` until its pilot boards; no final snapshot is taken at shutdown, so a planned reload rewinds too. The deliberation deviation (§6) now says durability would preserve the wrong thing. Proved on the in-process reboot rig with a cumulative store image (fifteen void arms by reason, both seat reconciliations, the tail restored before the first append, the cross-tempo resume at three cadences), on `mushd` (the resume, the wrong-snapshot void, the layer-off and `snapshot_ms = 0` voids, the lobby rematch, the observer rule across the resume) and on the split (the sessions the gateway held boarding the resumed match).
- **§6, §3.4 step 4, §3.1 "Rate independence" and the Q.7, E.4 and AF.1 entries above (the follower's circle, the shoulder, the refusal memory, the cell bound and the hunt fan)**: as built (16b cut AI.1, 2026-09-29; 16c `B-84` whole, `Q-AE8`'s second option built and its third, an arena-side cell budget, not built; `Q-AE7` at its default): the reflex follower knows its turning circle in integers (`r_cm = v_look × 57,296 / turn_rate_mdeg_s`, `v_look` the speed one decide tick ahead capped at the chassis's; a point at offset `(dx, dy)` is inside the circle iff `(dx² + dy²) × SIN_ONE < 2 r × |dx cos H − dy sin H|`, ahead of the beam iff `dx sin H + dy cos H ≥ 0`, all `i128`, no angle, no root, no float and no new state) and steers at the first of its route's next eight cells that is outside the circle, ahead of the beam and reachable over an open straight line, else the first outside and open, else the first open, else the route's first cell; whatever point it aims at, when that point is inside the circle the ordered speed is capped at the turn speed that just reaches it, so a machine converges instead of orbiting; consumption drains the route to the last of its first nine cells the unit stands in or can drive to in one step (passable to its row, not on the avoid list, within its slope limit both ways, on a diagonal at least one shoulder open), never across an edge the search refused; a route whose first cell is no longer a neighbour is kept while the nearest of the next eight cell centres is within `2 r + cell_cm` and one has an open line, and cleared with `searched_ms` otherwise, exactly as Q.7 did. One shoulder rule, `path::shoulder_state` (`Absent` off the window, impassable to the row or on the avoid list; `Steep` when either leg is too steep; else `Open`), in three readers: under a slope limit the search refuses a diagonal with a steep shoulder as before (`TooSteep`, `slope_refusals`) and a diagonal with no open shoulder as `Closed` (`corner_refusals`, new; the class gap `path_with`'s own doc named, closed under the limit; `path()` with no limit unchanged node for node), and a limited search that has expanded `POCKET_PROBE_CELLS` (1,024) nodes without its goal floods the goal's side once under the same edge rule and answers a sealed pocket as `NoRoute` for the pocket's cost; the follower's one-neighbour test counts the same two ways; and the steer drives a diagonal whose shoulders are both open at the diagonal cell and one with exactly one open shoulder at that shoulder's centre, while an undrivable first step re-plans a limited unit once (the route and `searched_ms` cleared; the new route's first step is search-accepted from here) and holds an unlimited one with its route. `note_blocked` is per cause: an `Occupied` stop is Q.7's rule unchanged; a stop by the ground at the route's own next cell clears the route and, only when the route was planned from the cell the machine still stands in (`ReflexUnit.searched_from`, hashed), records a `Refusal { at, cell, cause, goal, until_ms }` (`ReflexUnit.refused`, hashed) that refuses the same search for the same goal from the same cell by name until `REFLEX_AVOID_TTL_MS` has passed and counts it (`path_refused_unchanged`, expected nought on every lane and read nought on every one: at these rules the memory cannot be reached through `Arena::step`, since a route planned from here has a search-accepted first step, the ground is immutable and the avoid list grows only by `Occupied`; it is kept as 16c's named guard, a count being a controller defect), cleared by a spent search, an `Occupied` stop, an avoid entry ageing out, `adopt`, `forget_route` and `drop_to_doctrine`; a stop by the ground beside a route whose next cell is still a neighbour keeps the route with its one `blocked` line (the corner stop's own arm: motion reports the shoulder entered, never the route's cell); a routeless stop clears and records nothing. Both new fields ride the snapshot's derive, so `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` is 2 and AF.1's structural pin is re-blessed at 386 lines; the hash's item 3 folds both after `hold_retry_ms`. `path_cells` counts every search's expansions, found or failed (`PathScratch::last_expanded`: `path_max_cells + 1` on a `Budget` failure, the region or the probed pocket on `NoRoute`), and `[tacsim.harness].max_path_cells_per_pulse` (48,128 on eight sites: the largest post-fix per-wake peak over both arena lanes at seeds 2350 to 2353, 41,279, times 1.16 rounded up to a multiple of 1,024) is asserted per wake beside the query bound and never throttles (D-16-2). The hunt (E.4) is fanned: `far_entry_cell` answers E.4's cell when it is at least `HUNT_FAN_ROWS` (3, Chebyshev) from every hunt cell its side already holds, else walks the zone's rows out from its own (`cy ± 3, ± 6, …`) for the first clear cell under the shared scan budget, and E.4's cell when the fan runs out; `hunt_goal` reads the side's other reflex rows for it, so no new state and no new fold. Every decision is taken at the decide tick's own millisecond or the `blocked` line's; nothing per pulse. Measured on the CI cohort at seed 2350: `blocked` 212 → 103 (impassable 192 → 85, `too_steep` 2 → 0, occupied 18), path queries 713 → 460, deferred 391 → 148, failures 1 → 19 (sealed pockets now failing at the pocket's cost); on the authored lane the per-wake p99 at seeds 2350 to 2353 11.1 / 17.4 / 9.3 / 9.6 → 8.1 / 10.5 / 9.7 / 8.7 ms and the per-window 17.0 / 29.0 / 16.9 / 18.2 → 14.3 / 15.8 / 17.2 / 15.2, `path_refused_unchanged` nought on every run; the CI baseline blessed once with the reason. Proved: a slow-turning follower posed away from its goal arrives with at most two searches where HEAD circled and searched every tick (seven at HEAD); a limited unit at an exact cell boundary against a corner passes through the open shoulder from rest and at speed with no `blocked` line and one search; a both-closed corner holds an unlimited unit with one search and two parked tanks age out; a shove inside the circle keeps the route and one beyond it re-plans within a tick; a limited unit whose route comes back beside it, at a pinch and below a plateau, searches once and arrives; all of it one log at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms with a stop beside a kept route at one millisecond, corner refusals counted and no `too_steep` line; the memory refused by name and released four ways, in-source at three cadences; the two fields in the hash; the closed corner refused under a limit with `path()` unchanged and the m4 property green; the hunters of one side handed clear cells at three cadences. Player-facing, no new sentence: a wingman that wandered arrives instead of circling, a tank on a corner steps through the open shoulder, re-plans once or holds; a piloted tank keeps F.2's `Held:` line once per stop; no frame field, notice, verb, draw label or pack. A reload across this cut voids a running match on a box already on AF, logged once as `snapshot format 1 is not 2`; deploy between matches. Known and left (the AI row's Deviations cell): one or two `too_steep` motion stops at five of the eight seed-and-lane runs (the arc a turning machine drives is not the chord the open-line walk asks about; each is a controller defect to read, not a pin); the fan hands E.4's cell to a side with more hunters than its zone has rows; a corner closed on both shoulders by ground holds an unlimited unit for ever (every fielded unit is limited); the routeless aim (the goal as a direction) is unchanged; the search's diagonal price still assumes the corner cut; `bearing_of` stays `f64::atan2` in the heading order alone.
- **§3.7, §3.6, §3.2 and §5 (`VIEW BRIEF`: the window in sentences)**: as built (16b cut AG.1, 2026-09-29; 16c N17 at its default, built after the fighting features; `Q-AG1` to `Q-AG4` at their defaults: prose class words, a covered spot named by neither line, a stray word refused, line mode only): `VIEW` takes one second word. Bare, it draws the map byte for byte as before (the grid keyframe, `beyond_window_line`, `off_window_spots_line`); `BRIEF`, case-folded, says the same window in sentences; any other word answers the one usage line `VIEW [BRIEF]; BRIEF says the window in sentences.`, the same bytes for a slot designator, a provisional number, sixteen digits or a word and never an echo, so `VIEW BA` says nothing `VIEW ZZ` does not. It is an arm of the existing `view` behind the seat gate `cockpit_command` resolves once (no verb, no stanza, the legacy-handlers baseline unmoved at 97, the seatless refusal byte-identical by construction) and a second renderer of the one frame: `brief_lines(frame, terrain, spots, window, ranges)` in `match_/cockpit.rs` is pure and world-free over the pilot's own frame, the surveyed ground and the very payload `view_window` would push now (the one map-window rule, the Q.3 and B.4 entries above), built and then NOT pushed: the lines go out as `CONTACTS`' do through `say_and_frame`, followed by the status keyframe, so the one grid a pilot is sent is still a bare `VIEW`'s, and the two dark-window lines are shared by both arms (a ground gone between the arm's two reads is the no-ground line, never a panic). The brief, in order: a header in the footer's words (`Window around RA: cells 0 to 38 east and 0 to 19 north of 96 by 64; you at (6, 4), heading 045°; 10 m a cell.`, read off the payload's `origin_cell`, `width`, `height`, `extent` and `you`); the ground on eight bearings in compass order (`BRIEF_BEARINGS`, north first, clockwise) walked one cell at a time from the payload's own `@` (`origin_cell + you`) to the payload's own rectangle, runs merged by `TerrainClass` and said as prose from one exhaustive `class_prose` table (`bare ground`, `light woods`, `heavy woods`, `deep water`, `doorway`, `paving`; the legend keeps its labels), cells to metres by `ray_cm` (`k × cell_cm × 1,414 or 1,000 / 1,000`, root two per mille on a diagonal) rounded at `brief_step_m(cell_cm) = (cell_cm / 100).clamp(1, SPOT_RANGE_STEP_M)` (ten metres at the shipped cell and no coarser than a cell below it, so two runs never read one reach), each line `North: 50 m of grass, then road to 60 m, then grass to 150 m, then beyond the window.` where the map stops or `, then the edge of the arena.` where the ground does (a pilot the clamp put on a window's edge is on the arena's, so the no-runs line always reads the edge; at most 19 cells straight from the centre, 9 north and 10 south, 38 straight and 19 diagonal from an edge); every held contact as `CONTACTS` words it, in frame order (`BA, blue: bearing 045° (rel 000°), far, 450 m, 1 s old.`, the row's own fields, no lock or friendly mark) or, since 16b cut AP.4, `No contacts in the window.`, the brief's own sentence (`No contacts.` until then; the AP.4 entry below); `VIEW`'s own beyond-window sentence; `In the window: A The Ford, 190 m at 093°.` over the spots the payload's legend names (authored indexes, the owner rule of the V.1 entry: a spot drawn under a contact or the `@` is named by neither line, as by neither of `VIEW`'s), then `VIEW`'s off-window line verbatim, the two sharing one `spot_clause`, and since AP.4 the fixed `End of brief.` last. At most `13 + C` lines since AP.4 (`12 + C` before), one `Sys` event each (thirteen with no contact, 24 at six a side); no width budget, because a brief is `Sys` prose every client wraps at spaces (the line client, the TUI notices pane, the web ring; `Q-I1`'s 78-column grid budget stays the grid's). The observer rule holds by construction (every sentence is a function of the pilot's own frame, the surveyed ground and the payload `VIEW` would push; the brief names no cell outside the rectangle the grid draws, no spot the legend does not name and no unit) and is asserted in `observer_rule.rs` as a projection: every token of Ada's selected brief sentences before and after detection is a number, a word of the brief's closed vocabulary (`BRIEF_WORDS`) or a token of her own `VIEW`, `CONTACTS` or `STATUS` answers; no contact sentence and no side word before detection, one contact sentence per `CONTACTS` row after under the same designator, Bo's brief names nothing, `VIEW BA` and `VIEW ZZ` one refusal. The seventeen lines (sixteen before AP.4) of the fixture scene are pinned as `web/src/lib/fixtures/cockpit/view_brief.txt` beside the three JSON files (`TACSIM_BLESS=1`; read by `tests/cockpit.rs` and `include_str!`'d by `sim_cockpit.rs`; no renderer reads it). Not built, on purpose: the brief on the TUI or the web as a pane of its own (it reaches both as text), `BRIEF` on `SIM REPLAY`, an elevation word (the map draws ground, not height), line of sight (the walk describes cells and says the ground under a marker where the grid draws the glyph). Residuals: a 38-run bearing line (about 800 characters) floods a short notices pane as a long `CONTACTS` does; diagonal reaches are at the ten; at 50 cm cells the metre floor can merge two reaches (no such ground ships); under 80 by 24 the line client clips the grid while the header names the unclipped payload's rectangle.
- **§5, §3.7, §3.8 and §1 (first-time hints: the next verb, once per account)**: as built (16b cut AH.1, 2026-09-29; 16c N18 built on the owner's word of 2026-09-27 rather than at its with-the-second-player default, and `T-77`'s two Seeing sentences made numeric; `Q-AH1` to `Q-AH5` at their defaults: a tacsim document not the account record, no re-arm, staff read the lobby hint, no mark without a live connection, `@tutorial` not a `SIM` selector): at five points of a first match the simulator says one fixed sentence naming the verb that works there, once per ACCOUNT and never again — `Hint: SIM TEAM red (or SIM TEAM blue) claims a pilot slot; @tutorial off stops these hints.` to a Player moved into the Simulator Lobby by any route while a claim would be taken (the centre absent, `Idle`, `Selection` or terminal, and the walker's own account holding no slot); `Hint: walk into your side's team room (red or blue from the lobby) and type BOARD.` to every claimed slot's pilot after `SIM START`'s boarding announce; `Hint: the panel is yours now: STARTUP brings your power up.` on the pilot's first frame of the match whose power is `Off`; `Hint: VIEW draws the ground around you as a map, north up, with the named spots on it.` on the frame carrying `Power online.`; `Hint: LOCK <contact> holds the contact your panel just named; FIRE shoots your lock.` on a frame with power `Online`, a weapon aboard and a contact classified as the other side's (a wingman's classification, AE's gunless drone and a hostile told apart before `STARTUP` consume nothing). The three frame-borne points come from the pure `match_::tutorial::frame_points(first, &Frame)`, read in `arena_bridge::on_frames` before the mirror write and said after the pilot's keyframe in point order, only to a pilot holding the centre's slot for that match, so a staff seat from `@arena/start` (a match the centre holds with no slot), a watcher, a machine, a replay and a bystander read none. Each sentence is one of five string constants (`HINT_LINES`, 91 to 59 columns, nothing interpolated), so a hint can name no unit, pilot, designator or place and §1's observer rule holds by construction; a hint is `Sys` output on the arena's reply category (`NOTICE_CATEGORY`), rung by both panels as every cockpit reply on that category is (the tag route of 16b cut B.1), never a frame field, a notice kind, a ledger event or a line of the tape, the replay or the report. `tutorial::hint(ctx, actor, point)` says and marks a point only when a session with a live connection (`World::has_live_conns`, not `is_connected`) would read it, the layer is on, the sim's account oracle maps the character (fail closed) and the account's record is switched on and has not read it; a point refused by any gate is not marked and waits for its next occasion. The record is one `tacsim:tutorial:<account>` document per account (`{record: tacsim.tutorial, account, off, shown}`) on the module's own persist lane beside its pins, loaded at boot into `TacsimStore.tutorials` under the identity rule (a body under another account's key is skipped with a warning that carries no key), with no cap and no `Del`: bounded one-to-one by accounts, one bool and at most five words each, visible through the verb; keyed by the immutable `AccountId` §3.8 keys a slot by, so an alt made with `@newchar` reads nothing again, the opposite of AD.1's per-character macros. `@tutorial` (a Player verb, anywhere, layer on or off; one `legacy-post-image` stanza, the baseline unmoved at 97): bare answers `Hints are on; you have read {n} of 5.` or `Hints are off; you have read {n} of 5.`, `off` answers `Hints are off: the ones you have not reached stay unsaid.`, `on` answers `Hints are on: the ones you have read stay read.` and re-arms nothing read, any other word the one usage line, an unmapped character `This character has no account on record, so it has no hints to keep.`; one contract test resolves every command-shaped word of the five lines against the production registry (`SIM`'s next word against its switches). `help simulator` gains a Hints section, and `help cockpit`'s two Seeing paragraphs (16c `T-77`, landed without numbers by D.2) now carry the code's numbers as ceilings under a clear line of sight: the stock sight's 600 m by ground and motion (grass 80 per cent stopped and all of it moving, bare ground 90, road nothing, rubble 70/95, light woods 65/90, heavy woods 50/80), the thermal sight's 450 m by power (92 to 100 per cent under way, about two thirds stopped with power up, about an eighth shut down: `powered_permille` 650 and `cold_permille` 120 on both carriers), the 45-degree facet sectors, 7 m of flank against 3.5 m of bow, the stock tank's hull 400/250/150 and turret 350/220/140, the track's 120 three times in ten on a side band and the engine through the hull's rear, and the one correction that only from behind is the thinnest plate certain. Not built: a `SideFull` gate on the lobby point, a hint for the reclaim, the range or hold mode, a staff view or a `reset`, a TUI or web pane, per-character flags; a point marked in RAM whose document had not flushed at a crash is said once more after a resume, and a flushed flag outlives a rolled-back step (the 16b AH row's Deviations).
- **§5, §1 and §3.8 (a pilot that is a program: the external bot flies a pod)**: as built (16b cut AJ, AJ.1 to AJ.4, 2026-09-29; the owner's word of the same day, a hybrid brain, either role; 16c `Q-AJ1` to `Q-AJ5` at their defaults): no sentence of this document changed and no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved; what is new is a second reader of §5's line mode. `mush-bot --arena red` (or `blue`, or `/arena` at its prompt, or the goal loop's `arena` action) is an ordinary SSH line-mode client of the shipped game, with `tacsim`, `mush-sim` and `mush-server` as dev-dependencies only: `crates/mush-bot/src/arena/` classifies every line a seated pilot is sent (the cockpit's read replies and acks, every `notice_line` sentence, the lobby's and the centre's lines, the reclaim and resume sentences) into its own `Picture`, built only from its own session's text, so the observer rule holds for it by construction and it can hold no contact its platform's panel did not name. It types a closed vocabulary at no more than 4 lines a second on its own clock (16c `Q-AJ4`, under the session's flood bucket of 10 a second and the cockpit's 8 pending intents): the walk (`ooc`, `simulator`, the team room), `SIM STATUS`, `SIM TEAM`, `SIM START` only as its side's lead (its first claimant still holding a slot, after a grace; never under a human lead, `Q-AJ3`), `BOARD`, `STARTUP`, one `SIM LEAVE` on a stop while it holds a slot in `Idle` or `Selection` (since cut AM also when a stop finds a `SIM TEAM` or `SIM START` typed and unanswered: it waits for that answer, at most `[arena].step_timeout_ms` from its typing, then types the one `SIM LEAVE` whatever it learned and whatever the centre's state, none when the answer names the other side's slot), and the cockpit's `CONTACTS`, `RANGE`, `BEARING`, `WEAPONSTATUS`, `STATUS`, `LOCK`, `FIRE`, `HEADING` and `SPEED` (since cut AN also `VIEW BRIEF`, while its opening waits for the brief); never `SIM AI`, `SIM UNIT`, `SIM CONCEDE` or `ORDER` (`Q-AJ2`). A deterministic pilot written from `help simulator` and `help cockpit` alone (nothing of `arena/reflex.rs` read or mirrored) flies and fires, and an optional model sets its doctrine about every 1.5 s through the bot's own ledger and budget; the report counts a match won only on the centre's own `Match N is over (<word>).` naming its side. **What binds the game from now on**: the line-mode sentences are a contract with that reader. `crates/mush-bot/tests/arena_contract.rs` feeds the game's own pub formatters (the contact and weapon rows, the lock, range and bearing lines, every `FrameNotice` sentence) through `mush_ansi::render` at 120 columns into the bot's parser, and its live test boots the production composition inside the bot's test binary (under the bot's `serde_json` `arbitrary_precision`, the plan's canary; the match runs and ends, the ledger's decode there not asserted) and pins the lobby's private format strings, so a reworded cockpit or lobby sentence breaks `cargo test -p mush-bot` and not a match. The machines are unchanged: the report-only ten-match 1 v 1 figure (`arena_win_rate`, `#[ignore]`, owned by the `workflow_dispatch`-only `bot-win-rate` CI job) read 4 of 10 on 2026-09-29, and its reading is input for a later machine cut (`Q-AJ5`, the 16b AJ row).
- **§5, §1 and §3.8 (the external bot finishes what it starts)**: as built (16b cut AK, AK.1 to AK.3, 2026-09-30; the owner's word of the same day, run AK and keep chasing tail items as they appear; 16c `Q-AK1` to `Q-AK5` at their defaults): again no sentence of this document changed and no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved; the AJ entry above still describes the game's side of it, and what changed is the reader. The bot's pilot, still written from `help simulator`, `help cockpit` and its own transcripts alone, now hunts a hostile it lost (to the last plot first, then along the estimated course while the plot is under 90 s old, then one bounded box sweep, every aim clamped 60 m inside the 2.56 km ground, a `Held:` line ending a leg, a fresh hostile ending the hunt, a kill ending it and never chased); falls back only on its own hull (internals at half, or a hull plate read at a quarter or gone) and never wider than a 450 m ceiling on every band, which is the 480 m a stopped tank on grass is seen at less a margin (`[arena].band_m` 150 to 450); turns a held tank 90, 135 and 180 degrees from the heading it stopped on, 4 s apart, then backs it off; and types `FIRE` on each weapon's own clock (the ack plus the learned cycle plus a slack floored at one 500 ms engaged pulse, the cockpit's `Still cycling: N s to go` read as whole seconds), which took the share of `FIRE` lines refused as still cycling from 38.7 to 47.3 per cent a match in the lead's live smoke to 0 to 9.8 per cent in the re-measure. The optional commander gains a `hunt` stance; a `withdraw` is a hold of 5 to 30 s (10 when absent) counted once per episode, then a 30 s rest, that the pilot keeps only on a failing hull (otherwise flown as `hunt` or `orbit` and recorded as `commander-overruled`) and drops when it lapses or the commander stops for any reason; its picture names its own side and no longer echoes the model's note; an ask older than twice its period is given up; its HTTP timeout is at most 10 s. The report-only ten-match 1 v 1 lane now flies a 420 s match cap (16c `Q-AK3`) under one 100-minute deadline that always prints its figure, and read 2, 4 and 4 of 10 in three runs on 2026-09-30 (4 of 10 selected, against the report-only target of seven; `Q-AK5`); an ignored live commander lane that spends real model money runs only when the person running it sets `MUSH_BOT_LIVE_COMMANDER=1` with a key exported (`Q-AK4`), is run by no CI job, and has not yet been run. The line-mode contract of the AJ entry is unchanged in kind: the bot now also acts on the four `Held:` sentences, `Still cycling: N s to go.`, `{designator} destroyed.` and `Target destroyed.`, and `Power is already online.`, so rewording any of them is a change to that reader. The machines are unchanged; the runs' reading (a one-track tank circling toward its dead side, the fall back a no-op at the default band, a held-then-search loop into one slope, `FIRE` typed for guns lost with the turret) is input for the next bot cut, not for `reflex`.
- **§5, §1 and §3.8 (the external bot turns into the shot)**: as built (16b cut AL, AL.1 to AL.3, 2026-09-30; the owner's standing word of the same day, keep chasing tail items as they appear; 16c `Q-AL1` to `Q-AL4` at their defaults, `Q-AL2`'s pulse-rig bound loosened by the programme lead's ruling of the same day): again no sentence of this document changed and no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved; the AJ entry above still describes the game's side of it, and what changed is the reader, still written from `help simulator`, `help cockpit` and its own transcripts alone. **Fire.** A gun's cycle is the whole seconds a `cycling (N s)` row prints when read under one second after that gun's `Firing {w}.` (the post-shot `WEAPONSTATUS` goes 500 ms after the ack until it is learned); the clock is the ack plus that cycle plus a slack that starts at 300 ms, falls 10 ms a shot to nothing and rises 150 ms on the cockpit's `Still cycling` answer to a marked `FIRE`, to at most one 500 ms engaged pulse (since cut AN, with `[arena] lattice` on and the arena's 250 ms lattice learned, the next lattice point after the ack plus the cycle plus a 175 ms guard, 275 since cut AO, less the median round trip; see that entry), a row bounds it both ways, and a `Still cycling: N s to go.` refusal is tried again 250 ms into its last second; every due gun goes first, main gun first, ahead of the polls that can wait (`STATUS`, the periodic `WEAPONSTATUS`, `BEARING B` and, since cut AN, `VIEW BRIEF`), which keep a slot for a gun due inside the second unless 2 s overdue, and `WEAPONSTATUS` thins to every 8 s once every cycle is known; rounds come down by acks; a gun named in ``That unit carries no weapon called `{w}`.`` or refused `Empty:` is struck for the match, and a section lost with `its weapons are gone` holds every gun until a `WEAPONSTATUS` answers (or `This unit carries no weapon.`). **Nose-on.** The pilot's default engagement is `hold` (`[arena] engagement`, `hold` or `orbit`): stopped at the band with the bow on the hostile, range changed only along the line of fire, ahead to close and in reverse to open, with a 25 m hysteresis against the scope's 50 m rounding; `close` and `hunt` end in it, and the pilot's own fall back and a kept `withdraw` reverse with the bow on; a hit on a side or rear facet turns the bow, stopped, for 4 s toward the nearest live hostile whose row's relative bearing lies in the struck arc (never under `orbit`); with one track gone every turn the pilot makes goes the live way, a dead-side correction of up to 45 degrees is let stand and a larger one is ordered once; a `Held:` stop on steep, impassable or edge ground marks a point the next forward heading is steered round, and a reverse toward a mark becomes a stop. **Seen second.** The pilot stops facing a return not yet told apart (a row read; at most 6 s, a rest as long between, four a match) and waits stopped for 20 s, facing its estimate, for a hunted machine it has not hurt and that is not going away once its estimate is within 700 m, any hit ending a wait; it reads no ground (`Q-AL3`; since cut AN its opening reads `VIEW BRIEF`'s ground, see that entry). The optional commander gains `hold` (a model's `stand_off` still reads as it), is told the help's facet costs and sight rules and each contact's `rel`, and an overruled `withdraw` is flown as the pilot's engagement. The report-only ten-match 1 v 1 lane takes `MUSH_BOT_WIN_RATE_ENGAGEMENT` and prints the fire and shot gaps, the still-cycling count by gun, the stances flown and the hits taken by facet: on 2026-09-30 the hold read 5, 5 and 4 of 10 (4 of 10 selected on the guns' refusals, against the report-only target of seven; `Q-AL4`) with every facet hit taken on a front facet, and a forced orbit 4 of 10 with 47 of its 429 facet hits on a front facet; the machine gun's accepted shots went a median of about 1,500 ms apart against a line-mode floor of about 1,250, and still-cycling refusals stayed above one line in ten. The line-mode contract of the AJ entry is unchanged in kind: the bot now also acts on ``That unit carries no weapon called `{w}`.``, `This unit carries no weapon.`, the `Empty:` refusal, a section lost with `its weapons are gone`, a track's section-lost line, the facet a hit line names and the three ground `Held:` sentences, so rewording any of them is a change to that reader. The machines are unchanged.
- **§5, §1 and §3.8 (the external bot holds up on a real box)**: as built (16b cut AM, AM.1 to AM.3, 2026-10-01; the owner's standing word of 2026-09-30, keep chasing tail items as they appear; 16c `Q-AM1` to `Q-AM3` at their defaults): no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved, and the one sentence of this document the cut made false, the AJ entry's one `SIM LEAVE` on a stop, is amended in place there; what changed is the reader, still written from the help and its own transcripts alone, with no tactic changed and no win rate measured. **Replies on a slow wire.** A `CONTACTS`, `WEAPONSTATUS` or `STATUS` reply is no longer closed by a quiet gap: 150 ms after its last row the rows read so far are merged in and the reply stays open, and it closes (dropping what it did not name, clearing a lock with no lock line, replacing the sections) at the first line after it that is not one of its rows and, for `CONTACTS` and `WEAPONSTATUS`, not chatter, a hint or a receiver notice, before that line's own effect, so an identification, a lock or a hit read after the rows still stands; the lock line heads the first gun row after it across a gap for up to 2 s; a line whose pieces arrive apart is held whole for 2 s. The bot asks for a 120-column PTY, and a line read within 2 s of a column-0 `[channel]` line too wide to have held its first word is that line's continuation, never a game sentence, unless it could be the answer to a line the bot typed and still awaits; a ledger of those lines (the lobby's among them) sends each transient `please retry in a moment.` to the line it answers, so a transient answering a `FIRE` re-arms that gun at once; a gun's cycle is learned only from a row read within a second of its `FIRE`'s send, so on a round trip over about a quarter second it is never learned and the gun fires from its rows. A seeded latency rig (40 ms base delay, up to 300 ms jitter, replies split across writes, chatter or `Receiver clear.` dropped into one reply in three) reads a mean round trip of 821 ms on its seed, where the picture, the lock and the fire clock hold and neither gun's cycle is learned (`Q-AM1`). **The lobby and the REPL.** A stop with a `SIM TEAM` or `SIM START` typed and unanswered waits for that answer, at most `[arena].step_timeout_ms` from its typing, then types one `SIM LEAVE` whatever it learned, none when the answer names the other side's slot (`Q-AM3`); only a stop waits, a blocker or a match's end closing at once; `SIM START` goes only while the bot leads its side at that step; `look` and the walk act only on their own answer; `STARTUP` refused with `Your tank is a wreck` waits for the centre's end word, `That tank is no longer in the match` records the match left, any other refusal is retyped after the step timeout, and in a running match the step never blocks on a timeout; a person's pending command never holds the lane's tick, though `/send` still pauses the bot; while paused the lane types nothing and the lobby reads every line at the pause instant, and `/resume` moves every lobby wait on by the pause's length while the match's own times stay real (`Q-AM2`); a blocked multi-match run reports the blocker's sentence with the tally of matches flown. **What binds the game from now on**, beside the AJ entry's contract: one read reply's rows go out back to back from one outbox with no game line between them (the bot closes a reply at its first other game line; since 17b cut M1b.4 the arming line `  S<n>: …` under a `WEAPONSTATUS` row is one of that reply's rows and closes nothing), a channel line wraps flush and `mush-ansi`'s wrap keeps a run of spaces at a break (the continuation reads the untrimmed width at 120 columns), and the bot now also acts on `STARTUP refused. Your tank is a wreck.` and `That tank is no longer in the match.`, so changing any of these is a change to that reader. The machines are unchanged.
- **§5, §1 and §3.8 (the external bot fights from the muster)**: as built (16b cut AN, AN.1 to AN.3, 2026-10-01; the owner's standing word of 2026-09-30, keep chasing tail items as they appear, and his goal of a bot that beats the blue machine; 16c `Q-AN1` to `Q-AN3` at their defaults, `Q-AN3`'s wingman half not met until cut AO kept the wingman's opening): no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved, and the sentences of this document the cut made false, the AJ entry's typed vocabulary and the AL entry's polls that can wait, fire clock and ground, are amended in place there; what changed is the reader, still written from `help simulator`, `help cockpit` and its own transcripts alone, never `reflex` (`Q-AN1`). **The muster lane.** A second report-only ten-match 1 v 1 lane flies from the shipped muster with no pose on a 600 s cap (`arena_muster_rate`, run only by the manual CI job `bot-muster-rate`) and is the figure of record from AN on, the posed lane kept as the duel benchmark (`Q-AN2`); its reading adds who saw whom first (`SEEN FIRST`), the times from `Power online.` to the first contact and the first hit taken (`OPENING`) and the share of facet hits taken on a front facet (`FRONT SHARE`), and the lead's live commander lane can fly from the muster (`MUSH_BOT_LIVE_FROM_MUSTER=1`). **The opening.** With `[arena] opening = "wait"` (the default; `search` is the old drive for The Crossroads) the bot types `VIEW BRIEF` after its own `Power online.`, takes the muster from its first `STATUS` and the other side's entry as that muster mirrored across the ground (the size from the brief's header, else 2.56 km), steps off a road onto hiding ground (grass, bare ground, rubble or light woods, at most 80 m, never toward the other entry) unless it already stands hidden (since cut AO, with a back-off knob on, it first moves in toward the other entry until about 290 m of passable ground lies behind it; see that entry), and holds stopped with its bow toward the other entry until a hostile is held, a hit lands or six minutes pass; it then fires from the halt at a hostile coming on inside 600 m, the halt ending when it is hit, when the machine reaches the band, when the machine has neither closed nor published a track for 6 s, or after a minute. **The re-approach.** With `[arena] reapproach = true` (the default until cut AO.3's pooled runs shipped it off; see that entry), when a machine that publishes no track out-hits it (at least 8 hits taken, and 4 more than scored, over 20 s) the bot backs off bow on to 650 m of the machine's last plot, for at most 45 s (since cut AO 60 s, slanted off the machine or ended into its wait where the ground behind runs out), waits there 45 s for it to come on and fires from the halt, at most twice a match. **The broken machine.** With `[arena] follow_broken = true` (the default) and the muster known, a hostile hurt or hit 8 times and seen driving away (since cut AO also a track-less one whose range opened 100 m in 10 s, and never on a track over 20 m/s) is followed bow on at 350 m, never past 400 m of the other entry line, then backed from to 650 m of its plot (since cut AO bow on the machine, for at most 65 s, ended into its wait where the ground behind runs out) and waited for 90 s, its return fired on from the halt and the wait ending into the search with its trail dropped; no hunt and no re-approach begins while a follow or a return is in force. **The lattice.** With `[arena] lattice = true` (the default) a gun whose cycle is learned fires on the arena's 250 ms lattice, its phase read from the read times of the arena's own notices (hits, misses, contact notices and `Held:` lines; never an ack) and its clock the next lattice point after the ack plus the cycle plus a 175 ms guard (275 since cut AO, and a ready row never pulling the clock earlier) less the median round trip, off above a 200 ms round trip, and a gun refused twice inside 30 s goes back to the AL clock for 30 s. **Measured** (report-only, local, 2026-10-01): before the opening the muster lane read 4 of 10 with the first contact a median 85 s after power; the three default runs read 7, 5 and 2 of 10 (two tuning moves, neither kept), the first contact a median 158 s after power, every match seen first and every facet hit taken on a front facet; with the re-approach off 8 of 10, since from the muster it backs into the arena's west edge and never opens the range; the posed duel 4 of 10; the lattice did not reach the line-mode floor live (the acceptance edge about 250 ms past the ready instant, past the guard's bound, the machine gun's shots still a median of about 1,500 ms apart). Every run predates AN.3's last fixes, so the shipped pilot is unmeasured live. **What binds the game from now on**, beside the AJ entry's contract: the bot now reads `VIEW BRIEF`'s header sentence, its bearing lines in their four forms with their class words, the two dark-window sentences, the brief's order and its empty sentence (`No contacts.` then; since 16b cut AP.4 `No contacts in the window.`, the brief closing on `End of brief.`, which the reader moved to in that cut), and the board's two-space `mode:` line, and phases its fire from the read times of the arena's notices, so rewording any of them or moving the notices off the 250 ms lattice is a change to that reader; only the header (`arena_contract.rs`) and the rays (an end-to-end from the shipped muster) are pinned against the game. The machines are unchanged.
- **§5, §1 and §3.8 (the external bot keeps its back off the wall)**: as built (16b cut AO, AO.1 to AO.3, 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-02; the owner's standing word of 2026-09-30, keep chasing tail items as they appear, and his goal of a bot that beats the blue machine; 16c `Q-AO1` to `Q-AO3` at their defaults): no server, arena, pack, gateway, `web/` or harness source moved, and the sentences of this document the cut made false, the AL and AN entries' lattice guard and the AN entry's opening, re-approach, broken machine and wingman, are amended in place there; what changed is the reader, still written from `help simulator`, `help cockpit` and its own transcripts alone, never `reflex`. **The opening's room** (`Q-AO3`). With `[arena] opening = "wait"` and a back-off knob on (`reapproach` or `follow_broken`), the bot reads from `VIEW BRIEF`'s ground how much passable ground (road, grass, bare ground, rubble, light woods or water; any other class word it takes as blocking) lies behind it along the line between the two entries; short of 290 m (the back-off from the machine's 420 m to 650 m plus a 60 m margin, a shortfall of 30 m taken as enough) it drives in toward the other side's entry, stops, and reads a further brief, at most three legs, before the AN entry's step off the road; with no way in it says so once and steps or holds as before. A teammate (a contact the stream names as the own side, or an unidentified one within 100 m) no longer ends a wingman's opening. **The back-offs.** The re-approach's back-off and the return's slant the bow up to 30 degrees off the machine, in 10 degree steps, to leave 30 m of ground astern, and end at once into their own wait (`No room behind: …`) when no slant does, on a `Held:` read while reversing, or at held ground; their bounds are 60 s and 65 s, the drive the help's 27 kph astern on grass needs.
