The return keeps its bow on the machine and moves its plot only farther. **The broken machine.** A hurt machine leaving with no track is judged broken when its range opened 100 m in 10 s, a track over 20 m/s is ignored, a machine already inside the keep-out begins the return and no follow, and a followed row is flown to 6 s old under every doctrine. **The lattice.** The guard is 275 ms (tunable 150 to 300), about a lattice step past the instant the clock calls ready, and a ready row never pulls a lattice clock earlier. **The lobby and the REPL.** A pause that spans a match's boarding window and its start loses that match: the game drops a slot never boarded and answers the late `BOARD` `You hold no slot in the simulator.`, so the bot says `Match {n} started without us`, records the match left and claims the next; a running match is never given up on forty busy `STARTUP` answers (it is typed again after the step timeout); the runner ticks a paused lane, which reads and settles with the commander silent; the walk ignores a foreign room's late description; and the resume sentence says the lobby's waits go on where they stood. The optional commander's policy says the hold has room behind it and a back-off ends where the ground behind runs out. **The pooled measure** (`Q-AO1`, `Q-AO2`). Every win-rate figure of the two lanes prints a 95 per cent Wilson interval, each run keeps the knobs it flew beside its transcript, and an ignored pool lane (`MUSH_BOT_WIN_RATE_POOL`) reads kept runs together and refuses runs flown with different knobs. On 2026-10-02 (report-only, local) three default runs of one build read 2, 4 and 6 of 10, pooled 12 of 30 (25 to 58 per cent); the re-approach off 3 of 10 and the lattice off 3 of 10 (11 to 60 each), no difference called at thirty against ten (about a 35-point detection limit). The re-approach reached its wait in 11 of 31 back-offs, short of the half its own line had to show, so `[arena] reapproach` ships **false** and stays a key; the lattice refused 6.9 per cent of `FIRE` lines against 15.9 off at an equal machine-gun shot gap of 1,500 ms, so `[arena] lattice` stays on. The sixth run, at the resulting default, read 3 of 10, and the posed duel 8 of 10. The move in ran in every match and every match was seen first with every facet hit taken on a front facet, but the first contact came a median 137 s after power against AN's 158 s. **What binds the game from now on**, beside the AJ and AN entries' contract: the bot now sorts the brief's class words into passable and blocking ground, reads `VIEW BRIEF` again mid-opening, reads a designator from a `New contact` notice, and acts on `You hold no slot in the simulator.` in a running match, so rewording any of them is a change to that reader. The machines are unchanged.
- **§3.7 `FIRE` and `WEAPONSTATUS`, §3.1 "The seam" and §5 (the aged mirror)**: as built (16b cut AP.1, 2026-10-02; 16c `B-86`): the cockpit's mirror of a weapon's cycle is aged. `arena_bridge::CockpitFrame` carries `arena_ms` (the `Frames` message's `now_ms`, the arena's clock after the step that built the frame) and `received` (the world's `std::time::Instant` when `on_frames` stored it, one read per message), and `cockpit::aged_weapons(held, now, floored)` gives every row `remaining = max(0, ready_in_ms − the wall milliseconds since receipt)` with `ready = ready or remaining == 0`, read by `fire()`'s cycling pre-check and by `WEAPONSTATUS` alone through `carried`, so a `FIRE` typed once the gun is truly ready is forwarded in the unchanged sentence (`Firing cannon.`; above nought still `FIRE cannon refused. Still cycling: N s to go.`, refuse-not-clamp) and `WEAPONSTATUS` counts `cannon  cycling (2 s)` down to `cannon  ready` between frames. The aging is skipped while a staff `@arena/tempo` floor stands and for a frame built under a floor since lifted (`MatchMirror::floor_lifted_at_ms`, the mirror's `now_ms` at the lift, read against the frame's `arena_ms`), because under a floor the wall runs ahead of the arena. What the forward buys is the end of the local refusals and the retype and nothing more: an intent never pulls the arena task's wake in, the arena takes the `FIRE` at its next wake (the `WeaponReady` wake when the line arrives before it, the wake after when it does not) and no cadence is claimed; the arena's own `Still cycling` can still meet a `FIRE` forwarded inside the OS timer's overshoot or under overload's catch-up-capped pulses. Nothing under `arena/` moved, no hash, no protocol (`FRAME_VERSION` 1), no sentence, no help: the keyframe payload keeps the frame's own `ready` and `ready_in_ms`, and neither the TUI nor the web counts down, so a panel pilot can read `ready` on the line beside the frame's seconds in the pane until the ready frame (16c `Q59`, default not built). Whether a ready `FIRE` should pull the wake in is 16c `Q58`, default not built.
- **§1 (the story's ending), §3.8 (a side with no gun left), §4 and §7 (the cap)**: as built (16b cut AP.2, 2026-10-02; 16c `B-87`, and the owner's rulings of the same day under `Q47` and `Q57`): a side with an in-action unit that was armed at check-out and no in-action unit still carrying a weapon is eliminated, the clause a pure read in `Arena::eliminated` beside AE.3's drone clause (`armed_in_action` and `carries_gun` filled in the same walk, one `retain` after AE.3's), settled at the end of the substep the disarming impact fell in (`settle_elimination` stamps `now_ms` under W.2's rule) and folded into no hash. It is keyed on `weapons.is_empty()` and `armed_at_checkout()`, never on ammunition, so an empty magazine is not a lost gun and Q.7's `Spent` draw stands; both sides disarmed at once is the elimination draw (`draw (both sides eliminated)`, settled before `settle_spent`); there is no "while the other side still carries one" test, so against a side that never had a gun the armed side that loses its last one is out; a gunless tank beside a never-armed drone is out and a disarmed tank beside an armed teammate stands. By D-16-5 the pilot's side reads the same way: a one-tank side loses the hit that takes its turret, still able to drive, so the A1 duel now ends at the turret as well as at the hull (§1 amended; the story test, its split twin and the rematch test accept either ending, the elimination asserted and whichever section went). `out_of_action`, `settle_disabled`, `spent`, `is_dry`, the Withdraw arms and `UnitOutcome` are untouched, so a disarmed, driving loser still records `intact` and the debrief reads `lost 0 of 1` beside `eliminated` (16c `T-101`). `[tacsim.match].max_duration_ms` ships at 900,000 at every default site (`MatchConfig::default`, `TacsimMatchCfg::default` and its head table, `mushd.toml.example`, `deploy/instance/mushd.toml.tmpl`), the arena being an explicit OOC proving ground; `MatchConfig::validate` floors it at 60,000 and sets no ceiling while `MAX_READY_AT_MS`, `MAX_HOLD_MS` and `MAX_CYCLE_MS` stay hour-sized definition ceilings, so a Hold authored at or past the cap can never settle (16c `T-100`); `Q47`'s deadline tie-break is not built. `help simulator` states the cap and quotes the board's own end words (`ended — red`, `ended — timeout`; `SIM HISTORY`'s `red won (blue eliminated)` and `timed out (the clock ran out)`), no board arm added, and one `help cockpit` sentence says a tank that lost its guns still drives and that the last gun on a side ends the match. A live world's `mushd.toml` is the operator's and keeps 3600000 until edited.
- **§3.7, §5 and §3.6 (`WATCH`: the line-mode digest)**: as built (16b cut AP.3, 2026-10-02; 16c `N19` at its defaults, world-side and off until typed): `WATCH ON`, `WATCH OFF` and bare `WATCH` are the fifteenth cockpit selector (seventeen verbs with `BOARD` and `EXIT`; `COCKPIT_VERBS` 15, `watch` after `emcon`, registered by prefix like the others so an English verb of that name is shadowed game-wide, F.4's warning in its stanza), behind the one seat gate (`You are not at the station of a deployed unit.`), staging no intent and never refused on a wreck; the word is trimmed and case-folded, and any other word answers the one usage line. The switch is two RAM rows on `ArenaRegistry` beside the staff `watchers` (`line_watch`, `last_watch_block`), cleared with the seat (`unbind_pilot`, `untrack`), so it is off again when the pilot next boards. While it is on, `on_frames` pushes after the keyframe and before the AH.1 hints ONE `Sys` tagged `arena-notice` (so the TUI's cockpit ring and the web's notices list leave it out and it lands in their scrollback) of one to three lines joined by newlines from the pure `watch_lines(frame, ranges, weapons, pass_ms)`: `Watch RA: online, 24 kph, heading 090°[, HELD][, DESTROYED]` always; `Watch guns: cannon ready, machine_gun (mg) cycling (3 s); lock BA, 450 m, 46% to hit` (`empty` for a gun with no round, `; no lock` with none) only while the tank carries a gun; `Watch contacts: BA blue 087° far 450 m; X3 unknown 315° effective 200 m, plotted 4 s ago` only while the platform holds a contact, a plot's age named only once stale; each line within 120 columns with a `; +N more` tail (the guns line keeping its lock after the tail), no tempo word (`T-59`), no position, designators and the pilot's own numbers only, so the observer rule holds by construction and is asserted in `observer_rule.rs` (no `Watch contacts:` before detection, designators only after, the gate's one line for a bystander). The block goes out only when its text differs from the last one pushed to that pilot, so a moved cell, a tempo flip or a fresh plot's whole second pushes nothing and the push rate reads out nothing of the arena's tempo; the count against `Q-U2`'s 512 is at most one extra event per watching pilot per changed block, and nothing is built per pulse for a pilot who is not watching. `WATCH ON` says the held frame's block at once on AP.1's aged rows, so it agrees with `WEAPONSTATUS` typed the same instant (the pushed block reads the frame as sent). The bot reads every such line as neutral (`Line::Other`, pinned in `arena_contract.rs`; no reader change). `help cockpit` opens at fifteen verbs with a `WATCH` row and the section `Watching from line mode`. No wire shape, no new tag, no state slot, no hash.
- **§5 and §3.1 "The seam" (reply framing: `OUTPUTPREFIX` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX`, and the brief's and the pose's words)**: as built (16b cut AP.4, 2026-10-02; 16c `N20` at its defaults, opt-in per connection): PennMUSH-style brackets as transient World state, `OutputBrackets { prefix, suffix }` keyed by `ConnId` beside `live_conns` (never persisted; cleared at ConnDown and by a re-used id's ConnUp; a hot restart of the server half leaves none, because `ReAttachRec` carries no row: 16c `T-102`), with `dispatching_conn` set by the engine for a handler's duration. `Engine::step` pushes, round every non-empty typed line's WHOLE step, the prefix before the body as a `BestEffort` conn-addressed `Sys` tagged `output-prefix` (first on the wire on every outcome) and the suffix after `drain_deferred` as the new `DeliveryClass::Trailer` tagged `output-suffix`, released with the step's last batch on every outcome (with the step on a `NoWrite`, after the ack on a committing step whether it committed or aborted), so on a committing line the wire reads prefix, the `BestEffort` lines, the ack's `OnCommit` lines, suffix, on an aborted write prefix, the `OnAbort` refusal, suffix, and `Huh?`, the drained `Queued.`, a `respond` answer and a line B.5 deferred (bracketed in its own turn) all sit inside; every unsolicited notice lands outside, except that a notice from another step can land inside the pair of a line still saving, and a world-task refusal of a line held while that connection's earlier line is saving nests its whole pair inside the open one (the help tells scripts to pair the brackets as a stack). The world task brackets its own pre-engine refusals (`CommitPending`, `CommitTimedOut`, `StoreUnavailable`, `NotReady`, `IntegrityFrozen`) from the same row in one transmit to `SendTarget::Conn`, so a bracketed refusal reaches the typing connection alone where the bare one fanned out to every connection of the actor; the lines the gateway or the SSH front end answers itself (`@tui`, `@notui`, `QUIT`, `@history`, the `+act` family; the front door's too-fast, too-long, busy and restarting refusals) carry no brackets, and the local staff and async store ops answer after theirs. `OUTPUTPREFIX [<text>]` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX [<text>]` are two silent core commands registered exact-only through `Registry::register_exact` (typed in full, case-folded; a private `Entry.exact` the prefix scan skips, so `o` stays `ORDER`'s and `out` an exit's; `CommandCompatibility::ReadOnly`), bare clears, at most 256 printable characters, refusing `… refused. That is a setting of a connection, and this line came from none.` and `… refused. The text is at most 256 printable characters.`; the two words are on `STORE_READ_ONLY_ALLOWED`; `help macros` gains `Framing replies for a client script`. **The riders, a declared reader change** (this entry supersedes the AG.1 entry's `No contacts.`, `12 + C`, twelve and 23, sixteen-line fixture and off-window closing line, and the AN entry's `its No contacts.`, each amended in place above): `VIEW BRIEF` prints `No contacts in the window.` in its empty contacts slot (`CONTACTS` keeps `No contacts.`) and always ends `End of brief.`, at most `13 + C` lines, the fixture at seventeen; `@arena/pose` refuses as `POSE refused. {reason}` through `CmdError`'s own `Display`, the usage and `Unavailable` included, successes unchanged. The bot gains `Line::NoContactsInWindow` and `Line::BriefEnd` (the brief closes on the end line and completes the ground; `brief_tail`'s closing role retired), its lane, offline scripted server and `POSE_REFUSALS` move with them, and `arena_contract.rs` pins both constants; `observer_rule.rs`'s `BRIEF_WORDS` gains `End`, `brief` and `in`. The bot's reply-inference lane is retired in a bot cut after AP, not here. No `mush-ansi`, `mush-session`, `mush-net-ssh`, gateway, web code, pack, hash, slot or arena change.
- **§6, §3.4 step 4, §3.1 "Durability" and the I.3, Q.7, E.4, AI.1 and AF.1 entries above (the motor comes out of the reflex)**: as built (17b cut M1a.1, 2026-10-03, `10a5a0ec`; doc 17 §4, D-UM-3, the first cut of the unit-minds programme): the reflex controller's driving half is its own file, `arena/motor.rs`: a `MotorRow { goal, route, searched_ms, searched_from, avoid, refused, waypoint, from_waypoint, hold_retry_ms }` per hull of any controller in `Arena.motors: MotorTable` (the rows, the path budget window and spent, the A\* scratch), `MotorRow::age_avoid` (the avoid TTL sweep lifted from the head of `decide`), `follow` over the table under a policy — `Fallback::Waypoint` for the machine (the E.4 give-ups, the nearest-waypoint fallback, the Hold retry clock as before) and `Fallback::Notice` for a hull nobody's `decide` steers (the goal cleared and the hull told; a pilot's `DRIVE` since M1a.4) — and the kernels `step_is_drivable`, `first_step_is_open`, `line_is_open`, `walk_is_open`, `entry_cell`, `far_entry_cell`, `stand_off_cell`, `patrol_step`, `hold_arrived` and `fall_back_to_waypoint` `pub` as an audited surface (hold the plot and move the truth; the pickers read the ground and the own pose; the walks read terrain and the avoid list and never the index; a stop is learned at the `blocked` event; a pilot row ages its stops). The decision half (`ReflexUnit`, `decide`, the utility score, the clocks) stays in `reflex.rs` unchanged and is T0 of doc 17 §17 until M4; `set_controller(Pilot)` releases both rows through `Arena::release_reflex`, and the motor's readers (`hold_routeless`, `note_blocked`, `refresh_held_avoid`) gate on the row where they gated on the controller. The I.3 entry above reads with `MotorTable::forget_route` for `ReflexMemory::forget_route` and the AI.1 entry with `MotorRow.searched_from` and `MotorRow.refused` for `ReflexUnit`'s; nothing those entries describe behaves differently. The `state_hash` folds a reflex row's driving fields back out of the table in the old byte order, pinned by two constants read at HEAD before the move (the 1,000 ms replay's first and last hash, the shipped two-a-side duel's final hash, re-asserted at every later sub-cut of the cut) and a per-row section for motor rows with no reflex row; `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 2 → 3 with a `motors` section (AF.1's structural pin re-blessed at 392 lines; a format-2 document is refused by name at resume and the match voided, the owner deploying between matches). No sentence of §6 changed; the harness's deterministic block is equal and the lenses held.
- **§3.6, §3.7 and §5 (the row formatter moves into the arena library)**: as built (17b cut M1a.2, 2026-10-03, `b446b918`; doc 17 §3, D-UM-4): no sentence of this document changed and no fixture, golden, help page or sentence moved (`git diff --exit-code` on all three, exit 0). `frame_contacts`, `frame_contacts_held`, `range_m_rounded`, `held_range_m`, `settle_ranges`, `own_scope`, `contact_band`, `ContactRow` with `render` and `glyph`, `frame_lock`, `lock_designator`, `fired_by_default` and the four glyphs live in `arena/view.rs`, pure over a `Frame` and a range memory, and `match_/cockpit.rs` re-exports them so every caller (the bridge, the brief, `WATCH`, the bot's dev-dependency read) resolves unchanged; `contact_sentence` stays the cockpit's. The `CONTACTS` rows and the brief's sentences are pinned byte for byte in two halves, and the observer-name gate scans `view.rs` as a third text.
- **§3.6 "What a pilot sees", §3.7 `CONTACTS`, §5 and §4 (the frame grows for a reader that is not a person)**: as built (17b cut M1a.3, 2026-10-03, `27e3137a`; doc 17 §3, D-UM-5; `FRAME_VERSION` 1 → 2, a wire version and not a pack: a deploy needs the gateway restart and a fresh `web/dist`, and an older client against the new server prints the old row and drops nothing): every contact row carries its **aspect**, the word `facet_band_seen` gives off the plot (`front`, `left`, `right`, `rear` on the keyframe; `front`, `side`, `rear` in the row), on every hull; on a hull with a weapons station each row also carries `reads_dead` (the own gunner's kill, or a held reading showing destroyed a section the target chassis's definition marks fatal — the definition's mask, never the live wreck flag, and value-identical to the reflex's `contact_reads_dead` wherever a reading is held) and the fatal section words are published beside a held reading on every hull; **odds** per weapon against the two nearest classified contacts (`STOCK_ODDS_ROWS = 2`; the default weapon's entry first, so every client quotes one gun), resolved by `resolve_seen` through the new `Arena::aim_at` (the lock's aim generalised to any held contact, one trace to the plot cell), never for an unclassified contact and never on an unarmed hull; the standing **order** of the hull's squad as `FrameOrder { squad, objective, hold_fire, max_range_band, expires_ms }`, its contacts re-keyed at write time to the hull's own HELD rows or none (`null` on every piloted frame today); and **`t_ms`**, the arena's clock after the step that built the frame. The keyframe drops `weapons`, `odds` and `reads_dead` on a hull with no weapons station and gains `bands_cm` per weapon and the order's payloads; `Frame::same_as` compares odds at the printed whole percent and never `t_ms`, so the authored lane's `frames_per_pulse_mean` moved 18.867 → 18.875 and was re-blessed once (the only baseline field that moved). The `CONTACTS` row ends `  side  cannon 62%` on an armed hull with odds, `  side  -` where the fire control is not resolving the contact, and `  side` on an unarmed hull; the TUI's contacts pane carries the compact ` side 62%` / ` side` tail that fits its 46 columns at the 120-column floor (an `effective` row at a three-digit range that is blue, nose-on and armed, or carries a two-digit age, overflows by one column and wraps as scan lines do: a 16c residual, a narrower tail the lead's call); the web decoder and rows and the bot's `contact_row` and `arena_contract.rs` moved as a declared reader change (the tail optional for an older server); `help cockpit`'s Seeing paragraph says it; `view_brief.txt`, `contact_sentence` and `BRIEF_WORDS` untouched. Open for the lead: Q2 — `frame_odds` takes any classified contact nearest-first, so two wingmen nearer than the locked hostile take both rows and the locked row prints `-` while `LOCK` quotes a percentage (restrict to hostiles, or seat the locked contact first); `rekey_order` keeps a held but unclassified own row. 16c `T-90` is touched by `t_ms` and not closed (`STATUS` prints no clock, `RANGE` no step).
- **§3.7 (the verb table), §3.4 step 4, §3.1 "Pulse phases" steps 2 and 3, §3.6 and §5 (`DRIVE`: the pilot's helm handed to the hull's own drive-by-wire)**: as built (17b cut M1a.4, 2026-10-03, `7e45b9b1`; doc 17 §4 and D-UM-10 with the word changed: the verb is **`DRIVE`**, since `mush-core`'s baseline `goto` with aliases `go` and `move` holds the name and the registry refuses a collision; the plan's Q1 at its default): `DRIVE <spot|x,y|HOLD|OFF>` is the sixteenth cockpit selector (`COCKPIT_VERBS` 16, `drive` after `watch`, registered by prefix like the others, so `dr`, core `drop`'s former shortest prefix, is `Ambiguous(drive, drop)` game-wide while `dro` is still `drop` and `dri` is `DRIVE`; pinned and declared in its stanza, the baseline `goto` stanza untouched). A key spot by whole name or letter as `RANGE` resolves one, or an arena cell `x,y`, case-folded, stages one **continuous** `IntentKind::Goal(MotorGoal::Cell)` on the helm, counted on the seat's per-unit rate as the discrete orders are (`[tacsim.arena.intents].max_pending_per_unit`'s meaning widened by doc alone; a machine's or a mind's goal is never counted); the replies are `Steering for The Ford (A).` or `Steering for cell 12,34.`, `Holding here.` for `DRIVE HOLD` (a stop with no goal and no notice) and `DRIVE off; the helm is yours.` for `DRIVE OFF`; a destroyed, immobile or cold tank is refused in `HEADING`'s words, any other word gets the one byte-identical `DRIVE refused. That is not a spot, a cell, HOLD or OFF.` (a contact's designator included — nothing of any contact is read), a cell off the ground `DRIVE refused. That cell is not on the window.`, and the bare verb the usage line `DRIVE <spot|x,y|HOLD|OFF>; STATUS names the nearest spot.`. At the landing the goal is fanned as AI.1's hunt fan fans a machine's (a motor rule now, when the cell lies in another side's entry zone within `HUNT_FAN_ROWS` of a same-side goal) and driven once; then `DueKind::MotorTick` drives it every `decide_ms` on the stagger grid while the goal stands (the same bounded A\*, the same avoid list and refusal memory, the circle and the shoulder of AI.1), under `Fallback::Notice`: arrival within `ARRIVE_CELLS` clears the goal, stops the tank and says `DRIVE: arrived.` once; no route (the search over budget, no path, or a hull that cannot close under a standing goal) clears it, stops and says `DRIVE: no route from here; holding.` once — two own-platform notice kinds, `drive_arrived` and `drive_no_route`, pinned in `notice_lines.json` for the TUI and web rings and read by the bot as neutral lines. A `HEADING` or `SPEED` typed afterward disengages the goal silently (the motor's own orders never do), and within one pulse the pre-pulse buffer keeps whichever of a `DRIVE` and a `HEADING`/`SPEED` the pilot typed last (settled by variant: a cell against both, `HOLD` against speed alone, `OFF` against nothing). The mandatory cross-tempo fixture drives a seated pilot's tank at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms to one event list, one hash sequence and one final cell; the observer battery proves Ada's `DRIVE BA` the same bytes before and after detection. `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 3 → 4 for the new due kind (a format-3 snapshot refused at resume). Residuals: a wreck's motor row keeps its last goal and `fan_goal` reads it without a wreck check; `DRIVE_OFF_WINDOW` says `window` for the whole ground; a goal written onto a `Controller::Reflex` hull is kept by `Withdraw` as its route home (unreachable from any seat).
- **§3.1 "Pulse phases" steps 3 and 5 (the hash), §3.7 (a third controller), §6 (the deliberation layer's `Order` lands on a mind too)**: as built (17b cut M1a.5, 2026-10-03, `6164bc07`; doc 17 §§3, 4, 6, D-UM-2, D-UM-8, D-UM-9, D-UM-11, D-UM-20, D-UM-21, D-UM-47; the programme lead's note: no Rhai, no `mush-mind` crate, production stays on `Controller::Reflex`): `Controller::Mind` is the third controller (hash code 2; `pilot` 0 and `reflex` 1 unchanged; the observer's `n` flag beside `m` — retired by 17b cut M4.3: one flag, `m`, for either machine), set by no production path and existing in fixtures and the twin lane alone until M4 (the production fill from 17b cut M4.3). A mind thinks on `DueKind::MindThink` at the lattice millisecond `grid_at(unit, STOCK_THINK_MS = 1,000)` and lands on `DueKind::MindLand` one substep later (`t_ms + substep_len_ms()`, 250 ms on shipped content); the due loop holds a millisecond's mind kinds and runs them **last**, landings before thinks, in unit-id order, independent of `seq`, so step 3(c)'s order gains that tail. `MindState { think_seq, pending, memory bytes, notices (16, drained per think), ranges, order, strikes }` is hashed per row with no count prefix (step 5's hash gains the `minds` section after the motor rows) and carried by the snapshot (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 4 → 5; AF.1's pin re-blessed at 393 lines); the notice buffer is filled by `stage_notice` at staging time, so a think can read a notice staged earlier in its own millisecond. `Arena::mind_view` builds a mind's view from the one `frame_for` a pilot's keyframe is built from, projected at the keyframe's resolution and truncated to the twelve nearest contacts (since 17b cut M4.5c the lock and the standing order's contact seated, then every contact not known friendly, then the friendly ones, the kept rows still read nearest-first: doc 17 §3 as amended 2026-10-09 by the owner's two rulings): a mind reads what a pilot reads and nothing finer. `apply_machine_intents` is `Arena::order`'s body factored out for both machines, answering `Applied` or `Refused` per intent with every resolver's refusal folded in and an Emcon arm calling `resolve_emcon`; a think is atomic (more than sixteen intents lands nothing and keeps memory); `IntentSource::Mind` is refused `ORDER`, `RELEASE` and `PLACE` with `Channel` before any other gate, refused `Channel` on the fire channel of a hull with no weapons station, never counted on the seat window, and still meets the `Linkdead` gate (a lead decision before M4); `COCKPIT_CHANNEL_IDS = ["helm", "sensors", "fire"]` is the written-down mapping between the arena's `Channel` and the seat's `ChannelId`s. §6's `ORDER` path lands on a mind as on a machine: the `Order` arm writes a `Mind` hull's `FrameOrder` re-keyed to its own held rows, `RELEASE` and the ten-minute expiry journal `order_released`/`order_expired` for both, and a mind's `squads_of` membership was cut L's (a mind joined no squad until 17b cut L.2, which deals either machine controller into squads: the L entries below). `set_controller` releases the outgoing controller's rows and due kinds on every transition — which moves the production `state_hash` on one reachable path, a pilot's `DRIVE` followed by a `Released` control (`EXIT`, or a linkdead grace running out: the row and its tick go), pinned by its own direct and cross-tempo test; every other production lane's hash is unmoved and pinned. The scripted two-mind cross-tempo fixture holds one event list, one hash sequence and one job sequence at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms. `rhai` joined the arena library's forbidden substrings.
- **§6 (the native stock mind beside the reflex, and the divergences doc 17 §17 asks this document to hold)**: as built (17b cut M1a.6, 2026-10-03, `35af96dc`; doc 17 §§8, 17, 18, D-UM-30, D-UM-48): `NativeStockMind` (T1) is the reflex's `decide` re-expressed arm for arm over the mind's view and a canonical memory (`BTreeMap<SmolStr, serde_json::Value>`, keys at most 24 characters, at most 32 of them, values int, bool, null, a string of at most 32 characters or an array of at most 32 ints, encoded as sorted JSON so M2's Rhai memory encodes to the same bytes; fourteen stock keys), emitting `Startup`, `Goal`, `Heading`, `Speed`, `Lock`, `Fire` in that order with the fire half absent on an unarmed hull; the kernels (`best_hostile` with a `ContactId` tie-break and aspect as exposure, `line_to` the plot cell, the nav pickers, the fallback, the reach readers) are free functions both T0 and T1 call; `default_mind_runner` answers it. Its fire rule reads a gun ready at the landing millisecond as ready (`ready_in_ms <= lands_ms − t_ms`, M.2's rule). **Gate B** (`tests/twin.rs`, release): 30 pooled seeded headless matches on the proving ground, 15 seeds with the sides swapped, two stock tanks a side to the 900 s cap — T1 13, T0 17, draws 0, Wilson 95 per cent [0.274, 0.608] containing a half, beside a T0-against-T0 control pool of the same 30 (red 26, blue 2, draws 2; the control is red-biased, so the interval tests one side weakly); shots a minute T1 25.036, T0 24.749, control 25.217; `view_us` p50 4 / p99 16, `think_us` p50 3 / p99 10 on a sampled clone. **The named divergences of T1 from T0** (doc 17 §17's six, counted as thirteen instrumented counters on the live arena after each think's pulse, with one more no counter sees; first divergence at 101,377 ms): `tiebreak` (the `ObjId` tie-break → `ContactId`) 43; `line` (`reflex_has_line` to the true pose → `line_to` the plot cell) 0; `support` (the charge's own row → the charge's held `ContactId` or none) 0, a self-charged `Support` reaching T1 as `Support { of: None }` where T0 fights on doctrine (until 17b cut L.2, which re-keys a self-charge for a Mind hull to `Engage { target }` through the Engage arm's resolution: the L entries below); `reads_dead` 0 (value-identical by construction); `landing` (the think at the lattice and the landing one substep later, against the staggered `ReflexTick`) 10,436, the dominant one; `no_line` 0; `fan` (AI.1's hunt fan as a motor rule at the landing) 11; `order_hint` 0 and `order_end` 0 over no issued order, so upper bounds; `range_round` 16 and `deg_round` 0 (the view at the keyframe's resolution); `plot_cell` 28 (T1 keys its patrol and its waypoint fallback on the plot cell and its own object number); `fallback` 0 (T0's give-ups performed one think later off `drive_no_route`); and a fourteenth: a turret-killed T1 hull reads unarmed rather than dry and patrols and hunts where T0 withdraws. T1's Hold-mode arms (§3.8, 16b cut O.1) run only under unit tests, because the view carries no match mode; a hull whose `engage_cm` is under one 50 m range step never enters Engage under T1 (shipped content clear). Nothing a pilot reads changed; the harness lanes held their lenses with the deterministic block unmoved.
- **§2 W7, §6 "Deliberation", §3.7 `ORDER` and §4 (the principal: who an order is by)**: as built (17b cut L.1, 2026-10-04, `4d99b788`; doc 17 §§1, 3, 4, D-UM-52, D-UM-53 and the owner's ruling R-C: a mind never orders, only a character principal does, and W7's deliberation is an NPC lance-leader giving orders under their own warrant through the one path a pilot's `ORDER` takes): `Order` gains `by: CharacterRef` — `Character { id }` an actor object, `Persona { squad }` an OOC sim persona named `red lance leader 1` — required on the wire, a record field read by nothing that steers and never folded into `state_hash` (the production hash did not move; M1a.1's pins re-asserted), while `issuer` keeps its meaning (the hint scope) and its fold. The match record mints one sim persona per squad `squads_of` deals (`MatchRecord.leaders`, a defaulted field under `LIVE_FORMAT` 1; a piloted hull is in no squad, so these are the fill squads), the lane names it on `SquadBrief.leader` (never in the prompt) and the reply's order carries it; deliberation is requested per leader, a squad with no leader row spends no slot and a reply whose `by` is not its leader is dropped and counted; a reply the bridge cannot decode now gives its in-flight slot back (one of eight leaked before). **The admission**: `admit_order` runs first in `send_order`, so the cockpit's `ORDER`, the reply path and any direct caller meet one wall, `command_of`'s rule (16c Q38 as Y.1 built it) and nothing weaker: a character must be the driver at a seat `flying` resolves and be granted that squad for that unit (a lead any roster of the side that holds the unit; anyone else the wingman unit it names); a persona must be the record's leader of that squad with `issuer` `None` and the unit on that squad's roster; otherwise `IntentRefusal::NoCharacter` (`no character entitled to order that squad gave that order`), which no pilot can reach — the Y.1 entry's `ORDER` refusals are unchanged and byte-identical, a typed order's acknowledgement is byte-identical, and the order now carries the seat's character. `OrderWatch` carries `by` beside `issuer` (`ArenaRegistry::order_author`), and the shield (`human_holds`) is unchanged in body: a live order by a character other than the squad's lance-leader, today exactly a seated pilot's. `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 5 → 6 (`by` on every carried standing order; AF.1's pin unmoved at 393 lines and the same digest, since the standing order sits outside it; a format-5 document is refused by name at resume and the match voided, the owner deploying between matches). The lance lane scripts one lead `Character` per side in `by` with the W8 issuers unchanged; no sentence, keyframe, verb, slot, pack or draw label moved. Declared edges: a reply requested before the leader's hull was wrecked still passes the record-only persona admission and lands on the survivors; the watches are registry RAM and an AF.3 resume drops them while a standing order's `by` survives in the snapshot; the `ordered` tape line does not carry `by`, so the debrief cannot name the orderer (16c candidate).
- **§6 "Provider failure" (the lapse), §5 and §3.6 (one line-mode sentence), `@arena/status`, §6 "The reflex controller" (a Mind hull in a squad) and the Z.2 entry's `SUPPORT` (the self-charge on a mind)**: as built (17b cut L.2, 2026-10-04, `915fc415`; doc 17 §8, D-UM-15, D-UM-32, D-UM-49, D-UM-53): the "mind link dropped" sentence is gone (`MIND_LINK_DROPPED` deleted; `help cockpit`, `docs/ops.md` and the `state-slot-owners.toml` reason made true) and `say_expired_orders` speaks from the watch's `by` in two shapes, each watch once: a persona's `Squad red-1: red lance leader 1's hold order has run out; back on doctrine.` to every pilot of the squad's own side, never the other side, and to staff behind `Arena match {id}: `; a pilot's `Squad red-1: your hold order has run out; back on doctrine.` (*each `… back on their minds.` since 17b cut M5.6, §11*) to that pilot alone, with the character's name on the staff line (`its leader's` once the object is gone) — a declared narrowing: N.2's sentence told the whole side of a pilot's lapse where Q38's no-radio rule never told them of the order. The bot reads both as neutral lines (`Line::Other`, pinned in `arena_contract.rs`; no reader change). `@arena/status` prints `  orders: N by pilots, M by lance leaders` (was `by the machines`) and `  leaders: 2 sim personas[, red-1 Ada]` or `  leaders: none`, staff-only. **The squad holds either machine controller**: `Controller::is_machine` (`Reflex` or `Mind`) through the one builder `CustodyUnit::squad_input` at both `squads_of` callers, so a `Mind` hull is dealt into a squad under a leader and an `ORDER` reaches it through the unchanged path (the M1a.5 entry's "a mind joins no squad" ended), while the engine reads no mind memory (D-UM-49): a Mind member's signal state is `Startup` until doc 17's M4, so a leader's brief for a Mind squad has no `doing:` histogram; production fields no Mind hull before M4, so nothing a pilot reads changed and the harness's deterministic block is unmoved. **The self-charge**: `ORDER red-1 SUPPORT <own callsign>` landing on a Mind hull re-keys to `Engage { target }` through the Engage arm's resolution (the hint in the issuer's or the commander's scope, then the hull's own held row or none), so T1 charges as T0 does where the M1a.6 entry's blind supporter stood; its `order_expired` line journals `engage` where a reflex hull's and both hulls' `ordered` line say `support` (declared, untested); the twin's shadow gains the arm and its figures are unchanged. The mandatory cross-tempo fixture: three Mind hulls as one squad under a persona-led `Engage` with the hint in the commander's scope, one event list and one hash sequence at 5,000, 1,000 and 500 ms. Declared and not changed: the watch is per squad and latest-wins (16c Q43), so a pilot whose watch a teammate's later `ORDER` overwrote is never told their own order lapsed and since L.2 no longer hears the later issuer's lapse either, and a wingman's lapse is prefixed `Squad red-1:`; a per-unit watch stays the 16c candidate beside Q43. No pack, proto, web, gateway, verb, slot, namespace, digest pin, draw label or `FRAME_VERSION` moved; the deploy is the server half alone.
- **§3.1 "Pulse phases" step 3 (the order of a millisecond)**: as built (17b cut M1b.0, 2026-10-04, `0c156218`; doc 17 §6, D-UM-20 as amended 2026-10-04): the due loop still holds a millisecond's `MindThink` and `MindLand` while its other due events pop, and then runs them in two phases — every held landing (unit-id order, then `think_seq`), then whatever those landings scheduled at that millisecond (a beam's zero-flight `impact`, a goal's `MotorTick` on a substep end, a `StartupComplete` at a `startup_ms` of nought), then the thinks — where the M1a.5 entry above ran landings and thinks in one pass, so a zero-flight impact popped after that millisecond's thinks; two minds' beams at one millisecond are mutual as two `ReflexTick`s are (both `fired`, both `impact`); a `ReflexTick`'s beam impact at a millisecond still pops before that millisecond's mind landings (declared in doc 17 §24; M4's flip retires it). `MindView` gained the keyframe's `scan_tables`. Nothing a production match runs changed: the three substep pins hold and a new LINE-hash pin — the (h) seat script at three cadences, the shipped duel and the (j) field, 116 ledger lines recorded at `e23694b0` — holds through M1b.5.
- **§3.1 "Pulse phases" steps 2 and 4, §3.5 "Scheduled impact" (the fire law), §3.7 (a seat's condition slot), §4 (the ledger and the snapshot) and §7 (config)**: as built (17b cut M1b.1, 2026-10-04, `137e49c7`; doc 17 §5, D-UM-16, D-UM-17, D-UM-19; `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 6 → 7, a format-6 snapshot refused at resume by name, so a deploy falls between matches): a hull of any controller holds up to two condition slots (`Arena.conditions`: `FireCondition { weapon, target: Lock, Contact or BestHostile, min_permille, aspects, min_rounds_left, min_range_m, max_range_m }`, stable 1-based numbers, cleared on every controller transition, the wreck retain and per weapon at `RemoveWeapons`), folded as hash items 3d and 3e per row with no count prefix, so a hull with no slot folds nothing new and the production hash did not move by substep or by line; a slot fires the hull's LOCK only, when held and classified (`BestHostile` adds: of another side and not reading dead — since 17b cut M2.2 every target kind idles on a lock that reads dead, the M2.2 entry below; a `Contact` target is unlawful whatever the id until M5), at the weapon's own `WeaponReady` (its arm filled), at the hull's own sensor pass or a received share that refreshed the lock's row, at the startup flip, once after a mind's landing and, for a seat, after a `LOCK` or an arming resolved in phase 4 — never once per pulse — reading the plot only (`aim_at`, `resolve_seen`, the rounded range), through `apply_intent` under `IntentSource::Cond` (never on the seat window, exempt from the linkdead gate) and `resolve_fire_by(.., FiredBy::Cond { slot })` at that millisecond; the `fired` ledger line gains optional `by` (`cond`, or `mind` for a cited mind shot) and `slot`, printed by `@arena/report`; `IntentKind::Condition(Arming::{Arm, Disarm})` is a discrete fire-channel order queued at phase 2 through the seat window and resolved at the pulse's end in phase 4 in arrival order with `LOCK` and `FIRE` through `resolve_arming` (`NoWeapon`, `Unlawful`, then `NoSlot`; a disarm or re-arm drops the slot's held-shot stamps); `resolve_fire_by` gates a slot's shot after `Cycling` and `Empty` and before `Overheated` (hold-fire off the hull's standing order as `Unwarranted`), a pilot's typed `FIRE` never gated; three new refusal words, each with one sentence whose home is `CockpitError` so the cockpit's pre-check and the arena's landing refusal print one string — `unwarranted` `That has no warrant.`, `no_slot` `Every slot is armed; FIRE <weapon> OFF frees one.`, `unlawful` `The Compact floor is 10%; a condition may not ask for less.` through a third `RefusalDetail::Floor { permille }` (a `Contact` target's: `The Compact floor forbids that condition.`); `[tacsim.minds].odds_floor_permille` (default 100, validated 10 to 100 in steps of 10; the host, mush-config, the example and the template) captured into `Arena.odds_floor_permille` at check-out through `spawn_arena`, carried by the snapshot and never hashed, so a config reload mid-match moves nothing; `share_pass` answers receiver pairs only for hulls holding a slot, so a production share allocates nothing; the stock card's shape on `MindState.card`; the two slot notice kinds `slot_fired`/`slot_held` defined and staged by nothing until M1b.4. The `af1` pin moved once (393 → 396 lines).
- **§6 "The reflex controller" (the native stock mind arms its slots; the law for minds)**: as built (17b cut M1b.2, 2026-10-04, `cf0a78bd`; doc 17 §5, §11, §17): `resolve_fire_by` gates `FiredBy::Mind { slot }` in the order `Unlawful`, hold-fire, the warrant, with an uncited mind `FIRE` `Unwarranted`; T1 arms one `BestHostile` slot per gun for its first two weapons in component order at its think whatever the heat, at its card's discipline with the admitting ceiling as the range bound, and cites `Fire { weapon, slot }` only beside a fresh `Lock`; the twin probe gained seven counters (doc 17 §24: `cond_fire` 2,811, `cond_stale` 9, `unwarranted` 0, `unlawful` 0 asserted, `floor_held` 0, `slot_starved` 0, `slot_parallel` 449 — each armed slot firing its own gun at its own `WeaponReady` where T0 fires one weapon a tick, T1 at 30.491 shots a minute against T0's 24.977, a lead ruling owed), the pool 15 of 30 with Wilson [0.332, 0.668]. No production path sets `Controller::Mind`; the production pins did not move.
- **§5 (`FRAME_VERSION` 3: the arming and the floor on the keyframe; the odds rows), §3.6 "What a pilot sees" (Q2) and §6 "Deliberation" (the classified re-key)**: as built (17b cut M1b.3, 2026-10-05, `189aff85`; doc 17 §3, D-UM-4, D-UM-5, D-UM-15; `FRAME_VERSION` 2 → 3, a wire version and not a pack: a deploy needs the gateway restart and a fresh `web/dist`, and an older client prints the old rows): each keyframe weapon row carries `armed` (`null`, or `{ slot, target: lock, best or contact, min_permille, percent, aspects, keeps, min_range_m, max_range_m }`, never a designator) and the frame the top-level `odds_floor_permille`; one `arming_words` line (`S1: >=60% side/rear keeps 8`) printed under an armed weapon by the TUI pane (wrapping past 46 by the pane's rule), the web's rows and line mode; `REFUSAL_WORDS` gains `unwarranted`, `no_slot` and `unlawful` in both rings; the four TUI goldens and three web fixtures re-blessed. **Q2 built** (17b row M1a's residual (1)): `frame_odds` seats the hull's lock first when classified of another side, then classified hostiles nearest-first with `ContactId` ascending, the first `STOCK_ODDS_ROWS`, never a friend and never an unclassified row, so `CONTACTS`' odds column prints `-` on a friendly row, a friendly lock and an unidentified return (the help's Seeing paragraph rewritten; the M1a.3 fixtures' hand-built friend rows and the bot's expectation edited as a declared reader change). **M1a's residual (2) built**: `rekey_order` keeps only a classified held row through its one closure and the `Support` arm, so an order landing before classification leaves a mind on no target for its TTL where the reflex re-resolves every tick (`order_hint_late`, doc 17 §24). Fan-out `panel_bytes_max` 7,931 of 16,384; `frames_per_pulse_mean` unmoved.
- **§3.7 `FIRE` and `WEAPONSTATUS` (the pilot's slot), §5 (two notice kinds, the help) and the AM entry's reader contract**: as built (17b cut M1b.4, 2026-10-05, `69880812`; doc 17 §5, D-UM-18): `FIRE <weapon> WHEN <condition> [AND <condition>]…` (at most four, no `OR`) over the eight slot-fitting terms — `odds [weapon] >= n` 1 to 100, under the match's floor refused AS TYPED off the held frame in the arena's own bytes (`FIRE cannon refused. The Compact floor is 10%; a condition may not ask for less.`), a rule with no odds term at the floor; `aspect = front, side or rear`; `range < or > n m` against the rounded range, inclusive (strict since 17b cut M2.2, the M2.2 entry below); `band = close, effective or far` as fixed metres; `rounds > n or n%`; `reserve`; `target = identified`; `lock` — with one outside refusal (`FIRE cannon refused. That condition is not one this core takes; help cockpit lists the eight it does.`) and the count's (`… Every slot is armed; FIRE <weapon> OFF frees one.`, the arena's `no_slot` the same bytes; the cockpit's count pre-check gone since 17b cut M2.2, the arena's refusal a pulse after the acceptance line), `FIRE <weapon or ALL> OFF` the disarm (`Cleared: cannon fires only when you say.`, `Cleared: your guns fire only when you say.`), the English written back with no slot number (`Arming cannon: fires your lock when your chance to hit is 60% or better and it shows its side or rear.`), every pre-existing `FIRE` line byte-identical; `WEAPONSTATUS` prints `  S1: >=60% side/rear` under an armed weapon's row, every row and arming line under 120 columns; two notice kinds staged on the shooter's hull alone — `slot_fired` `Cannon fires at BA by S1 (62%, side).` and `slot_held` `S1 held the cannon: ….` at most once per slot and contact per 10 s — with the rings' words and `notice_lines.json`'s five cases; `help cockpit`'s `## Arming a weapon` and Refusals; the `fire` and `weaponstatus` stanzas refreshed, the baseline unmoved. **Reader contract** (a declared delta to the AM entry): the bot reads the arming line as `Line::WeaponArming` it never acts on and does not close an open `WEAPONSTATUS` reply at it, reads the `unlawful` sentence as a refusal that opens no reply, and the two notices as other lines. The cross-tempo fixture of a piloted arming and the SSH transcript (`FIRE cannon WHEN odds >= 60 AND aspect = side,rear` firing only on flanks while a typed `FIRE` still fires) are the gate's.
- **§6 "The reflex controller" (the fill controller), §7 (`[tacsim.minds]`), §4 (two ledger kinds) and the staff verbs (`@mind/set`)**: as built (17b cut M1b.5, 2026-10-05, `d2d49389`; doc 17 §11, §14, D-UM-43, D-UM-44): `[tacsim.minds].fill_controller = "reflex"` or `"mind"` (default `reflex`, a lab knob on the uncarried block, set by no production config; retired by 17b cut M4.3, whose `fill_class` replaces it and whose fill is a mind on every config) makes `match_::start` and `@arena/start` check unseated hulls out as `Controller::Mind`, dealt into squads by L.2's `is_machine` and flown by T1 with its slots armed, the board's `flown by doctrine` unchanged; the staff verb `@mind/set <unit>[=<preset> or =<slider> <value>]` (gated as `@arena`, a non-baseline stanza; the six presets and the sliders refused by name with the range, the step or the choices, `min_odds` against the match's captured floor on the registry's mirror) offers `ArenaMsg::MindCard` into the match's inbox, applied in phase 2 to the arena's own row under its own floor check and journalled as `mind_card` (the whole card) or `mind_card_refused` (`not_mind`, `unlawful`, a card error's text) under the phase's hash, printed by `@arena/report` for staff and never by the pilot's fold, the read-back written from the tape with its lag said; `MatchMirror` gains the captured floor, the controllers, the armed set and the cards; the stock mind's `ignore_order` key became `ignore_until_ms` so a newer order is obeyed at once; the bot's `#[ignore]` lane `the_bot_against_a_conditioned_t1_over_thirty_pooled_matches` prints `MIND RATE …` and `MIND RATE INTERVAL …` (15 of 30, Wilson 33 to 67 per cent at the gate; 16 of 30 in the builder's parallel run) and runs by hand, in no CI job. No pack, no proto; nothing a live world must take by hand.
- **§3.5 "Draw labels" (a sixth kind), §3.5 "Scheduled impact" and the M1b.1 entry (one read-dead rule for every slot target), §3.7 `FIRE` and the M1b.4 entry (strict range terms; the cockpit's count pre-check gone), the M1b.5 entry's `@mind/set` (the slider pair), §4 (the definition behind an `Arc`) and §6 "The reflex controller" (a mind's strikes)**: as built (17b cut M2.2, 2026-10-05, `fb703386`; doc 17 §7, §8, §11, D-UM-29, D-UM-31 and the lead's rulings (a) to (c), recorded in doc 17 §24): `DrawLabel::Mind { unit, think_seq, n }`, encoded `mind:<unit>:<think_seq>:<n>` (a unit mind's n-th draw of one think), is the sixth label kind beside `hit`, `facet`, `burst`, `classify` and `contact`, pinned by the contract test and drawn by nothing on a production path (still true after 17b cut M4.3: neither `NativeStockMind` nor `standard.rhai` draws; the production hash moved at the flip for the controller byte and the landing timing, never for a draw); a condition slot's warrant reads its target as not reading dead for `Lock` and `Contact` as it did for `BestHostile` alone, so a slot stops firing at a wreck whose track still stands (a cross-tempo fixture whose match outlives the kill; a typed `FIRE` unchanged); `range < n m` fires strictly nearer than `n` and `range > n m` strictly farther (`max_range_m = n − 1`, `min_range_m = n + 1`; the warrant and `arming_words` unchanged), the arming's reply echoing the figures as typed while `WEAPONSTATUS` and the held-shot sentence show the stored metre (`S1: >=10% under 599m`, `outside 201 to 599`) and `help cockpit` says which; the cockpit's slot-count pre-check is gone — an arming past a full table is accepted on the line (`Arming missiles: …`) and refused by the arena a pulse later as the notice `FIRE missiles refused. Every slot is armed; FIRE <weapon> OFF frees one.`, the one wall — with two effects declared: `range < 200 AND band = close`, where both read 200 before, is refused as a differing duplicate (`That condition is not one this core takes…`), and `range > 200 AND band = effective`, refused under M1b.4, is taken at 201 to 400 (any range term one metre past a band edge flips the same way; `range > 200 AND range < 201` compiles to an empty window and is accepted, a residual); `@mind/set` refuses a `break_off_pct` or `fight_home_pct` that would put fight-home above break-off, and a preset that would on the match's doctrine (`Rearguard would put fight_home_pct 75 above break_off_pct 70; fight_home_pct is at most break_off_pct`), never clamping, journalled `mind_card_refused` as before; `Arena.definition` is an `Arc<ArenaDefinition>` shared with the bridge's resume (the snapshot records the digest; `SNAPSHOT_FORMAT` 7 unmoved); a Mind hull's faulted think (a runner fault word or an intent overflow) lands nothing, keeps its memory and counts `strikes` up to three, three running being the drop to the native stand-in until M3's ROM — a rule on the carried field, no hash or format moved, `mind_faults` a never-hashed counter. No sentence, keyframe, verb, pack, proto or production hash moved; the twin's T1 record equal to M1b.5's; the deliberation layer untouched.

doc 17 §9, §5, §13, D-UM-33 to D-UM-35, D-UM-16 and §24): every shipped chassis but `target_mk1` carries a `core` component (the five line tanks and the scout in `turret`, the heavy in `hull`, the bipeds in `torso`, the drone in `body`) with ONE cell `core_class` (0..=3, born at the content's class: the line tank family and the bipeds II, the heavy III, the scout and the drone I) and the capability `tacsim.core` under the minted schema `tacsim.core.profile` (the fitting's record, appended last to `schema_payloads()` on AE.1's precedent), authored in a ninth stock pack `content/cores/cores.json` (`tacsim.pack.cores` v1; `KINDS` nine, `CONTENT_KINDS` seven, `OWNED_KINDS` fifteen, `DEFINITION_KINDS` twelve); the ten chassis packs re-versioned for the component in M3.1 and the seven with a consequence word again in M3.6 (the line tank family and the scout at version 4 with turret `[remove_weapons(turret), destroy_core]`, the heavy at 4 with `[remove_weapons(turret), degrade_core]`, the bipeds and the drone at 2, the target at 1), a live world taking them at the next server-half restart's `reset_stock_tanks` refit; `frames_per_pulse_mean` unmoved); `state_hash` folds no cell but `heat`, so the hardware itself moved no production hash. **The pilot's slots are the core's** (doc 17 D-UM-16): the condition row is sized per hull by `Arena::slot_capacity` (a line tank's pilot four slots for two guns, the heavy's six for three, a scout's two), so the M1b.4 entry's "an arming past a full table … refused by the arena a pulse later" is unreachable on shipped content and `docs/help/cockpit.md`'s "Two slots" paragraph became "Your core's slots";
